The sweep runs only once the first-time grant finished for every member (`verify_started.granted`; a failed or running grant never counts, so a failed step never removes members who should have had the role). Roles given pass the same filter as a normal verify (`verify::safe_roles`: no permit roles, no dangerous permissions). Left out: bots, quarantined members, anyone with a permit or Owner/Extra Owner/Trusted Admin (also at the deadline), members with a running time limit, servers where only suspicious joiners must verify. Queue the sweep only after the channels really became Member-only.

**A stored staff role deleted in Discord broke the channel fixes, and Fix All called it fixed.**
The Overview row "Staff cannot use their channels" looked only at staff roles Discord still has, but its Fix and the Information part of "Channels without the standard permissions" wrote overwrites for every stored staff role (permit and level roles). A deleted one gave 404 and an "internal error" toast, and `info_access` stopped each Information channel at that write (staff go first), so members never got Information. That Fix answered ok with a `failed` count the page ignored, so Fix All counted it as fixed and the row stayed.
*Avoid:* every write of a stored role id checks it against Discord's role list first (`channel_access::alive`, `live_staff`, shared by the row and the fixes). A refused write says which channel and role and why (`channel_access::put`); an unexpected error names the issue, not "internal error" (`internal_text`, never the URL). A refused staff write in Information does not stop the member writes there (only a member or bot write does; @everyone's deny comes last). A fix that answers ok with `failed` or `catch_stays` shows as partly fixed (`refused` in the Overview page). The quarantine channel fix refuses with a plain reason when the stored quarantine role was deleted. `staff_access`, `information`, `info_access`, `verify_access` filter; the standard set and catch channel already stop when a main role is gone; Auto Setup's own staff roles are made in the same run.

**A captcha decoy could be drawn cut off at the image edge.**
Spread decoys were centred anywhere, so some were cut at an edge, and a test asserting a clear corner failed at random.
*Avoid:* keep every drawn shape whole inside the image (`captcha::glyph` clamps the turned box 1 px inside). A test of random output asserts only what holds for every draw, checked over many draws.

**A captcha answer could be guessed without limit.**
A wrong answer only said "Wrong answer" and kept the code alive until it expired, so a script could try many readings of one image. Also a real font draws `l` as a bar like `I`, and the answer ignores case, so `i`, `l`, `I`, `L`, `O`, `o`, `0` must stay out of the answer set.
*Avoid:* every answer check counts wrong answers per code and drops the code after `verify::MAX_TRIES` (3); the member presses Verify for a new image. When a font changes, check which characters look alike in it before they can be an answer. The image captcha and simple sum share one answer path (`app.captchas`, the code modal), so both get the limit; web verification uses Turnstile.

**A Discord file link was stored, and it stopped working after about a day.**
Slash punishments saved the `proof:` attachment's CDN URL in `cases.proof`. Discord signs file links and they expire after about 24 hours, so proof on cases older than a day no longer opened (dashboard Records, mod log). The ticket relay's "Attachment N" fields had the same link. A file link looks lasting, but is not.
*Avoid:* never store or post a Discord file URL as the lasting place of a file. Put the file on a message Brim owns and keep that message's jump link (`commands::send_case_log`: the proof goes onto the case's mod-log message). A file already on the same message needs no link. When a file cannot be kept (no Mod Logs channel, too large, download failed), tell the moderator the link expires; never drop it silently.

**A button on a saved message opens what a panel opens, so it needs the panel's proof.**
The ticket button's check accepts only messages recorded in `ticket_panel_posts`, and the panel sync edits or deletes every message in that table, so a saved message listed there would be rewritten into a panel. An "I accept the rules" button that gave the verification roles itself would be a way past the captcha, a raid's raised mode and the suspicious-account check.
*Avoid:* a ready-made button is stored as an `action` (`rules`, `ticket:<type>`), never as a `custom_id`; the dashboard makes the id when it sends and drops such buttons from a DM copy. `tickets::panel::posted` accepts a saved message's copy only by its Sent log row in the same server whose button names that ticket type. The rules button runs the Verify button's own decision (`verify::decide`) and gives roles only where that is a plain grant (`accept_step`); otherwise it sends the member to Verify. Posting a ticket panel takes a Trusted Admin, so saving, sending or editing a copy of a message with a ticket button does too (`embeds::opens_tickets`). An undo button on a mod log builds the typed command from the stored case (`undo::command`) and runs `modcmds::run` as the presser, so it has no checks of its own to forget; a presser with no permit is refused before any lookup or claim.

**A free permission grid could undo the shapes Brim keeps.**
The first Advanced grid took any role and ran after the quarantine and verification steps, so it could let the quarantine role or @everyone (past verification) into a channel, edit the verification channel, or hide a channel from a staff role without the owner check. "Read-only" and Public also read and changed Send denies during a lock, so Public could unlock a locked channel.
*Avoid:* a free editor of overwrites is applied before every shape Brim owns (quarantine, verification), which always win. It takes only roles the server may set (`Ctx::grid_role`: no quarantine, bot or managed roles), refuses @everyone View while main roles exist and the verification channel, and a staff role's View deny counts in `unstaffed` like hiding the channel from staff. Admins only and Owner only count there too. Nothing in Channel Setup reads or changes Send while `recovery::no_backup_now` says a lock is on (`Ctx.locked`).

- **A proof read after the ban found nothing.** A punishment typed as a reply stored the replied message as verified proof but read it after the ban, and the ban deletes the member's last messages (7 days by default). *Avoid:* read what a punishment is about before it runs (`proof::replied` in `confirm::Pending::run`); store it after the case exists.

**Layout Guard took open ticket channels as part of the server layout.**
Each open ticket showed as "Made in Discord, not in the layout", and "Save again" wrote it into the layout, so the guard reported it deleted once the ticket closed. A layout image was the whole live channel list; Brim's temporary ticket channels were not told apart.
*Avoid:* `layout::temp_channels` reads Brim's ticket records and the held lock's `locked_channel` (the lockdown's `#server-locked`) by channel id (one query, never name or category, so nobody can hide a channel from the guard with a ticket-like name or by moving it into the ticket category). Open ones are left out of the live side of the compare, of every save, push and roll back; the saved side drops any channel a ticket record names, so an old layout holding a ticket channel shows nothing when it closes. A closed ticket whose channel still exists counts like any other channel. Channel Setup marks the row "Ticket" or "Lockdown": no drag, rename, delete or move, and `hold_fixed` drops those draft fields on the server. A new feature that makes temporary channels joins this list.

**A cleanup of Brim's webhooks knew only one of their users.**
`message_webhooks` held only Message Builder webhooks, so the 30-day sweep (`msghooks::sweep`) and the dashboard `forget_unused` deleted a webhook no saved message used, which would take a feed's webhook too.
*Avoid:* every query that deletes rows of `message_webhooks` (or the webhooks) also keeps a channel that a feed posts in (`not exists (select 1 from feeds ...)`). A new user of that table adds itself to both queries.
## 2. Concurrency and restarts

- **A push's layout version was read after the push.** `after_push` read the newest version once Discord was done, so an Owner's "Save again" became the base: the new channel was added twice and the "layout changed during the push" guard never fired. *Avoid:* read the base version at the start, inside the lock, and write with `HAVING max(version) = <that version>` (`layout::record`); a mismatch skips the write and says so. A push saves no version with a serious problem the saved layout did not have (`blocking` on both, compared by check id).
- **One server's big job delayed another server's panic start.** `panic_start` and `nuke_revert` shared the one-job-at-a-time heavy worker with `backup_load` and `raider_scan`; a scan of a 30,000-member server held another server's panic start 3.25 s (minutes for bigger ones). *Avoid:* kinds that must start at once go in `jobs::URGENT`, with their own worker (`scheduler::run_urgent`, each job in its own task); any kind that can run for minutes goes in `HEAVY` (test: `a_big_job_in_one_server_does_not_delay_another_servers_panic_start`). A panic could then start in the middle of a backup load, whose later steps wrote the locked permissions back: a full load checks for a lock, panic or running nuke revert before every step (a revert and a load would recreate the same channels), and a stopped load writes no server settings or role order (`backup::halt`, test: `a_full_load_stops_when_panic_or_a_nuke_revert_starts`). Heat panic could do the same.
- **Two dashboard-queued job kinds were left out of the run-time re-check.** #312 re-checked the asker of lock, unlock, lock update, panic start and backup jobs, but not the early `panic_end` (moved to now with `ended_by`) or the Raider List decisions (`raider_check`, `raider_lift` at server 0, no asker). Root cause: the list came from jobs that carried `by`, not from every place the dashboard queues or moves a job. *Avoid:* when a check covers "jobs a person queues", grep the dashboard (and MCP) for every `jobs::enqueue`, `insert into scheduled_jobs` and `update scheduled_jobs`, and decide per kind. An early `panic_end` is re-checked as panic start (Owner, Extra Owner, Trusted Admin); a refused one must not delete panic's only end, so `panic::keep_timed_end` puts the job back at `ends_at` (where the dashboard moved it from) without the asker. `panic::end_payload` keeps `ends_at` with `ended_by`, and the put-back adds no second end on a retry. A Raider List decision carries `by` and fans out only while that person is the bot owner; a dropped one DMs the bot owner. Left as is: `appeal_accept` (`decide` checks permits at run time), the timed `unban`/`timeout_renew` of a mod action, the member's own `verify_*` jobs, `permit_sync`, `verify_sweep`, `note_log`, and `raider_notify` DMs (a security alert must never be dropped).

**Every event posted its logs before raid detection, anti-nuke and automod ran.**
`events::run` awaited each log post, which waits on the log channel's rate limit (about 5 per 5 s), so the 300th joiner's detection ran minutes late; `Logger::handle` also returns the due held logs of every server, so one server's delete flood slowed all others. A failed config read there (`?`) ended the whole event. *Avoid:* the event path never awaits a log post: `events::run` sends logs in a spawned task with the event's span and an `InFlight` drop guard (a panic must not keep shutdown waiting); a failed config read skips only the logs. One task per event, not one sender for all servers.

**A failed command reply or lookup skipped automod.**
The ticket relay and command steps before `automod::on_message` used `?`, so a 403 on the reply or a DB error in `commands::ignored` left `t!ban x <scam link>` unchecked. *Avoid:* the command block runs in its own `async` block whose error is only logged; automod always runs after it.

**A failed join lock read skipped the whole join.**
`lockdown::join_lock(..).await?` returned from `on_join` before raid counting, verification and the Join Gate, so a pool timeout in a raid let every joiner in unchecked. *Avoid:* an unread lock is logged and skips only the lock's kick or ban. Do not queue a retry: `raid_punish` does not read the lock again, so it would kick joiners when no lock is on. `on_join` returns only when `punish_or_retry` removed the joiner (a lock kick that did not remove them, by refusal or a retry due in 30 s, must not skip counting, holding and the gate).

**A raid's log, owner DM and lockdown came before its punishments, and punishments ran one at a time.**
`join_raid` awaited the trigger log, `alert_owner` (up to 3 s) and `lockdown::lock` (one Discord call per role or channel) before punishing the first wave, and each punishment awaited its DM. The mention spam lock did the same in automod. *Avoid:* punish first: the raid spawns the log, DM and lock task (not awaited) and punishes the flagged list at once (`join_all`); the lock must not wait for the whole first wave either. The mention spam lock runs in `automod::check` after the punishment, as auto slowmode does. An earlier flagged joiner's level is read from the roles of their join (`RaidDetector::set_roles`, a DB read), not by a member fetch per flagged joiner (they queue before the bans). A member update forgets those roles, so that member is fetched again; a TA or EO added by id is still seen, because the level is resolved again.

**A Join Gate, bot adder or Raider List ban refused by an outage was never retried.**
These paths called plain `punish`, so a 503 left the account in the server with nothing queued, and the gate counted it as removed (same as "A join lock's failed kick let the joiner stay"). *Avoid:* they use `punish_or_retry` (it takes the gate's `Detail`), and "removed" comes from its result. `queue_retry` keeps one pending `raid_punish` per (guild, user) with the harshest action, in its enqueue SQL (the join lock, Raider List, raid and gate could each queue one). A Raider List outage counts as "the list acted" (the raid and the gate skip the joiner); a refusal does not (the join goes on, verification holds them). The retry of a list kick or ban writes its case, `raider_actions.case_no` and mod log (`raiders::join::retried`), so staff can undo it.

**Verification fetched the joiner's level on every join.**
`verify::on_join` called `is_immune` (a member GET) though `on_join` had read the level from the join's roles: one extra GET per joiner in the bans' bucket. *Avoid:* `verify::on_join` takes the `immune` that `joiner_immune` read; unread (None) holds the member.

**A guild_info refill put back what an invalidation removed.**
A cache miss made four REST reads and inserted with no check, so an owner transfer or role edit that invalidated during the reads was lost for 5 minutes. *Avoid:* generation rule as `lockdown::LOCKS`, per guild: invalidations bump that guild's entry in `hierarchy::GENERATION` under the `guilds` lock, and a fill is kept only when it did not change. A global counter made one server's events discard the fills of all others.

**Replayed voice audit entries counted at "now".**
`voice_nuke::note` counted through `antinuke::count`, so moves made during a gateway gap landed in the live window. *Avoid:* `note` takes `replayed` and counts through `count_at` with `replay_age`, as the other cards do.

**One failed step stopped the rest of ChannelDelete.**
`tickets::on_channel_delete(..).await?` returned before the log channel and verification channel remakes. *Avoid:* in a handler with several independent steps, each step logs its error and the next one runs.

**Background sweeps stuck and kept state for good.**
The message webhook sweep took 50 rows with no order, kept every row Discord refused (the same 50 came back each hour), and ran inside the scheduler tick. `MENTION_HITS` kept one entry per member who ever hit a scoped rule. *Avoid:* a sweep orders by age, moves a refused row forward (4xx; an outage is retried), and runs in its own task; a map keyed by member drops entries past the longest window (an hour for scopes) once it grows.

**The join lock, join gate filters and raid punished an immune joiner, and a bot's adder was punished on an unread level.**
None of these join steps asked the joiner's level, so an Extra Owner or Trusted Admin who rejoined during a join lock was removed; a failed adder lookup counted as "not immune". *Avoid:* `on_join` reads the level once from the join's own roles (`joiner_immune`, no member fetch per join). Immune: every punishing join step is skipped. Unread: nothing punishes now, `hold_back` queues the `raid_punish` retry for a kick, ban or timeout (it reads the level again), and the rest of the join still runs (verification holds them; never return early on an unread level). A bot is never immune, so it is not read. The bot adder uses the same rule. The Raider List (`exempt` with `?`) and the post-join username filter (`is_immune` with `?`) already refuse on an unread level.

**A join lock's failed kick let the joiner stay, and skipped the re-quarantine.**
The join lock ran before `requarantine_rejoin` with plain `punish` and returned its error, so a 5xx left a quarantined nuker in with no role and no retry. *Avoid:* `on_join` re-quarantines first, then the join lock uses `punish_or_retry`. A security step that comes first in a handler must not return early before a step that contains.

**A Join Gate retry sent the punishment DM again.**
`punish` sends the DM before the action, and each `raid_punish` retry called `punish` (six DMs). *Avoid:* `punish` is `tell` then `act`; `punish_job` calls `act` only. The first try still DMs before a kick or ban Discord may refuse (not changed).

**A DB error at GuildCreate skipped the missed-rejoin quarantine.**
The `guild_left` delete and `owner_seen` used `?` before `requarantine_missed`, which queues its retry only once it starts. *Avoid:* in `on_guild_create` each step logs its error and the next one runs.

**The message cache logged content older than 24 hours.**
`MessageCache::remove` ignored `TTL` and `prune` ran only on an insert into the same guild, so a quiet guild (or one the bot left) kept text past the 24 hours PRIVACY.md promises. *Avoid:* `remove` answers like `get` (expired is missing), and `flush` (every event and the timer) runs `prune_all`, which also drops guilds with nothing left.

**A DB error before or in a nuke run skipped containment, the report or the retry.**
`quarantine_and_revert` queued its guard job and read `still_contained` with `?`, so a DB error left the nuker free. A failed `revert_actor` returned with `?`, skipping report and panic, and the guard job was deleted anyway. *Avoid:* a failed guard write runs without a guard; `still_contained` returns a bool and a failed read counts as not contained. An unreadable config in `revert_and_report` and `quarantine_as` runs the defaults (auto quarantine on, no Quarantine role, so roles are cleared) and logs the error to Anti Nuke; the run is then done (no retry for the config error alone). Without the config the Quarantine role is unknown and counts as "other", so `row_flags` keeps the row: no `released` (saved roles stay) and the row's `on_join`. A member who already has a quarantine row keeps their roles then (owner decision; stripping would take the Quarantine role that holds them); revert and report still run. Such a member still loses every role with dangerous permissions (`strip_dangerous`: a row can outlive a release by hand) and counts as quarantined only while at most one non-managed role is left. A retry does only what is left: `resume_nuke` contains whenever `still_contained` is false, and reports and runs panic only when no run reported yet; `mark_reported` (in memory and as `reported` on the guard job) runs right after every report step. `revert_actor` reads the deleted-object rows before any inverse runs, so a failed read cannot leave run inverses unmarked. A failed final `UPDATE action_log` is a warning; its rows stay in memory (`UNMARKED`), are skipped by the next revert and marked with it, so no deleted channel or role is recreated twice. Accepted (owner decision): when the `reported` mark cannot be saved and the bot restarts, the retry reports a second time. A failed revert logs, reports with an empty summary plus a failure log, counts towards panic, and returns the error at the end; the guard job is finished only on `Ok`, so it reruns the revert.

**A failed panic end log retried the end, and start dropped a dashboard end.**
`panic::end` returned the end log's result, so a refused log made the job retry and write the saved category overwrites again. `end_payload` replaced the whole payload (losing `ended_by`) and pushed `run_at` 60 s ahead, delaying a dashboard end during start. The backup step read the config with `?` after the locks were taken. *Avoid:* the end log is best effort. `end_payload` keeps `ended_by` and leaves `run_at` alone when set (`ends_later` handles the early end). A failed config read in the backup step reports `Backup::Failed` and goes on.

**Command rate limits were shared across servers.**
`RateLimiter` and `command_uses` were keyed by (user, action), so a mod's uses in server A counted in server B and could quarantine them there. *Avoid:* key is (guild, user, action); `command_uses` has `guild_id` (migration 4005, nullable for a rollback); rows without a guild are skipped at the restart seed. Any per-user in-memory state must hold the guild in its key.

**A permit sync gave a role to a member quarantined during the run.**
`permit_sync::roles` read the quarantine list once, then made one REST call per change for minutes. *Avoid:* right before each give, check the quarantine again and skip the member as held; after a give, check again and take the role back if a quarantine began during the call (its saved roles miss it). A long loop of REST calls re-reads any state a guard depends on.

**The first permit migration ran one query per member under the config lock.**
`migrate_members` ran a SELECT per member inside `settings::locked`, so on a big server every config save waited. *Avoid:* read the permit roles once and query only members who hold one. Keep work under `settings::locked` small.

**Two presses of one button both acted.**
Two moderators pressing Ban on one report both banned and a later press overwrote `handled_by`; two quick presses of a ticket DM's Send relayed twice. *Avoid:* claim before acting: an in-memory claim per report (`reports::Acting`, freed on drop) and `WHERE status='open'` on the close; the ticket prompt's delete is the claim (10008 means a press already took it). Build a drop guard only for a won claim (`then(|| ..)`, never `then_some(..)`): an eager guard for a lost claim drops at once, under the lock it re-takes (a deadlock), or removes the winner's entry. `t!enable`/`t!disable` flipped the state, so a repeated `disable` turned tickets on: a command named after a state sets that state.

**`closeall` said "all closed" when closes failed, and the metrics endpoint died on one error.**
Failed closes were only logged, and `metrics::serve` returned on the first `accept` error (EMFILE). *Avoid:* a bulk action reports "closed X of Y" (`tickets::close_all`, after a Yes/No through `confirm`) and does not delete the channel that holds its own answer; an answer to a message that may be gone is best effort, never `?`; a long-lived listener logs an accept error and goes on, with a cap on parallel answers.

**Two clicks could decide one appeal twice.**
`appeals::decide` read the appeal row without a lock and opened its transaction later, so two staff both passed "still open". Its timeout end read the member with `let Ok(..)`, so a Discord error looked like "no timeout". *Avoid:* the transaction starts first and the row is read `FOR UPDATE`; read the member with `http_err::member`.

**Two tickets at once made two ticket categories.**
`tickets::ensure_category` looked for the category and created it when missing, with nothing between. *Avoid:* a per-server `tokio::sync::Mutex` holds look and create together (one bot process per database); per server, because it is held across Discord calls.

**A panicking event handler left the in-flight count up.**
`events.rs` decremented `IN_FLIGHT` at the end of the handler, so a panic skipped it and shutdown waited forever. *Avoid:* a drop guard decrements it on every exit.

**A timed thread lock never ended.**
`lock_marked` queued an `unlock` job for timed server locks but not thread locks, so `t!lock 1h` in a thread stayed locked. *Avoid:* the thread branch queues the same `unlock` job with its `lock` kind, and a thread lock or unlock first drops older timers of that thread (`forget_timers`), so an old timer cannot lift a newer lock. The dashboard's "unlock all" does not delete every `unlock` job: it does not lift threads, so their timers stay, and the bot drops the timer of each lock it lifts.

**The message cache counted messages, not bytes.**
`MessageCache` kept 50k messages per server, so 4,000-character messages held about 200 MB per server. *Avoid:* a per-server byte budget (`MAX_BYTES`, 16 MB) also evicts the oldest; the byte count is kept on insert, edit, remove and prune, and a test checks it against the map.

**Verification jobs treated any Discord error as "member left".**
`verify::deadline` and `verify::web_grant` began with `let Ok(member) = guild_member(..) else { return Ok() }`, so a 5xx or timeout ended the job: an unverified member never removed, or a web-verified member never given roles. *Avoid:* use `http_err::member`: `None` only for a member who is gone, an error (the job retries) otherwise. (`purge.rs` and `nsfw.rs` use the old form only for best-effort details.)

**A backup restore merged two same-named roles or channels.**
`backup::plan` matched image objects by id, else by name among live objects not yet used. With two "ticket" channels and one deleted, the deleted one name-matched the survivor before the survivor's id match ran, so nothing was recreated. *Avoid:* a name fallback skips every live object that some image object matches by id (`role_hits`, `chan_hits`). Test: `a_deleted_twin_is_recreated_not_merged_into_the_survivor`.

**An early timeout lift could lose the member's main roles.**
`timeout_roles::on_member_update` deleted the `restore_main_roles` job (DELETE ... RETURNING) then called `restore`; a Discord error left no job and no roles. *Avoid:* when a claim deletes the job first, a failed run queues the payload again. (The scheduler path keeps the row until the run succeeds.)

**A failed `action_log` insert skipped the anti-nuke count.**
`count_at` returned on the insert error before `on_actions_at`. *Avoid:* log the insert error and keep counting; only that action's revert row and its count after a restart (`seed_windows`) are lost.

**A second heat panic replaced the first wave of raiders.**
`panic::note` inserted a fresh raider set and end time, releasing wave 1 early. *Avoid:* while heat panic is on, a new trip adds to the live set and keeps the later end.

**Two in-memory maps grew until restart.**
Unanswered captchas (`app.captchas`) and emptied anti-nuke windows (`AntiNuke::windows`) were never removed. *Avoid:* a map keyed by member or actor drops stale entries once it passes a size cap, as `cool`, `remember_flag`, `remember_code` and `on_actions_at` do. `App::nuke_heat` (per actor) had the same gap and now drops decayed entries.

**Unlock deleted its snapshot first.** (#97, #111)
Unlock deleted the snapshot, then aborted on the first error (a deleted channel or role), leaving the server half locked with nothing to retry from. A restart in the middle did the same.
*Avoid:* lock state is a row `locking` → `locked` → `unlocking`, deleted only when the permissions are back. `lockdown::resume` finishes cut-off work at startup. A failed step is counted and the rest continue.

**Queue the undo before the action.** (#111)
A restart between a temp ban and its unban job made the ban permanent. Same for removing main roles on timeout and for timed locks.
*Avoid:* queue the undo job first, then act. A failed action leaves an undo that finds nothing and is dropped. Nuke reverts get a guard job that reruns after a crash.

**Events run concurrently.** (#100)
`main.rs` spawns a task per event, so messages in flight after a trigger each added a strike; one burst could hit the strike cap and send several DMs, cases and logs.
*Avoid:* claim the punishment (`HeatState::claim`, one per 10 s). Anything that must happen once needs an atomic claim: `DELETE … RETURNING`, `WHERE status='open'`, `SKIP LOCKED`.

**Anti-nuke tripped once per event, not once per nuke.**
Past the limit every audit entry returned `Quarantine`, each in its own task, so a 20-channel nuke started about 16 quarantine-and-revert runs. They read the same unreverted rows, so the backup recreated the same deleted channel several times (the copy has a new id, so the next run still saw the original as missing) and opened as many cases and owner alerts.
*Avoid:* one run per (guild, actor) (`App::nuke_runs`). A later event only logs its row and asks the running run to revert once more; the run frees its claim in the same step that finds nothing new, so no action falls between two runs. The `nuke_revert` guard job is due after 120 s and a run under rate limits can take longer: the scheduler skips a guard whose run is alive, and the job path takes the same claim. An actor an earlier run already quarantined only gets the revert, no second case or alert. `panic::start` undoes its claim and category exemptions when its `panic_end` job cannot be saved (else mod commands stay locked with nothing to end them), and checks and inserts `panic_runs` in one step (else a second start saves the already exempted overwrites as the old ones).

**A revert applied same-second actions in any order.**
`revert_actor` read rows without `ORDER BY`, and `plan_revert` sorts by whole seconds, so two server renames in one second could be put back in the wrong order, leaving the attacker's first name. Root cause: a sort key coarser than the order of events, tie left to Postgres. Only name and icon reverts depend on order.
*Avoid:* read rows `ORDER BY id DESC` and keep the sort stable, so ties stay newest first. (The dashboard revert already orders by `created_at desc, id desc`.)

**A late rerun put back a name from a rename in between.**
O → X → Y → Z: the first run read two renames and restored O, then the row of Y → Z reached the rerun and set Y, the attacker's name. Compare-and-set on `from` alone was not enough: when the first run read before the last row was logged (live already Z), it skipped both rows and the rerun still set Y. Root cause: a "set to value" inverse applied per row, while each run sees a different subset and the right target is the oldest row of the incident. *Avoid:* the bot revert (`revert_actor`, `/revert`, the guard job) replaces all name and icon ops with one op each holding the value of the actor's oldest row since `since`, reverted or not (`settle_guild`), applies it only when the live value differs, and marks the newer rows reverted. The dashboard keeps compare-and-set on `from` (`still_set`, after a GET of the guild): staff pick rows already logged and it reverts them newest first; a row whose change is no longer live is listed under Skipped and left unreverted, so selecting the rest later still walks back to the original. Rows without `from` (written before) apply as before.

**A claim freed only on the normal return stayed taken after a panic.**
`voice_nuke` set `running = true` and cleared it only when the fetch loop returned, so a panic in a fetch left the guild claimed for good. `nuke_runs` had the same hole: a panicking run kept the actor claimed, so later events only logged rows and the guard job skipped the "running" run. A guard alone kept a second bug: `rerun` frees the claim when nothing new came in, then the run awaits `jobs::finish`, and its final remove could delete a claim a newer run took meanwhile (a third run next to the second). *Avoid:* free an in-memory claim in a `Drop` guard (`voice_nuke::Claim`, `antinuke::RunClaim`) taken right after the claim succeeds, and when anything else can free the claim first, let the guard remove only its own (per-claim token, `antinuke::release`).

**Replacing a job dropped a report that was due but not yet claimed.**
A raid trigger called `jobs::replace`, which deletes every pending job of that kind in the guild, so a raid starting right after the last one deleted its report job before the scheduler took it. Join Raid's `raid_end` had the same pattern. An extended voice raid's end was also only in memory, so `restore` after a restart ended it early. *Avoid:* `replace` only where one job per guild is the meaning (`panic_end`, which cannot start again until its end job ran). A job per event uses `enqueue`; a job that re-queues itself enqueues too (finished after it returns). Write a moved deadline into its job (`voice_raid::extend`), and read `jobs::pending` in `run_at` order so a restore keeps the latest per guild.

**A run marked rows it never read.**
`revert_actor` read the actor's rows, worked for seconds, then marked every row since `since` as reverted; what the nuker did meanwhile was marked too and never undone, not even by `/revert`.
*Avoid:* mark only the ids you read (`WHERE id = ANY($1)`). Never close work with a range condition when rows can arrive while it runs. Marking every id read is still too much: a failed inverse, an op the restore filters dropped and a deleted channel the backup did not recreate would be marked reverted and never retried. Mark a row only when its op ran (or its target was already gone): `antinuke::undone` for logged inverses, and `backup::recreate` returns the ids that exist again. The dashboard revert already marks per success.

**Two locks at once saved a locked server as the snapshot.**
`lock_for` checked for an active lock, planned with several HTTP calls, then saved with `ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE`. Mention-spam lock calls it for every message past the limit, so a second call could plan after the first began applying and overwrite the first snapshot with locked permissions; unlock then restored the locked state.
*Avoid:* lock and unlock of a guild run one at a time (`lockdown::exclusive`). An in-process lock is enough only because the dashboard queues jobs and never writes `lock_snapshots`; a second writer process needs an advisory lock (see the Channel Setup entry below).

**An undo outlived what it undoes.**
Jobs queued to end something (a temp ban's `unban`, a long timeout's `timeout_renew`, a timed lock's `unlock`, panic's `panic_end` unlock) were not removed when it ended another way, so they hit what came later: a permanent ban after a temp ban was lifted by the old job, an untimeout or accepted appeal was timed out again by the renewal, a manual unlock and new lock was lifted by the first lock's timer, and panic's end lifted a role lock it did not make (its `lock` had returned false). Root cause: "queue the undo first" without the other half, "drop the undo when the thing is gone or replaced".
*Avoid:* every ban, unban, lift and new timeout drops the user's older pending job (`jobs::cancel_user`, `scheduler::forget_jobs` in the bot; the dashboard's appeal accept and revert call `cancel_user` in their own transaction), keeping only the job it just queued (`keep`). A lifted lock drops its own timer and clears panic's claim on the role lock (`lockdown::forget_timers`); `panic_end` carries `locked` (set by `panic::own_lock` only when panic's own lock call saved a snapshot, `lockdown::lock_saving`) and unlocks only then. Ban Sync does not skip a member who only has a local temp ban that ends before the copy would. The renewal is dropped only after the new timeout's PATCH worked (inside `timeout_until`, keeping its own job), so a failed new timeout keeps the old chain; a ban drops renewals too. A renewal that finds the member gone (10007) retries hourly until the timeout's end, since Discord keeps the timeout for a rejoin. Nuke reverts re-ban (`Inverse::Ban`) on purpose without dropping anything (it restores the earlier state).

**An undo kept less than it undid.**
An accepted appeal stored `{"op":"ban"}` and the visible timeout end, so a revert gave a temp ban back as permanent and a 90-day timeout as at most 28 days (the dashboard's PATCH past 28 days was refused). Root cause: the real end lived only in the job about to be dropped (`unban`, `timeout_renew`). *Avoid:* read the end from that job before dropping it and store it in the inverse (`Inverse::Ban.until`, `Inverse::Timeout.until` from `until_secs`); a revert queues the matching job again (bot `antinuke::run_inverse`, dashboard `ops::run_inverse`, 28-day chunk plus renewal) and skips a punishment that would have ended by now. A revert deletes the job it queued when the Discord call fails (both `run_inverse`; else a failed ban leaves the `unban` to lift a later permanent ban). The appeal's status, the action-log row with the end and the job drop are one transaction (else a crash between made the retry record a permanent ban as the undo, or answer "already handled" with no verdict DM and the log buttons left on), together with the Raider List allow of an accepted ban appeal (`raiders::allow` on the transaction; it once ran after the commit and was skipped on a resume). A retry by the same staff member that finds the appeal already accepted only sends the missed verdict DM (`appeals.verdict_sent_at`, migration 3922) and closes the log.

**A second ban keeps the first ban's reason, so a reason cannot prove who owns a ban.**
Ban Sync laid a copy over a shorter local temp ban and took the ban's reason as proof the copy was its own. Discord keeps the first reason and writes no audit entry when a banned user is banned again, so the copy still read as the temp ban: the source's lift called it "banned again by hand" and left it (the temp ban became permanent), and a retry skipped it as "already banned". A staff ban over a plain copy looked like the copy too. Root cause: ownership read from a field Discord does not overwrite.
*Avoid:* a copy laid over a local temp ban records the reason Discord shows and the temp ban's end on its row before the ban (`over_reason`, `temp_end`, migration 3902), and `bansync::owned` counts that ban as the copy once the temp ban's unban job is gone, at `lift`, `banned_by_us` and the copy's retry. A lift before `temp_end` keeps the ban and queues its unban at the old end (then drops the copy's own unban job); after it, the copy is unbanned as usual (option B, pending the owner's answer). A staff ban over a done copy also leaves the old reason, so `owned` first asks `hand_ban_since`: a ban or tempban case by someone other than the bot newer than the row's `done_at` means banned again by hand, for plain copies too (a Discord-app ban cannot be made on an already banned user, and an unban first lifts the row). `temp_end` counts leased unban jobs, a copy fails and retries while the temp ban's unban is due or running (`temp_ending`), and the lift's new unban job, the drop of the copy's job and the row mark run in one transaction. Ban here goes through `banned_by_us`; nothing else reads a copy's ban reason. The copy path deletes the temp ban's unban job itself with `?` before the row becomes banned (`drop_temp_job` in `finish_ban`), since the ban's own `forget_jobs` is best effort and a leftover job would disown the copy and lift it at the old end.

**A deleted object came back only from the newest backup.**
`backup::recreate` read only `latest`, so a backup that missed the object (taken mid-nuke, or after a delete not logged in time) made it unrecoverable, and the auto backup could run during a nuke or panic and save the damage as the newest image.
*Avoid:* recreate each id from the newest image that has it (`backup::newest_with`), and skip the auto backup while a nuke run or panic mode is on (`backup::under_attack`). A role from an older image may predate staff removing a permission, so it comes back without `DANGEROUS_PERMS` and the anti-nuke report says how many.

**A gateway event and its audit entry run in their own tasks.**
The ticket-category exemption read the category that the `ChannelCreate` task stores. When the audit task ran first, the lookup missed and a ticket bot's channels counted towards its limit.
*Avoid:* do not rely on the order of two events. `note_parent` stores every channel (also "no category"), so a miss means "not seen yet", and `parent_of` waits up to 0.5 s for it.

**An invite diff per join picked a code by luck.**
`used_invite` fetched invites in each join's own task, so fetches could finish in either order and the older list overwrote the newer; it had no baseline after a restart, took the first grown code in HashMap order, guessed a vanished code as used and read a missing `uses` as 0. Root cause: a diff of shared state in parallel tasks, with a guess where data was unsure.
*Avoid:* events that must be diffed in order go to one actor per guild before `tokio::spawn` (`invites::observe` in `main.rs`), and an unsure match is stored as unknown (`invites::resolve`). A join arriving within 1 s after a fetch may already be in that list, so it joins the batch and the list is read again (up to 5 rounds); dropping the batch made every busy server and raid unknown and emptied `raid_log.invite_code`. A 403 on the list waits 10 minutes before joins ask again (403s count towards Discord's invalid request limit, which blocks the whole bot); GuildCreate seeds are spread over 10 s. A vanished code counts as its last use only when it was one use short of its limit; expired or event-deleted codes count as no use. A guild's actor checks it still owns the guild before each event, so a GuildDelete and quick GuildCreate never run two writers. No other state is diffed from REST in per-event tasks.

**Join-time steps race each other.**
A `MemberUpdate` (verification, another bot, the bot's own role adds) can run before or alongside the `MemberAdd` task, and a late update can come from the membership before. Saving roles "per user id" would let it overwrite the saved roles before the rejoin reads them.
*Avoid:* key per-member state by membership: `member_roles` rows carry `joined_at`, an update writes only when its `joined_at` matches the row, and the rejoin claims the older row in one statement (`sticky::claim`), so a replayed `MemberAdd` gets nothing.

**Membership was checked when roles were claimed, not when they were added.**
Sticky roles checked the member once, at the claim, then added roles later (a job a minute on, after verification, when a timeout ended). By then the member could have left and rejoined, been banned, quarantined or sent back to verification, and still got the old roles. Errors after the claim (a failed read, a refused add) dropped the claimed list, and a join where any step acted (even a Log hit) froze the row, so the next rejoin got roles from two memberships ago. `timeout_roles::restore` had the same gap and gave main roles back to a quarantined member.
*Avoid:* every delayed add carries the membership it was claimed for (`joined_at`) and checks again right before the add: same `joined_at`, setting on, no quarantine row, not pending (`sticky::due`). Do fallible reads before a claim, and put whatever a claim took and could not finish into a job. A ban leaves a tombstone row instead of deleting, so a late event of the old membership cannot write it back.

**Member role adds share Discord's rate-limit bucket with punishments.**
A mass rejoin giving every member's roles back would fill the per-guild member bucket that timeouts, kicks and bans need right then.
*Avoid:* bulk role or nickname work on members gets a per-guild cap (`sticky::take`, 20 adds a minute) and queues the rest as a job instead of dropping it.

**A switch checked in only one of two call sites.**
`on_audit` calls the quarantine hold from two places. Kicks, bans and timeouts checked `quarantine_hold`, role changes did not, so staff were quarantined with the switch off.
*Avoid:* when a feature has a switch, grep every caller of its entry point and check the switch in each, or check it once inside the function. `hold` checks it inside, after the join-quarantine branch: an unverified joiner's role change is always undone (that punishes nobody); only the punishment follows the switch.

**A join quarantine counted as a nuke target.**
Verification's "quarantine on join" writes a quarantine row with no saved roles. The quarantine hold treated it like an anti-nuke quarantine, so an autorole bot giving a joiner its role was quarantined and lost its role permissions on every join.
*Avoid:* the hold undoes the role change on a join quarantine but punishes nobody. Code that reads `quarantines` must tell the two kinds apart, by the `on_join` column (next entry).
The same happened on a rejoin into a real quarantine: `requarantine_rejoin` put the quarantine back and the autorole bot's role, a second later, made the hold quarantine the bot. A bot's role change within 60 s of a rejoin's quarantine is undone without punishment (`note_rejoin`, in memory: after a restart the bot is punished as before, which fails safe); a human doing the same is still quarantined. The join branch also undid with `?`, so a Discord error skipped `guard_perms` for that entry and left the role on silently: `undo_role_change` tries once more after 2 s, then posts "Could not take back roles" in the anti-nuke log, and never punishes.
*Avoid:* a step whose failure must not stop the rest of an audit handler does not use `?`; retry or report it where it fails.

**"Nothing saved" was used to mean "join quarantine".**
The fix above told the kinds apart by an empty `saved_roles`. But a staff, Raider List or time-limit quarantine of a member with no roles also saves nothing, so such a member could verify out of it on the web page; and an autorole landing before the join quarantine filled the list, making it a real one: the joiner could never verify and the autorole bot was punished. Verification also lifted the join quarantine only while "quarantine on join" was on, so switching it off left members held with a stale row, and a rejoin then logged an anti-nuke "rejoined while quarantined" for a joiner who had only not verified.
*Avoid:* never infer a kind from what a field happens to hold; store it. `quarantines.on_join` (migration 3900) is set only by verification (`antinuke::Kind`), a real quarantine always wins over a join one (`on_join AND EXCLUDED.on_join`), and `self_grant`, `grant`, `hold` and `requarantine_rejoin` read the flag. `grant` ends a join quarantine whatever the setting is now, and a rejoin into a join quarantine counts as verifying for sticky roles. A join raid hit that keeps the member (Log, Warn, Timeout, Rename) drops their saved sticky roles (sticky-roles plan decision): kept pending, a raider got them back by verifying (web, staff `/verify`) or by leaving and rejoining. `restore_pending` reads the member's live timeout before it claims, because web verification and staff `/verify` work during a timeout, which holds the main roles. Rows from before the column with nothing saved were marked join, as they were treated until then. The time limit's Quarantine is still a join one only when the member had no roles, until an owner decision says whether verifying should lift it.

**Long work under a global lock.** (#100)
Similarity compared whole 4000-character messages (quadratic) while holding the global heat lock.
*Avoid:* bound the input (first 500 characters) and keep work under shared locks short.
The same holds for a claim: `voice_nuke` held the guild's fetch claim while counting, and counting can start a nuke run of tens of seconds, so no other voice update of the guild was fetched meanwhile. The claim covers only the fetch (`run` takes `fetch` and `apply`); the one-run-per-actor claim of the nuke run is separate and unchanged.

**Listeners must survive a reconnect.** (#100)
The config listener died after one DB hiccup, so dashboard changes never applied until a bot restart.
*Avoid:* long-lived listeners restart in a loop and reload their caches after reconnecting.

**A failed log must not stop protection.** (#100, #133)
The timeout DM went out before the timeout worked, and a failed anti-nuke report stopped panic mode.
*Avoid:* fixed order Contain → Revert → Report → Panic. Logs and DMs are side effects; their failure never skips the next protective step.
Same mistake found in Join Raid (a failed raid log or report job skipped the lockdown and every punishment, and one raider who had left stopped the punish loop), in automod (the mention-lock log and lock came before the strike, the timeout log before the strike-cap lock) and in `warn_actions`. A loop that punishes several members logs a failure and goes on with the next.
`voice_nuke::apply` broke it with `?`: the audit deltas were already stored as seen, so one actor's failed whitelist read or count dropped every later actor's delta for good. Root cause: `?` inside a loop whose input was already marked done. It logs per actor and goes on.
Join Gate: a refused gate punishment (403 on kick) ended `on_join`, so the join raid check never ran for that joiner. Gate punishments log and go on.
`panic::note`: a failed heat panic log returned `Err` after heat panic was on, and automod (`automod.rs` twice), `activity::heat_up` and anti-nuke's heat path passed it on with `?`, so the strike, timeout or `on_actions` count that tripped it never ran. The panic report and lockdown logs in `panic::start` did the same and ended the nuke run that called it. Those logs only warn, and every heat caller goes through `panic::note_heat`, which logs a failure and returns. Also `catch::on_message` (a failed log handed a caught message on to automod as uncaught) and `verify::deadline` (a failed log failed the job, which would punish again); both warn. `native_action`'s Report log was left: its caller already logs per action and goes on.

**A gateway event's value can be older than the bot's own write.**
Events run in parallel tasks and Discord sends a channel update for every edit (a rename too), so a ChannelUpdate handled after the bot's own PATCH can still carry the old slowmode. Auto slowmode read that as "staff changed it" and put it back, undoing its own slowmode.
*Avoid:* don't decide from an event field when the bot itself writes that field. Read the live value (`GET channel`) and compare with both the old and the bot's value (`slowmode::staff_value`), or skip the event when a later compare-and-set already handles it (the off job reverts only its own value). `enforce_quarantine` and `catch::keep_top` act on event values too, but their writes are idempotent, so a stale event only repeats them.

**A claim row without its job blocked the channel for good.**
An `auto_slowmode` row is claimed with `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING`. When its `slowmode_off` job gave up after its retries (Discord down, a missing permission retried to the last attempt), the row stayed and every later start found "already owned". Also: a failed start deleted row and job even when the PATCH might have landed, the not-calm re-queue made a job for a row staff had removed, and a 403 was retried until the job gave up.
*Avoid:* a claim takes over a row with no pending job (`DELETE … WHERE NOT EXISTS (job)` in the claim's transaction, so a parallel claim conflicts instead of doubling it). Treat 50001/50013 as final (`http_err::forbidden_any`): end the claim and log it. Re-queue only while the row exists, under `FOR UPDATE`. Scope a job's rows to its own guild (`AND guild_id = $2`). A start that stops early because the channel already shows the auto value also gives a stuck row a new off job (`requeue_stuck`), since the claim's takeover never runs then.

**A data request whose job was dropped stayed "approved" for good.**
`datareq::run_job` returned an error when the bot owner was unknown (the Discord application lookup at start failed), and any other failing try errored too. After the last attempt the scheduler dropped the job, the row stayed `approved` without a job, `/mydata` refused a new request ("already asked") and the dashboard answers only `pending` ones. *Avoid:* a status row that waits for one job goes back to a state a person can act on when that job is dropped: an unknown bot owner (it cannot become known in this run) reopens the request at once instead of retrying, and the scheduler's give-up branch calls `datareq::gave_up`, which puts an undone request back to `pending` (test: `datareq::tests::gave_up_reopens`). Checked and fine: `auto_slowmode` (entry above), `raider_lift` (logs for a by-hand fix), `appeal_accept` (the appeal stays open; a dropped job unblocks a new accept); the Raider List decisions change no row that the job must finish.

**State built from gateway events starts empty after a restart.**
The logger tells voice joins from moves by the channel it last saw each member in, and that map started empty, so after every restart or new gateway session the first move of each member already in voice was a "join". It was only a wrong log line (marked `shortcut:`) until the voice raid counted joins from it: a restart during a busy event could start a false raid. Root cause: the old value came only from earlier events, which Discord does not replay.
*Avoid:* seed such state from `GuildCreate`, which carries the whole snapshot (`welcome::on_guild_create` seeds `Logger::seed_voice` from `voice_states`, replacing what the guild had), and read the old value under the same lock right before the event overwrites it (`events.rs`). Both must happen before the task's first await: a seed after `app.cfg.await` and a DB write wipes voice updates handled meanwhile, and an old channel read after it lets a member's second update take the lock first so both read the same old channel. `events::run` seeds a `GuildCreate` and records a voice update (`Logger::voice_update`) first, and logs it afterwards with `handle_voice`. `thread_parents` is filled from `ThreadListSync` and thread events, the ticket-category lookup (`parent_of`) only pairs an event with its own audit entry, and Join Raid and heat start empty on purpose (a restart counts from zero, it never counts twice).

**Heat lives in memory.** (#111, accepted)
A restart resets running heat. Strikes and timeouts survive. This was left as is on purpose.

**A restart reset every limit window.**
Anti-nuke's per-minute/hour windows, the permit command limits and the heat panic list lived only in memory, and a restart can be forced: a nuker at 4/5 bans who crashed the bot started again at 0. Root cause: the counted actions were in `action_log`, but nothing read them back.
*Avoid:* a limit that protects against an attacker is rebuilt at startup before the gateway (`antinuke::seed_windows`): anti-nuke from `action_log` rows of the last hour (`counted` holds how many actions a row adds: a merged voice entry its growth, a second inverse row 0), command limits from `command_uses`, heat panic from `heat_panic`. Both new tables are pruned hourly in `welcome::purge_left`. Personal heat (above) and Join Raid stay in memory on purpose.

**A permit without its own limit had none.**
Auto Setup gives Admins and Moderators ban and kick without limits, and anti-nuke skips the bot's own audit entries, so a hacked mod could mass-ban with `t!ban id1 id2 …` where the same account in Discord's UI would be quarantined at the anti-nuke limit. A breach of a permit limit quarantined them but left the bans already made.
*Avoid:* `permits::command_limit` falls back to anti-nuke's limit for the same action (`commands::nuke_fallback`: ban, kick and timeout, one less, as a permit limit trips past its value and anti-nuke at it; only with anti-nuke on). Under that fallback bans and kicks share one rate limit key (`commands::limit_key`), as they share anti-nuke's card (per-action keys let a mod do the limit in bans and again in kicks). On a breach `gated` quarantines and runs `antinuke::revert_breach` (the actor's last hour, case bans included). Every staff command goes through `gated`; the dashboard's own rate limit is D24.

**A nuker under every limit, or with quarantine off, never reached panic.**
Anti-nuke heat at 100 % only counted towards panic, which needs 3 distinct accounts, so one nuker spreading actions over several cards below each limit was never stopped; and Quarantine On Limit off dropped the Panic step with the quarantine. Root cause: one switch or signal fed only the step next to it.
*Avoid:* 100 % anti-nuke heat contains and reverts like a limit (`antinuke::breach`), and `nuke_plan(false)` still ends with Panic. When a switch turns one step off, check the steps after it still run.

**A category exemption covered anyone who moved a channel there.**
Ticket categories and panic's Whitelisted Categories exempted every channel create and delete in them, for any actor, so a nuker moved channels in and deleted them uncounted and never recreated. The panic list was only documented as "left unlocked by panic". Root cause: an exemption meant for one kind of actor (ticket bots) was keyed only on the place.
*Avoid:* an exemption names who it is for: ticket categories exempt only a bot (`antinuke::is_bot`, a failed lookup counts), never a channel that moved in from outside in the last hour (`App::note_category` before the first await of the gateway task, `moved_in`), and panic's list is panic-only. No other module reads `whitelisted_categories`.

**Panic's "lockdown" let everyone keep writing.**
Panic locked with `LockKind::Role` only, which strips dangerous perms (Mention @everyone and the like) and never Send Messages, so @everyone wrote everywhere and the Whitelisted Categories exemption changed nothing. The exemption also gave @everyone Send by removing a Send deny the category already had (a read-only category opened), and only ever wrote the @everyone overwrite, also when the lock silences other main roles. Root cause: the step was named "lock the server" but used the lock that covers only roles; nothing tested what members could still do.
*Avoid:* panic takes two locks, `Role` and `Server` (main roles, or @everyone, lose `SILENCE` and voice when the lockdown voice option is on), each with its own overlap check, so a lock staff made first leaves the other part to panic. `panic_end` keeps one owner flag per lock (`locked`, `locked_server`), written before the lock, again after it (a staff unlock in between cleared it), and cleared when no lock was saved (a crash between lock and flag left everyone muted for good); a new panic does not start while an old `panic_end` job still waits for its retry, or its replace drops the claim on the old locks; `lockdown::forget_timers` clears the one whose lock staff lifted, and `panic::end` reads the flags from the job as it is now and tries both unlocks. A whitelisted category gets, per main role, an allow for exactly the bits the server lock takes from that role (`panic::taken`), never a bit the role lock takes too (Mention @everyone), never a bit the category already allows or denies the role, and for a role other than @everyone never a bit another role's overwrite denies there (a role allow beats every role deny, so a Muted role would write; the Quarantine role's deny is left out for a role quarantine takes, never for a managed role, which quarantine keeps). Each exemption is saved in the job before it is put; if panic's server lock was not made, the categories close again at once. A category id from another server is refused. A combined lock (`Mixed`, `All`) takes the parts no active lock covers (`LockKind::rest`), so a join raid during panic still locks joins. `t!unlock <kind>` checks the permits of every active lock it lifts, not only of the kind typed (`unlock` lifts every overlapping lock, a combined one too), and `t!lock` names what it took. A part a lock from before panic held when panic started is marked in `panic_end` (`waits_role`, `waits_server`), and panic takes it once an unlock lifts a lock overlapping it (its timer ran, or staff lifted it), once only, and then owns it (`panic::held` before `lockdown::unlock`, `panic::relock` with the lifted kinds after). An owner flag alone could not tell "a lock from before panic" from "staff lifted panic's own lock", so every unlock re-muted the server. Not while `start` runs (`PanicRun::starting`, a per-start token so an old start's guard never clears a newer one); a relock that panic's end missed is lifted again at once (`unlock_now`). A failed relock keeps waiting (unless panic's own lock holds the part already, so two relocks at once leave no stale wait), and the end of `start` takes a waited part whose lock ran out while it ran (`catch_up_waits`, after `start` marks itself done, so nothing falls between them). Relock and catch-up share `take_waited`, which lifts again a lock that an end of panic missed. Panic marks a lock as its own inside the guild's lock mutex, right after the save (`lockdown::lock_marked`), so a second take or an unlock always sees the mark. Cleanups that lift "panic's own" lock after an early end know it by its saved time (`store::lock_created`), not by its kind: the end may have lifted it and staff made a new lock of the same kind. `relink_roles` rewrites a snapshot with `store::update_lock`, which keeps its state and age. A role a restore made again under an active lock is locked like the old one and its snapshot entries move to the new id (`lockdown::relink_roles`, after `relink` in both restore paths), or it was the one main role still writing. Entries are saved before the PATCH, and a role in two locks gets one PATCH with what both take (one PATCH per lock from the same read gave the first lock's bits back). An early end from the dashboard that ran, or is running, while `start` took its locks is caught at the end of `start` (`panic::ends_later`), which lifts its own locks (only a lock of exactly its kind still active, so a staff `All` stays) and closes the categories. Not covered: the lock's time is read a few ms after the save, outside the guild's lock mutex, so an end plus a new staff lock of the same kind in those ms can still be mistaken for panic's; a failed time read leaves panic's lock after an early end (fails toward locked); a part whose lock failed with nothing holding it waits for an unlock that may not come. A role with Send that is not a main role keeps writing, as with `t!lock` (owner decision); a non-synced child channel of a whitelisted category stays locked; a crash after the flag but before the lock lets panic's end lift a server lock staff made later.
**Manage Roles handed out a whitelist.**
Anyone with Manage Roles could give themselves a role that carries a permit (and its anti-nuke whitelist), then nuke uncounted. Root cause: who may hand out a permit's role was left to Discord's hierarchy.
*Avoid:* giving a member a permit role is a guarded action (`antinuke::guard_permit_roles`): the role is taken back and the actor quarantined unless Owner, Extra Owner or Trusted Admin. Runs with anti-nuke on, after `guard_perms`, also for a self-grant. Only a guarded permit counts (owner decision): every permit except the weak ones in `permits::WEAK_PERMS` and `WEAK_WHITELIST` (cases, notes, mod stats, reports, tickets; word, link and report immunity; automod immunity is guarded). Reaction-role, level and autorole bots hand the weak ones out, so they are only logged. List the weak ones, never the strong ones: listing the strong ones missed the per-action `wl_*` flags (the original D16 attack), revert, backups, appeals and `lock_*`. A new permit is then guarded by default.

**A failed whitelist lookup dropped the action.**
`on_audit` used `app.whitelisted(..)?`, so a 5xx or timeout returned before the action was logged or counted: a nuke during a Discord hiccup was never stopped or reverted. An error in the exemption check was handled as "exempt".
*Avoid:* on a protection path a failed exemption check means "not exempt" for counting. The instant tripwires (`guard_perms`, the hold) still stop on the error, as #97 asks for staff. During a nuke the level is reused for 5 s (`App::whitelisted`), cleared on every member update or leave before the event's first await; a lookup that raced a clear is not kept (as D66).

**The unauthorized-bot filter let a bot in when the audit lookup failed.**
A bot whose adder could not be read (5xx, 429, entry not written yet), or whose adder's immunity check failed, counted as authorised. "Unknown" was read as "allowed" on a protection path.
*Avoid:* `welcome::bot_adder_retry` retries once after 1.5 s (each try bounded to 3 s); Join Gate blocks the bot when that fails too, finds no entry, or the adder's level cannot be read. The mock Discord answers `audit-logs` (`Mock::audit`, `fail_audit`).

**During a Role lock the stripped permissions were read as the real ones.**
A Role lock saves each role's permissions and strips them, so sticky roles, verification, `strict_member_roles` and "enforce permits" saw a stripped Admin role as harmless; when the lock ended the member held Administrator.
*Avoid:* a check that judges a role dangerous reads `lockdown::unlocked_info` (live permissions OR the active locks' saved ones): `sticky` (3 places), `verify::grant`, `antinuke::guard_perms`, `enforce_permits`, `events::public`. Active locks are kept in memory (`lockdown::active_locks`, also read by `guard_lock`), forgotten on every lock write in `lockdown.rs`: these checks run per join and per audit entry. `info` shows live permissions on purpose; `setup` checks the bot's own role (never locked).

**A role's Discord permissions made its members staff.**
Automod, activity heat and auto slowmode exempted anyone whose roles had Manage Messages, Administrator and the like (`App::staff_by_perms`, `slowmode::exempt`), during a Role lock by the saved permissions too. A raider given such a role before a panic stayed exempt for the whole lock; anyone with Manage Roles could hand out exemptions. Two sources of trust: permits and Discord permission bits.
*Avoid:* use our permits, not Discord permissions (owner decision). Exemptions, staff status and immunity come only from permits, whitelists and Owner/Extra Owner/Trusted Admin (`App::whitelisted`). Discord permissions only judge whether a role or channel is dangerous or visible. A failed permit lookup counts the message (`slowmode::exempt`): lookups 429 during a raid, and failing open exempted every raider. `misc.staff_only_permitted` has no effect now: still parsed so old configs load, values kept, hidden from the dashboard.

**A new delete type was not watched.**
Deleting threads and forum posts (audit type 112) was not an anti-nuke action. *Avoid:* the "Thread & forum post deletions" card (`Action::ThreadDelete`, opt-in, nothing to restore). A new action needs the enum, `ALL`, `watched`, the dashboard's `NUKE_CARDS` and the UI's `NUKE_WORDS`.

**A lost gateway session lost its audit entries.**
Discord replays missed events only on a resume. After a new session (`Ready`) the audit entries made in the gap never arrived, so a nuke during a reconnect was never counted. *Avoid:* `welcome::saw_audit` keeps the newest audit id per guild; `on_ready` runs anti-nuke on the entries after it (`catch_up_audit`). The last handled id is saved in `audit_seen`, loaded before the gateway (`load_audit_seen`), so the first `Ready` after a restart reads entries made while the bot was down too.
A replay is not a live event:
(1) A replayed entry counts at its own time (snowflake), never now (`antinuke::replay_age`, `AntiNuke::on_actions_at`, `action_log.created_at` backdated, no heat); older than the hour window it is not counted; a burst in the gap still trips.
(2) No gateway event, so its category comes from the channel or the latest backup (`replayed_parent`); a ticket made and closed in the gap is counted.
(3) Take the start point in the gateway loop before the `Ready` task is spawned (`note_ready`); a second `Ready` keeps the older start; take the floor before `on_ready`'s first await.
(4) Live and catch-up bring the same entries: `saw_audit` returns whether an entry is new (last 1000 ids per guild); both run anti-nuke only then. The dedupe ring (2500) must exceed a whole catch-up plus the live entries meanwhile.
(5) Page back with `before` (newest first) up to 1000 entries; past that, or when every retry fails, the Anti Nuke log says the gap was not checked (the gap is unpinned).
(6) Batch the `audit_seen` write every 2 s (`done_audit`, `flush_audit`): an UPSERT on one row per guild in the event path serialised a nuke's entries on its row lock.
(7) "Replayed" is a flag from the catch-up (`antinuke::on_replayed`), never inferred from the host clock (a clock an hour fast made live entries too old to count).
(8) A replayed entry re-judges every window that holds it, with the live entries already counted after it (`on_actions_at`): a nuke split across a reconnect tripped nothing.
(9) Past the hour window an entry is still logged with `counted` 0 (revertable); the Anti Nuke log says how many were not counted.
(10) The saved id never passes an entry still being handled (`audit_inflight`, left when its `InFlight` guard drops, also on panic; a 30 min expiry only bounds a leak) or a gap whose catch-up has not succeeded (`audit_pinned`); `action_log.audit_id` lets a catch-up skip entries counted before a crash lost the batched id.
(11) Record every handled entry in `audit_handled` (tripwires, lock set-backs, voice and exempt entries too; not when anti-nuke is off or for the bot's own entries; pruned after a day); a catch-up skips them and every `action_log.audit_id`: replaying a tripwire entry from before a restart took back a role an Owner had since re-granted.
(12) Replayed tripwire entries have no age limit, on purpose: `guard_perms`, `guard_permit_roles` and the holds judge a grant still in force, so an old one is undone and punished like a new one; only counting has the hour window.

**Staff could take a lock apart one channel at a time.**
During an active lock, deleting a locked overwrite, removing its deny or giving a stripped role its permissions back went unwatched (overwrite changes are not a watched action, a role's permission change counted only as a rename), and nothing reapplied the lock. *Avoid:* `antinuke::guard_lock` checks every role update and overwrite update or delete against the active locks' snapshots (`role_perms`, `channel_denies`, `prior`): the change is set back from the audit entry, logged, and counted on the Channel Updates or Role Updates card (when on). An Owner, Extra Owner or Trusted Admin is only logged. Every check in `on_audit` runs whatever an earlier one returned and the first error is returned at the end (`keep`): a `?` on `guard_perms` or `hold_overwrite` skipped the set-back, and one on the kick/ban hold skipped the count (D21).

**Panic's backup load deleted what the bot made after the backup.**
A restore deletes every live role and channel missing from the image, protecting only the Quarantine role and log channels. Open ticket channels, a remade verification channel and Auto Setup's roles made after the last backup were deleted. *Avoid:* `sections::restore_keep` keeps a fixed list of config fields (`KEEP_FIELDS`: quarantine role, log channels, ticket category and log channel, anti-nuke's ticket categories, verification channel and roles), the permit roles and open tickets' channels; the bot's restore (panic, `/backup load`) and the dashboard's preview both use it. Do not keep every id in the config: most fields are Editor level, so an Editor could list a nuker's Administrator role in an automod list and panic would leave it. A keep or exempt list built from config names the fields it trusts.

**A quarantine left channel access from overwrites.**
Quarantine swapped roles only. A member's own allow overwrite, or one for a managed role they keep (booster, a bot's role), still let them read and act (an allow beats the Quarantine role's deny); a new overwrite for a quarantined member was never checked. *Avoid:* after a real quarantine `contain` calls `hide_channels` (own step, after the role change and the `quarantines` row, so `save_row`/`restore_row` are untouched): in each channel that grants them access it adds a member overwrite denying those bits and saves the old one in `quarantine_overwrites`; `Inverse::Unquarantine` puts them back. `hold_overwrite` undoes a member overwrite that gives a quarantined member new access and quarantines the actor. A managed role's overwrite that later grants more (`hold_role_overwrite`) gets the same member deny for every really quarantined member who holds the role, without punishing the actor (staff set up booster channels). Join quarantines are left alone.
Access taken back works both ways: a lost deny is a grant like a new allow, so clearing the quarantine's own member deny or deleting that overwrite (`ChannelOverwriteDelete` goes to `hold_overwrite` too) is put back and punished; a managed role's lost deny gets the member deny. A "more access" check reads both the allow and the deny change. When the actor's quarantine whitelist cannot be read, both holds (and `guard_perms`, `guard_permit_roles`; `guard_lock` sets back without counting) undo the grant but quarantine nobody and say so in the Anti Nuke log (`hold_whitelisted`; owner decision, undo not punish): returning on the error left the member what they were given. In a nuke the hiding runs after the revert (`contain_with(.., false)` in `revert_and_report`) so it does not hold up recreating deleted channels.

**The bot's own rows were undone like anyone's.**
`/revert actor:@Brim` lifted every quarantine the bot made, gave a nuking bot its managed role's Administrator back, and unbanned automatic bans.
*Avoid:* the bot counts as an immune target (Owner or Extra Owner with `include_owners`), and a revert of the bot's rows never runs Unquarantine or RolePerms (`antinuke::bot_contain_op`; the dashboard's Revert Center skips both and lists the bot as immune too).

**A resumable run read its intent from the state it was changing.**
Channel Setup Push decided "this channel follows its category" from live Discord. Once the category's overwrites were pushed and a child's step failed, the child no longer looked synced, read as an exception on the next push, and stayed public.
*Avoid:* a run resumed by recomputing a diff stores every decision derived from the old state before its first step (Push writes `reset` into the draft for each follower), and stops at the first failed step instead of running the rest (deletes included) against a half-changed server.

**A per-process flag does not stop a second writer.**
Push guarded itself with a static `HashSet` of guilds, so a second dashboard process could push at once, and a draft PUT or DELETE during a push was overwritten by the push's write-back. A stale PUT after a push brought back `new-*` keys already created; the next push created them twice.
*Avoid:* hold a Postgres advisory lock for the whole run (`pg_try_advisory_xact_lock` in a transaction the run owns, so it is freed when the task ends or the process dies) and refuse other writers with 409 while held. Record keys a run has used up (`next`) and refuse them when they come back. Run the steps in `tokio::spawn` and await the handle, so a dropped request does not stop a run half way; log what will happen before the first step and the result after; record a failed save instead of returning with `?`.

**A dropped sqlx transaction kept the push lock after the answer.**
sqlx only queues the ROLLBACK on drop; it runs when the pooled connection is returned in a spawned task, so the advisory lock (`lock`, `pg_try_advisory_xact_lock`) outlived the response and the next PUT or Push got "a push is running" (about 1 in 60 runs of `draft_is_saved_discarded_and_pushed_only_when_safe`).
*Avoid:* a transaction that holds an advisory lock which others try-lock ends with an awaited `rollback` or `commit` on every path before the answer, early returns included (`release` in `dashboard/src/channels.rs`; the whole push runs in one task that releases at the end). Drop is only the fallback for a cancelled request or dead process. Other `pg_advisory_xact_lock` users (`bot/settings`, `bot/tickets`, `dashboard/store.rs`, `core/cases.rs`) wait instead of trying, so a late ROLLBACK only delays them.

**No row lock across Discord calls.**
The Raider List lift held `SELECT … FOR UPDATE` on its action row while it unbanned, so a slow Discord held the lock for the HTTP timeout and a second run waited. The lock was only there to make the case and notice happen once, which a claim does without waiting.
*Avoid:* read without a lock, make the Discord call (it must be safe to repeat: the unban checks the ban is still the list's), then claim with `DELETE … ` and act on `rows_affected == 1`; only the claimer writes the case and log. A lift that gives up after its last try posts "Raider List removal failed" to the mod log (`raiders::lift_gave_up`). Left as is: `appeals` decide (a person's click) and `backup::track_created` (needs the lock; short GET).

**A transferred server kept its old owner's trust.**
Ban Sync groups servers by Discord owner, so a server transferred to you joined your group with sharing on and could ban people in all your servers; the DM said it had left its group. Its Extra Owners and Trusted Admins (set by the old owner) kept full control. An owner change only moved the server between groups; it never asked whether the new owner wants what came with it.
*Avoid:*
- On an owner change (`bansync::on_owner`, GuildCreate and GuildUpdate) switch `ban_sync.enabled` off and remove EO/TA in one transaction with the `guild_owners` update and a config version, store what was removed (`owner_handovers`, migration 3920), and let only the new Discord owner keep it (Overview row "Ownership changed", `ops::keep_staff`). Keeping never turns Ban Sync on; the new owner does that with 2FA, and it then groups with their own servers.
- The dashboard memoizes the Discord owner for 30 s, so the old owner could still switch Ban Sync on or add himself as Extra Owner: `api::is_discord_owner` (used by every owner-only write and `level_of`) also refuses when `guild_owners` names someone else or the user is `owner_handovers.old_owner`.
- EO/TA were not all: permits the old owner set stayed on (his alt with an owner, full-immunity or dashboard-edit permit kept power); the card gave back all staff with one click and showed only counts; a rollback to a version from before the change wrote old EO/TA, permit roles and `ban_sync.enabled = true` back; `keep_staff` took row locks before the config lock (reverse of `on_owner`: deadlock); `on_removed` deleted the `guild_owners` row, so an owner change while the bot was out looked like a first sight. Fix: `on_owner` also deletes the roles of permits with `owner`/`dashboard_edit` or the `all` whitelist and stores them (`owner_handovers.paused_roles`, migration 3921), names the weaker permits that stayed on (`other_permits`, listed by the card and the Ban Sync page: `ops::weaker_permits`), and records a handover row on every owner change.
