Overview shows one row per paused person or role (name, id, previous owner marked), each with its own Keep, plus Dismiss. `api::rollback` keeps EO, TA, the roles of strong permits and Ban Sync off for a version older than `owner_handovers.at` and says so (`held`). Every dashboard writer of the permit tables takes the config lock first (`store::write_permits`, `keep_staff`, `claim_key`). `guild_owners` rows stay when the bot leaves; groups skip servers in `guild_left`; the next GuildCreate compares with the kept owner.
- The pause list named only `owner`, `dashboard_edit` and `all`, so permits with `antinuke`, `quarantine` or a `wl_*` flag, and the `antinuke.quarantine_whitelist` user ids, kept their exemptions; the rollback hold-back looked at the old version alone, so a permit made weak before the change got its guarded perms back. Fix: one rule in core, `permits::guarded`: every permit is guarded except perms outside `GUARDED_PERMS` with only `WEAK_FLAGS` (filter and report flags), so a new flag is guarded by default; `on_owner` and the dashboard's rollback both use it. `on_owner` clears the quarantine whitelist into `owner_handovers.quarantine_whitelist` (migration 3923), kept per person on Overview. A rollback never makes a role guarded again: a permit guarded in the old version keeps its current roles when guarded now, keeps its current perms when weak now, and comes back without roles when gone.
- The Ban Sync copy cancel ran after the commit, so a failure left the old group's copies pending and the retry found the owner recorded and did nothing: the cancel is in the transaction; after the commit only cache drops and best-effort DMs run.
- A failed `on_owner` was not retried, so the new owner had no Owner level until the next guild event: events call `bansync::owner_seen`, which queues `owner_check` on failure (reads the owner from Discord, retries on the job lease). The dashboard checks the memoized owner against a Discord answer at most 2 s old (`Discord::guild_owner_fresh`; a lookup error counts as not the owner): when Discord confirms the user but `guild_owners` still names the previous owner, the user keeps Owner level and a retry is queued, but owner-only writes (Ban Sync, Extra Owners, Keep, purge) wait until the bot has paused the previous owner's staff.
- A retry dropped after repeated failures was queued again on every dashboard request and filled the log: `owner_state` queues at most one `owner_check` per server and hour (per dashboard process). While that reset waited, the real owner could not remove the previous owner's Extra Owners: `put_permits` allows an Extra Owners change that only removes people when Discord confirms the user (`owner_state == Some(false)`), never one that adds.

**Ban Sync's hourly cap was counted outside the row's transaction.**
`share_job` and `import_job` each counted the users shared in the last hour, then wrote rows; a share and an owner-started import at once both saw room and passed the 30 per hour cap.
*Avoid:* `offer` counts and writes in one transaction under `pg_advisory_xact_lock(hashtextextended('ban_sync_cap:' || source, 0))`, and says whether it hit the cap. Send the rest stays outside the cap on purpose (owner's choice). See "A count checked a cap, then a second statement inserted" (#183).

**A button read its state, acted, then marked it done.**
The Raider List alert's Ban/Kick/Quarantine read `action = 'alert'`, ran the punishment and only then set the row. Two staff pressing at once both passed: two cases, DMs and mod logs, or a ban and a kick.
*Avoid:* claim first with `UPDATE ... WHERE action = 'alert'` and act only on `rows_affected == 1`; put the row back when nothing was done. Left as is: Ban Sync's Ban already claims first; appeals decide claims after the unban (safe to repeat; case and log come after the claim).

**A staff button skipped the panic lock and the rate limit.**
The Raider List alert's Allow button wrote the allow straight away, while Ban, Kick and Quarantine go through `commands::apply`/`modcmds::run`, which call `gated`. During panic mode's mod lock, or past the rate limit, a hacked staff account could still let listed raiders in.
*Avoid:* every button or command that acts for staff calls `commands::gated` with the permit it acts under before it changes anything, also when it only lifts or allows.

**Advisory locks on raw ids share one key space.**
`report` locked the reporting owner's user id, then `add` locked the reported user's id, both as plain `pg_advisory_xact_lock(id)`. Owner A reporting B while B reports A took the two keys in opposite order: deadlock.
*Avoid:* give each kind of lock its own key, `pg_advisory_xact_lock(hashtextextended('<kind>:' || id, 0))` (as `raider_user:`, `raider_owner:`, `channel_drafts:`). Other raw locks (`cases::create`, ticket numbers, config saves) take one guild id per transaction, so they cannot cross.

**"It is gone, so an earlier try of mine removed it" needs proof.**
The lift took a missing ban to mean an earlier try had unbanned, and wrote an unban case in the bot's name even when staff had unbanned by a path the list never saw.
*Avoid:* record the intent (`raider_actions.unbanning = true`) just before the Discord call; only a retry that finds that mark writes the missed case.

**A count checked a cap, then a second statement inserted.**
`token_create` counted live MCP tokens, then inserted in a separate statement: parallel requests at 9 tokens all saw room (up to 20 made against a cap of 10). `propose` (open MCP changes, each a DM to the owner) and message reports (10 per reporter an hour) had the same gap. A single `INSERT … SELECT … WHERE (SELECT count(*)) < n` does not help under READ COMMITTED: parallel statements do not see each other's uncommitted rows.
*Avoid:* count and insert in one transaction under `pg_advisory_xact_lock(hashtextextended('<kind>:' || key, 0))` (`mcp_tokens:` and `mcp_writes:` global, `reports:` per guild and reporter), taken right before the insert and never held across a Discord call. A unique index is enough when it already lets only one win (`appeals_one_open`). Already safe: appeals daily limit (unique open index); Raider List report cap, ticket numbers, config saves (locked); Ban Sync hourly cap (sequential jobs; an owner's import can overlap a share job by a few, left as is); in-memory caps (rescue claims, verification remakes, 2FA fails) check and push under one Mutex.

**A ticket was marked closed before its log and channel delete, and a half-opened one blocked the member.**
`staff::close` set `closed_at` first, so a restart before the delete left a live channel that no longer counted as a ticket (no close, no relay), and a second close returned silently. `dm::create` took the number with `channel_id = NULL`; a restart before the channel was made left that row open forever, and the unique open-ticket index then refused every new ticket of that member in that server.
*Avoid:* tickets have a `status` (`creating`, `open`, `closing`, `closed`, migration 3930; no CHECK against `closed_at` yet, so an image rollback can still close). A close claims `open` → `closing` and queues `ticket_close_check` in the same transaction, treats a 404 on the channel delete as done, then sets `closed`; the check closes a ticket still `closing` whose channel is gone and opens any other one again (staff told). `on_channel_delete` leaves a `closing` ticket to its close. `claim_number` removes the member's own `creating` row older than 10 minutes; a create whose row was removed meanwhile deletes its channel. A member DM that finds its ticket channel gone (deleted while the bot was offline) closes that ticket and asks for the message again. `closeall` takes only `open` tickets. Not covered yet: a channel made just before a restart stays as an empty orphan (phase C).

**A quarantine row was read as "still contained".**
A nuke run decided "already quarantined, only revert the late entries" from the row alone. The row stays when a Quarantine Whitelist user gives the roles back by hand, with Quarantine Hold off, or when the hold's undo fails, so a nuker released and nuking again was only reverted: no new quarantine, case or owner alert. A later quarantine also kept the old saved roles (`ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING`), so a demoted mod got the old Mod role back on release.
*Avoid:* decide from Discord whether the stored state still holds. `still_contained` needs the row and the member holding only what `quarantine_roles` leaves (`held`), and for a bot its managed role cleared; a member who left falls back to the row; a failed read counts as not contained. A new real quarantine saves today's roles and restarts `created_at` only when the member holds a role quarantine would take and joined before the row was written (a rejoin with autoroles, a member with no roles, or one missing a recreated Quarantine role keeps the valid list). The failure undo (`restore_row`) is a compare-and-set on the row version `save_row` wrote (`xmin`, new on every write; `xmax = 0` tells an insert from an update), so a quarantine written meanwhile is never deleted or downgraded; a re-applied one (rejoin, sticky roles: `Kind::Again`) keeps the saved list, and a failed role change puts the old row back. `dehoist`, `timeout_roles`, `sticky::due`, `commands` and `info` still treat any row as held, which only holds back more.

**A failed side step skipped the protection after it.**
`guard_perms` undid the change with `?` before quarantining, so when the undo failed (role above the bot, a 403) the actor was not quarantined; the hold did the same. The nuke report sent its log with `?` before the owner DM, so a nuker who hid the log channel from the bot also kept the owner from hearing of the nuke.
*Avoid:* an undo, a log or any other side step logs its error and lets the protection (contain, alert) run. Same pattern in `panic.rs` `start` and the other `?` under D13: fixed by g2a (see the `panic::note` D13 note above).

**The quarantine overwrite hid the verification channel.**
`enforce_quarantine` (ChannelCreate and ChannelUpdate), `t!setup` step 5 and the dashboard's Auto Setup and score Fix denied the Quarantine role View Channel in every channel, the verification channel too. With "quarantine on join" a joiner could not see the Verify button and was kicked at the time limit. A ChannelCreate of a channel the bot just made can arrive before the bot saved it as the verification channel.
*Avoid:* the verification channel gets its own quarantine overwrite, not a skip: allow exactly View Channel and Read Message History (an extra allow such as Manage Messages breaks the shape), deny the rest of `QUARANTINE_DENY` plus Use Application Commands and Use External Apps (`brim_antinuke::verification_shape`). Skipping it left a real quarantine free to send, react, make threads and invites there, never repaired and hidden from the score; the channel id is an Editor setting. Every writer uses the shape: `ensure_channel`, the remake after a delete (replaces the copied quarantine overwrite), `t!setup` step 5, `enforce_quarantine` (also repairs a channel D55 already hit), the dashboard's Setup Channel, `quarantine_channels`, Channel Setup's push; the score checks for it. Setup Channel also puts the shape back once the channel is saved (`reshape_verification`): with verification off the bot denies a new unsaved channel, and nothing repaired it when verification was switched on later. A shaped channel that is not the saved one counts as a new verification channel only while verification is on and no live channel is saved (a saved id that 404s counts as none); `enforce_quarantine` takes `verify::CHANNEL_LOCK` only for that check, because the bot saves the channel it made under the lock and its ChannelCreate can come first.

**A write to the permit tables without a config version came back with a rollback.**
`statics 10 ?remove` deleted a Trusted Admin straight from the table, and the rescue claim added an Extra Owner the same way. Every config version keeps a `permits` copy, so a rollback rewrote the removed TA, a Permits page opened before the removal saved it back (version check passed), and History's "Diff vs current" compared two stale copies. One piece of state in two places (tables and version copy), with writers updating only one.
*Avoid:* every write to `extra_owners`, `trusted_admins`, `permits` or `permit_roles` runs in a config-version transaction: the dashboard's `store::commit` and the bot's `settings::save` take the copy from the live tables (`permits::snapshot`), and a table-only change appends a version (`store::bump`, the bot's `locked` + `append`). Read permits you write back after any Discord call (Auto Setup). Take the config lock before the first permit-table write (`store::write_permits` does): the bot's TA edit locks first, so a dashboard path that wrote rows first and locked in `commit` deadlocked with it.

**A cache filled after an await kept an older value than the NOTIFY that cleared it.**
`App::cfg` read the newest config, awaited, then inserted it. A save whose NOTIFY removed the entry between the read and the insert left the older config cached until the next save. *Avoid:* the config cache has a generation that every forget moves, and a load caches only under the generation it started with (`CfgCache`). Any lazy cache with an external invalidation needs the same.

**A save after a Discord call lost the race to an unrelated save.**
`set_bot_actions` read the config, and after the AutoMod rule was made in Discord, committed with that version; any save in between answered 409 and the rule had no bot actions. *Avoid:* a config write that follows a side effect reads the version under the config lock in its own transaction (`store::locked`). Keep Discord calls outside that lock.

**A slot freed only by the next write locked the page.**
A profile change holds a `reserved` row so one runs at a time; a row left by a restart (an Update restarts the dashboard) was freed only when the next change came in, but the page greyed its buttons while any row was reserved, so none could. *Avoid:* free stale claims on every path that reads them (`profile::sweep` in both the page load and the write), and finish a claim with `where status = 'reserved'` so a late answer cannot overwrite what the sweep wrote.

**An idle timer fed only by gateway events counts the bot's downtime as quiet.**
`last_activity_at` is set when the bot sees a message; Discord does not replay messages sent while the bot was offline. The first sweep after a restart would close a ticket the member wrote in an hour ago.
*Avoid:* before an idle action, read the channel's `last_message_id` from Discord (a snowflake holds its time) and skip the action when it is newer than the cutoff.

**A retried job counted its own attempt again.**
The verification deadline counted a time out before the kick or ban ran. When the removal failed, the job ran again and counted again, so one real time out could reach the 3 that bans, and each failed ban left an unban job behind. A wave of joiners that never verify could also fill the invite cap with retry invites.
*Avoid:* count an attempt only after the action it counts has worked; remove a job queued for an action that then fails; cap anything a joiner can make the bot create (retry invites: 20 per server per hour, none during a raid or lock, none with verification off). Keep such counts in the database (`verify_timeouts`), not in memory: the bot restarts several times a day. A delayed job about a member must check the member is still in the same stay: the verification deadline skips a member who joined less than the time limit ago (a quick rejoin). A timed unban must lift only its own ban: the verification time-out unban checks the ban reason first, so a ban staff put on top stays.

**A try limit was checked in one statement and counted in another.**
MCP confirm compared the DM code in one UPDATE and counted a wrong code in a second, so parallel confirms all reached the compare before the count hit 3: about N guesses per change instead of 3. The cap was per change and a refused change frees its slot, so a token could loop propose and guess without end (each change DMs the owner).
*Avoid:* the compare and the count are one UPDATE with `attempts < MAX_TRIES` in its WHERE, so the row lock queues parallel guesses and no code is compared past the cap; a right code costs no try. A token that sends 9 wrong codes in 24 hours (three changes' worth, counted by `attempts`, so a change left to expire still counts; refused-only counting let two misses and an expiry escape) is revoked with its open changes closed (`revoke_if_guessing`); the claim UPDATE also needs a live token (a confirm already past the token check cannot run); the owner gets a DM and makes a new token to use MCP again (owner decision). Already atomic: dashboard 2FA (`auth::attempt`, one mutex), rescue claim (one mutex), job queue.

**A second code was free to guess, it shared the dashboard's lockout, and a cut-off change was stored as failed.**
After a right DM code, a wrong authenticator code put the MCP change back to pending without counting a try, so one DM code could be replayed with new authenticator guesses until the owner's 2FA locked, and that lockout was the one dashboard login uses: an AI client could lock the owner out of the dashboard. The change ran inside the request, so when the MCP server gave up after 30 s the handler could be cut off halfway, and the 15-minute sweep then wrote `failed` for a change that may have run; the AI saw "dashboard unreachable" and would propose it again. Each guard counted only the first factor, and the request that the client could drop wrote the outcome.
*Avoid:* every wrong code on a change counts a try, whichever factor (`reopen(true)` in `mcp::confirm`), and counts towards the token's 24-hour revoke; MCP authenticator misses have their own lockout in `TotpAttempts` (`mcp_fails`) with the replay guard shared; the change runs in its own task and writes its outcome there (not after awaiting the task in the dropped request); every status write after a claim needs `status = 'running'`; a cut-off or erroring run is `unknown`, and a confirm of a closed change says what became of it. `auth::attempt` has only these two callers; the bot's hourly sweep (`welcome.rs`) now writes `unknown` instead of `failed`. The DM names the server with its id first (`server_label`): a name is set by server staff and could copy another server's name and id; `plain` also strips control, bidi and zero-width characters.

**One fixed MCP token read and wrote everything, and the AI saw more than it needed.**
`MCP_TOKEN` in `.env` was shared by every AI client, could only be changed on the server, and its holder read every server with no trace staff could see; the OAuth login got the write tools too; the AI read NSFW scan rows and member ids and names in log lines. Access was granted per installation, not per client, and reads were not logged.
*Avoid:* every MCP login is a dashboard token (`tbmcp_`, checked by hash through `mcp_token_name`, revoked on the dashboard and refused at once) or an OAuth login that only reads (no write tools listed, a write call refused); writes forward the caller's own token, so the dashboard checks the same login. Each read of a server writes `mcp_reads` before the answer goes out; a read the log cannot record is refused. The MCP role writes only that insert, in an explicit `SET TRANSACTION READ WRITE` (its default is read-only); the grant names only `guild_id, tool, via` (with its own `id` or `at` it could block or bury the real rows); only tools that take a `guild_id` log it, and `jobs` without one logs every server it listed. `mcp_token_name` is SECURITY DEFINER with `public.mcp_tokens` and `pg_temp` last, so a temp table cannot shadow it; a token of the wrong shape is refused before any query. A Raider List verify acts like a confirm, so it alerts too. `redact` masks member ids and names and keeps server, channel, role and message ids. The owner gets a security alert DM (a queued `raider_notify` job, so it outlives an update's restart) on Raider List confirms, updates and token changes (owner decision).
## 3. Automod

**A short "gg" from seven members made a crowd.**
Every short text's full heat went into the shared crowd pool, so seven members who each typed "gg" within about a second were all deleted, struck and timed out. *Avoid:* a text under 10 characters (after `cure` and the alphanumeric filter) with no link and no mention never joins a crowd pool (`Crowd::MIN_POOL_CHARS`); it still heats its author. Crowd defaults are unchanged.

**An unread level, whitelist or strike row left the scam or spam message posted.**
`check_message` used `?` on the whitelist and level reads (a member GET per tripped message, in the bucket the bans use), and `strike_member` on the strike read and write, all before the delete. *Avoid:* read the level once per message from the message's own member roles (`author_level` through `App::cached_level`; a member fetch only without a member, none for a webhook); the words, links, automod, crowd and trip checks all use it. Moving only the trip read left a member GET per blacklisted link for the whitelist. The level cache also serves `punish_crowd`'s `whitelisted` reads. A failed read deletes the message, punishes nobody and returns the error; `strike_member` deletes the tracked messages when the strike read or write fails.

**The raid ID-farm check was quadratic under the raid lock.**
`flagged` searched a `Vec` of farm ids per joiner and `farm_ids` compared every bucket with every other (about 25 million steps per join for a farm of 5000), under `app.raids`, which every server shares. *Avoid:* `farm_ids` returns a `HashSet` and counts each bucket's margin with sorted buckets and prefix sums. Scanning every bucket when the margin spans more buckets than exist stays quadratic, and nothing caps that (`id_margin_ms` of a year at Hour granularity). Work under a shared lock stays near linear in its input, whatever a setting says.

**A crowd trip deleted the owner's post, and that post counted towards the crowd.**
Every message went into `Crowd::on_text` before the immunity check, so the owner and two members posting one link made a crowd of three; `punish_crowd` deleted every post and only skipped the strike. *Avoid:* immune and `automod`-whitelisted authors never join a crowd (`crowd_exempt` in `check_message`, from the message's own member roles: a member fetch per message would queue behind the punishments in a raid; a failed lookup counts them in).

**One spammer's own heat made the next member who said the same short text a crowd member.**
The crowd pool added every post's heat with no cap per member, and "tripped within the window" took any text. *Avoid:* cap each member's share of a crowd pool at half the max (as `ChannelHeat::shares`); only a long text or link joins a crowd that tripped in the window.

**A trailing dot in a link host skipped every domain check.**
`evil.com.` is `evil.com` to a browser, but missed the phishing, NSFW, blacklisted domain and shortener checks. *Avoid:* `filters::hosts` and `sites::host_of` trim trailing dots; `SiteList::parse` trims them from entries too.

**A downloaded domain list could hold an entry that hits normal chat.**
The phishing and NSFW lists come from a third-party repo with no guard, so an entry like `discord.com` or `co.uk` would act on normal links in every server. *Avoid:* `SiteList::parse` refuses and logs `too_broad` entries: a fixed list of sites chat links to (and their subdomains), bare shared hosting suffixes, and two-label country suffixes.

**The scam-link filter hit Discord's own image links.**
`is_nitro_steam_scam` did not list `discordapp.net` (`media.discordapp.net`), `discord.gift`, `discord.new` or `discordstatus.com` as official, so the host check ("contains discord, not official") flagged them. Each hit is `malicious` (full heat, not skipped by the automod permit) and counts as Raider List scam evidence. *Avoid:* a brand-host check lists every official domain of that brand. The lure words still match inside other words on purpose ("freenitro").

**Spam similarity was slow under the lock every server shares.**
`check_message` holds `app.heat` while `heat` compares a message with up to 5 earlier ones (500x500 Levenshtein each), so a long-message spam raid slowed automod in every server. Moving the comparison out of the lock fails: two messages of one member run in parallel and each must see the other in the history, or a burst of copies is missed. *Avoid:* keep the read, score and remember under one lock; make the score cheap. `best_similarity` returns 1.0 for an identical text and skips a comparison whose length gap already caps it below the best so far.

**A normal join paid two Discord calls for Raider List evidence.**
`join_raid` always calls `raiders::raid_flagged`, and `add_evidence` looked the server up (guild with counts, the bot's member) before it checked the list, also when nobody was flagged. Those calls ran before verification. *Avoid:* `add_evidence` returns at once for an empty list. A raid still pays them once per flagged batch.

**A Discord AutoMod rule edited by someone without a permit still drove Brim's punishments.**
Brim runs its own actions (timeout, kick, ban) on hits of a native rule. Anyone with Manage Server can change that rule's keywords, so a member without a permit could make Brim punish innocent messages. *Avoid:* `native::on_rule_audit` reads the audit entry of every rule create or update (live and replayed, anti-nuke on or off). A change by someone who is not Owner, EO, TA or a `dashboard_edit` permit puts the rule in `automod_rule_untrusted` (migration 3925), and `on_native_hit` skips it until a trusted change. A failed permit lookup counts as untrusted; it runs once per entry inside the D20 inflight guard; the newest audit id per rule wins, so a replayed or slower older trusted entry cannot clear a newer untrusted one. Hits between the gateway's rule update and its audit entry wait up to 30 s (`native::rule_changed`). The bot's own entries keep the verdict, since its keyword sync rewrites only part of a rule; a dashboard edit marks the rule trusted itself (`lockdown::trust_rule`). A rollback drops an old `ban_sync` section. The bot's own keyword rule is synced back by `automod::on_rule_change`; no other Discord-side setting feeds a Brim action.

**The audit-log guard for AutoMod rules misses changes it never sees.**
`automod_rule_untrusted` is filled only from audit entries. A bot without View Audit Log, more than 1000 entries while it was down, or a change made before the deploy leaves no verdict, so the rule still drives Brim's actions. *Avoid:* the bot acts only on a rule state a trusted person approved (`automod_rule_approved`, migration 3926). `discord_api::rule_fingerprint` hashes everything that decides hits (lists sorted, missing fields read as empty), so the twilight model and Discord's JSON give one hash (test `twilight_and_discord_json_give_one_fingerprint`). Approvals come from: a dashboard create, edit or switch (`discord_api::approve_rule` in `lockdown.rs`, with the rule Discord returns; a switch approves only if nothing but `enabled` changed since the stored approval, else the Editor is told to review and save it); a trusted actor's audit entry (`native::on_rule_audit` fetches the rule); the bot's keyword sync (actor = bot). The bot keeps each rule's latest fingerprint from gateway create and update events and a fetch of the guild's rules on a miss; a failed fetch takes no action. At GuildCreate, while the guild has no done marker, rules without an approval are approved once (`approved_by` 0, the rule-0 row marks the guild done) unless `automod_rule_untrusted` holds them. A not-approved hit logs once per rule and fingerprint. The audit guard and its 30 s hold stay as a second layer and to name who changed the rule.

**The first-start rule approval ran at the first hit and stopped on a log failure.**
`first_start` ran from the first native hit, so a rule changed between deploy and that hit was approved in its changed state, and a failed approval log (`?`) skipped the guild's done marker, so the next fetch approved again. *Avoid:* `native::on_guild_create` runs the fetch and the one-time approval while the marker row is missing; a hit only fills the cache. Log failures are warnings, never `?` before a marker or a state write (as in `drop_bots`). A guild that adds the bot later, or returns after the purge deleted its marker, gets its rules approved once at that GuildCreate (test `a_guild_back_after_the_purge_gets_its_rules_approved_once`).

**The keyword sync approved a rule it only partly wrote.**
`sync_keyword_rule` sent only the keywords, regex, action and switch, and the bot approved the rule Discord returned, so an exempt role or channel or allow list added in Discord by someone without a permit was kept and approved by the next sync. *Avoid:* the sync owns every field the fingerprint covers: the full `trigger_metadata` (allow list empty), `exempt_roles` from the roles of permits whose whitelist has `words` or `all` (`automod::word_exempt_roles`: only roles that exist and may carry a permit, at most 20), and `exempt_channels` empty. Only then is approving what Discord returns right. `on_rule_change` compares the whole rule (`rule_matches` folds keyword case, then compares fingerprints), so any other change in Discord is synced back. Permit saves write a config version, whose NOTIFY runs the sync. Discord's exempt roles exempt every holder of the role, while Brim's own filter reads only the member's top permit; the local filter still counts every word (`synced_words_still_count_locally`).

**A trusted audit entry approved a later change.**
`on_rule_audit` cleared the hold (`seen_audit`) before it fetched the rule, and approved whatever the fetch returned, also when a newer gateway update had already changed it. *Avoid:* write the approval before `seen_audit`, and only when no gateway update of the rule came more than `LATER_MS` (5 s) after the entry's snowflake time; that later change waits for its own entry. A failed fetch is logged in the AutoMod log and approves nothing.

**The rule cache outlived a new gateway session.**
`RULES` and `FILLED` were filled once per process. A new session (not a resume) can miss rule events, so a held fingerprint could be old. *Avoid:* GuildCreate clears the guild's `FILLED` flag and `RULES` entries (under the `FILL` lock); the next hit or the first-start fetch reads them again.

**Three throwaway servers are three owners.**
Strong evidence from 3 different server owners listed a user by itself. Anyone can make 3 accounts, give each a new server and invite Brim, then get any user listed and banned in every server that enforces the list. Root cause: "different owners" was taken as "independent witnesses" without asking what an owner costs.
*Avoid:* evidence keeps its source server's member count and the time the bot joined it (`Detail.members`, `Detail.bot_joined`); only servers with `RAIDER_MIN_MEMBERS` members that had the bot for `RAIDER_MIN_DAYS` count; a bot account never lists by itself; an automatic listing only alerts until the bot owner verifies it (`UNVERIFIED_ALERT_ONLY`). One owner's raid or spam detection makes no entry until a second owner flagged the user. One person can still own three qualifying servers, so `RAIDER_AUTO_OWNERS` is 5. Other source counts are fine: Ban Sync counts the users a source shares (a rate limit, not a vote), and the dashboard's owner counts only sort the review queue.

**Filters only saw plain text.**
Links in `[x](https://..)`, `||spoilers||` or `**bold**`, lookalike letters, zero-width characters, spaced letters (`f u c k`), forwards, polls, user-installed app replies, display names and voice-channel prefixes got past the filters. *Avoid:* filter on `filters::urls` (finds links anywhere) and `filters::word_forms` (cured text). When Discord adds a new place for user text, run it through the instant filters too.

**decancer breaks `String += &String`.**
Its `AddAssign` impl makes `s += &other` ambiguous once decancer is in scope. *Avoid:* use `push_str`.

**Exemptions that were too wide or too narrow.**
Whitelisted channels and roles still got base message heat, other bots' slash-command replies counted as webhook spam, and saying "gg" twice in a quiet channel gave a timeout. *Avoid:* every new heat source checks the whitelist, and quiet-channel heat counts once per user. Add a test with both spammers and normal chatters in one channel.

**A bot rename reads as a new name.**
The post-join username filter dedupes on username + global name + nick. Its Rename action changes the nick, so the echo update looked like a new bad name and the bot would rename on every update; a dehoist nick on a member the filter already punished would punish them again. *Avoid:* for Rename the dedupe key leaves out the nick, and a clean nick counts as done (`joingate::post_join_key`). Auto-dehoist skips a name the filter matched, and its output always starts with a letter or digit, so dehoisting twice changes nothing.

**The verify button judged "suspicious" on other names than Join Gate.**
Join Gate checks the cured username and display name; the verification Start button checked only the raw username. A member caught by the username filter at join (lookalike letters or the display name) passed without a captcha in "suspicious only" mode. *Avoid:* build the name for `is_suspicious` with `joingate::names`. Discord's spammer flag has the same gap: only the join frame carries it, so the Start button asks Discord (`Api::public_flags`) when the flag would turn a Grant into a challenge, and a failed lookup means a captcha.

**Personal heat has no ceiling, so a pool can be filled by one member.**
"Heat punishes one member at 100% first" was used to drop auto slowmode's member count. But `HeatState::apply` never caps: one 20-mention message is 315% alone, and the pool is fed before the punishment gates (grace window, automod whitelist, failed timeouts, strikes off). One member could slow a channel, or every channel. *Avoid:* a shared pool caps each member's share at max heat (`ChannelHeat.shares`), and its trigger is above one share (`SLOW_PCT` from 200). The pool starts over when max heat or decay changes, or old heat outweighs the new caps. Before relying on "X stops it first", check that X has a ceiling and runs before the thing it guards.

**Some content arrives after the post.**
Discord adds link and GIF previews in a `MESSAGE_UPDATE` without `edited_timestamp`, and `events.rs` skipped those, so an image filter that only looked at new posts missed every linked image. *Avoid:* a filter on images or embeds also reads preview updates (only that filter, not the whole automod run), and is idempotent per message (a unique key), because the same message arrives again as an update, an edit and through the catch-up sweep.

**Text and images in places the filters did not read.**
The link, invite, scam and word filters read only content, forwarded content and polls, so a webhook or user-installed app could post a phishing link in an embed or link button with empty content. The NSFW scan, image heat and QR check skipped forwarded images, and a user-installed app's reply lost its attachments because it runs as an edit. Thread and reaction names were checked on raw text, and thread renames not at all. Root cause: each filter collected its own input, and a new carrier was added to some only.
*Avoid:* the instant filters read `filter_text` plus `app_text` (embed text and URLs, link buttons and text blocks of webhook and app posts, skipping Discord's previews of links already in the content; a poster's embed is always `rich`, so never taken for a preview); heat still counts only `filter_text`, so a feed's long embed is no spam. Images come from `automod::attachments` (own plus forwarded). Forwards add their `rich` embeds and components (a forward does not say who wrote the original). Files can be new when `automod::files_can_be_new`: a member's post, or any webhook or app message (a deferred app reply too), since those can add files in an edit. A setting that exempts a source from automod (Monitor Webhooks) also stops the image scan, its row and its hit log. Names go through `filters::word_forms` like messages. A thread rename deletes the thread only when the audit log shows its owner renamed it to that name within the last minute (`activity::owner_renamed`); otherwise it is only logged: a `ThreadUpdate` names no actor and also comes for a lock, tags or pins. An edit of a webhook or app message reads only files not seen before (`automod::Seen`, one per check, keyed by (message, attachment) because a forward keeps the attachment ids). A file is marked only once its scan row is saved or its QR image decoded, so a dropped or stopped job is read again. Every webhook or app update (not Brim's own) goes through `on_edit` when its filtered text (content plus `app_text`) or its file list is new for that message (`automod::new_text`, a bounded map keyed by a per-process random hash, since a fixed-key hash can be collided to pass a bad edit), so an image-only fill gets its scan and QR read; otherwise only the preview image scan runs; a LOADING mark misses button updates and races the create. A server-installed app's reply stays exempt from punishment, but its image hit is logged under `interaction_metadata.user`, and the raider hold checks that user too. A message caused by a button click (`automod::clicked`, user- or server-installed app) punishes nobody: the clicker did not write it, so the filters only delete and log it, and the raider hold and the NSFW credit skip it. The NSFW "has new files" check counts only files the scan would queue (`nsfw::scannable`). A flagged rename is logged once per (thread, name).

**An exemption checked after the count, or not on every path.**
Native AutoMod scopes counted a whitelisted member's hits and checked the whitelist only when acting, so their hits filled a server scope for the next member. A bad emoji name in a reaction, and a bad thread name, timed out an automod-permit holder, while the same word in a message is exempt. *Avoid:* check the exemption before anything is counted or deleted, and use the same exemption on every path of one filter (`activity::words_exempt` = words or automod whitelist, as messages get). The mention lockdown counted whitelisted members' pings in full the same way (h4): it now counts only pings the mention, @everyone and automod whitelists do not cover.

**A failed step returned before the next protective one.**
A failed strike timeout returned before the strike-cap lockdown, and a failed case did the same. Native rule actions and reaction or thread heat timeouts failed with only a trace line. *Avoid:* as in "A failed log must not stop protection": keep the error, run the remaining protective steps, return it last, and report a failed action with `action_failed`. Every path that records a strike runs `automod::strike_cap_lock` (messages, reactions, threads, and a warn threshold's timeout). `filter_action` stops after a failed ban, but nothing protective follows there.

**A done marker written before the action.**
The NSFW scan row was saved as a hit before automod acted. A failed action left the row, and the unique key then made every later pass (preview update, edit, sweep) skip the message for good. *Avoid:* a marker that makes later passes skip work stands only once the work succeeded. Mark the errors that come after the punishment landed (`automod::Punished`; a saved strike counts as landed) and free it on every other error, so a brief DB error before the punishment is retried, while a failed case, log, lockdown or strike timeout after it never punishes twice. Free the whole unit a pass checks: the sweep skips a message with any row, so every row of the message goes, and its attachment ids are forgotten.

**An early return in front of an unrelated protection.**
Heat panic's raider hold sat after "automod off", so with automod off raiders posted freely. The catch channel's test mode returned "handled", so automod skipped every message there. A restore job with no `joined_at` and a `?` on the member lookup gave main roles to a rejoined unverified member, and retried for a member who left. *Avoid:* before an early return, check that everything below it belongs to the switch or case it tests. A delayed role add carries its membership and treats 404 as done (`http_err::member`), and skips a member whose roles are held for verification (`sticky::pending`). A job that gives roles back to a quarantined member adds them to the quarantine's `saved_roles` instead of dropping them, so the release returns them.

**An exemption that covered more than it was for.**
The automod permit (which Auto Setup gives staff) and a webhook whose bot owner is on the anti-nuke whitelist (PluralKit, Tupperbox) returned before every filter, so a hacked mod or a relayed post could send a scam link, an NSFW link or image, or an invite untouched. The catch channel let any permit holder write. Root cause: an exemption meant for spam heat sat in front of the whole punishment path.
*Avoid:* say what an exemption is for and cut there, and check which levels a helper covers: `App::whitelisted` is true for Owner, Extra Owners and Trusted Admins too, and the first fix punished them; they stay exempt (`Level::immune`). A custom permit keeps only the scam, malicious and NSFW parts (`automod::UNEXEMPT`), acted on whatever their heat weight, and its exempt spam takes no punishment claim (the claim comes after the permit check); an exempt webhook still has any instant-filter hit (`INSTANT`) deleted and logged; the catch channel exempts only Owner, Extra Owners and Trusted Admins (`Level::immune`). Native rule hits and reaction/thread names keep the permit exemption (word and spam filters).

**A burst guard that also softened a different punishment.**
The 10 s grace claim after a heat punishment turned a scam or NSFW hit whose filter says Ban into a plain delete, and the crowd's similarity part tied with the scam part and won the "biggest part" pick (`max_by` keeps the last of equal values). Root cause: a guard against punishing one burst twice was applied to every punishment, and a tie went to whatever was pushed last. Letting instant hits skip the window multiplied a scam burst into one warn or ban per message; a per-message "done" marker checked before acting and set after let two passes over one message both punish, and blocked a Ban after a strike on the same message.
*Avoid:* rank punishments (delete < warn < timeout/strike < kick < ban) and, within the window per member (`HeatState::claim_rank`) and per (message, author) (`automod::ACTED`), act only on a stronger one; claim before acting, under the lock, and give the claim back on an error that is not `Punished`, only while the stored claim is still this pass's own (compare and restore), so a stronger claim another pass made meanwhile stays. A tie in `top_part` goes to the first part, and `heat` adds every instant part (blacklisted words too) before the spam parts.

**Handing a filter to Discord dropped what did not fit.**
"Use Discord AutoMod for words" switched the local word filter off for messages, while the synced rule silently left out words over 60 characters, words past 1000 and regex past 10 or 260 characters, and a failed sync was only a trace line. Skipping only the words Discord held also fails open: Discord's rule never sees webhook, app, forward or poll text, exempts admins, can be edited or deleted in Discord, and an in-memory "synced" flag does not survive a restart. *Avoid:* a message that reached the bot was not blocked by Discord, so the local filter always reads every word (with lookalikes); the Discord rule is extra, never a reason to read less. The sync still runs on each save, alerts the automod log when it fails, and syncs the rule back when someone changes or deletes it in Discord (once a minute at most). Thread and reaction names use the same local list, so "Migrate words" only turns the sync on and keeps the lists. Same root cause for reactions and threads: with the auto timeouts off, `activity::heat_up` skipped the strike and the strike-cap lockdown that messages still record.

**Two settings that together meant "do less".**
A filter action Timeout without its own duration hands the length to the strikes; with the strikes' auto timeouts off that became a delete only. *Avoid:* when a setting defers to another system, check what happens when that system is off. `automod::own_action` uses the strike timeout length then.

**Unrolling a short link visited the attacker's server.**
The shortener lookup followed redirects, so the target's server saw the bot's address (not behind Cloudflare), could answer the bot differently from members, and could hold the check for up to 12 s. It also ran only with invites or crawl on, while bad sites and NSFW links read its result. *Avoid:* a HEAD to a known shortener host with redirects off, read `Location` only, and ask again only while it points at another shortener (3 hops at most), within 12 s for the whole message; the final host is never contacted (`Sites::unshorten`). A client that does not build means no unrolling, never a default client that follows redirects. Run it for every filter that reads the target host.

**One shared queue let one server starve the others.**
The NSFW scan's lanes were FIFO across all servers, and a full backlog dropped the oldest job of any server. *Avoid:* a queue shared by servers takes turns per server (`Scanner::take` serves the server served longest ago) and drops or moves from the server with the most jobs (`most_jobs`, for a full backlog and a full live lane alike). The per-member cap (4 live images) was already in.

**A DM into the server skipped the server's protections.**
Ticket DMs are relayed into a staff channel but were never read by automod, and new tickets opened during a raid or lockdown and without limit, so a raid could flood staff with scam links and slurs through the bot. Root cause: the filters hang off guild `MessageCreate`, and a DM has no guild.
*Avoid:* text that the bot carries into a server goes through that server's filters. `tickets::dm::filtered` runs the word (local list; Discord's AutoMod never sees a DM), scam, phishing and invite filters before a ticket opens or a message is relayed; new tickets wait during a raid or lock (`lockdown::raid_or_lock`, shared with auto-dehoist) and are capped at 3 per member and 20 per server per hour. A quarantined member opens a ticket only when given no reason (automatic quarantines, or staff with an empty reason), since tickets are then the only way they learn why; a staff quarantine with a reason gets a short refusal (`tickets::dm::quarantined_with_reason`, counting only cases since the current quarantine row began, so an old lifted case does not block): the staff intro says "Quarantined", and a quarantine DM without a reason says they can DM Brim while tickets are on (`reply::reach_staff`). Automatic quarantines (anti-nuke, rate limits, the Raider List, a rejoin) queue a `quarantine_dm` job (`reply::queue_quarantine_dm`), so the DM never holds back the containment or the revert, and queue none during a join or voice raid, for a bot, or for a join quarantine. The DM is also skipped during a lock or raid and is sent at most once per member per 24 h (a `quarantine_dm_cooldown` marker job), so a re-quarantine loop cannot spam a member. Not covered: shortener lookups and attachments.

**A raid outcome was assumed, not checked.**
`joingate::on_join` skipped the Join Gate and verification once the raid's action *would* remove the joiner, so a raid ban that Discord refused left the joiner in with no quarantine, and a raid set to Kick cancelled a Join Gate Ban, so the raider could rejoin at once. *Avoid:* decide on what a punishment did, not what it was meant to do: `punish_or_retry` returns whether the member was removed, and the gate still bans after a raid kick.

**A judgement on role permissions read the live roles during a role lock.**
A role lock strips permissions from roles, so a saved Administrator role looked harmless and `t!verify` could free it. *Avoid:* judge a role by its unlocked permissions (`lockdown::unlocked_role_perms`, the oldest lock snapshot wins over the live role). The rejoin grace for an autorole bot covers any number of bot changes in its 60 s, but only roles with no dangerous permission and no permit, read live (a failed lookup or a role Discord does not list counts as not plain; the cached guild info missed a role created a moment before); anything else quarantines the bot as before. A single-use grace brought back the false positive for a second autorole or level bot.

**A startup catch-up read one member per row.**
`requarantine_missed` did a member GET for each `quarantines` row at every GuildCreate, in the bucket that kicks and role edits share, and rows from members who left pile up. *Avoid:* read members in pages (`GET /guilds/{g}/members?limit=1000`, its own bucket), and put the check off while a raid or lock is on, in one `requarantine_missed` job per guild (`jobs::replace`; `enqueue` started a chain per GuildCreate) and for 30 minutes at most, then run anyway (a GuildCreate takes the first wait from the pending job, so a re-identify does not restart the 30 minutes; `lockdown::Deferred`). The job is in `jobs::HEAVY`: it pages members and would hold back unbans and raid retries in the serial light worker. A failed run queues the job too, and runs take a per-guild lock, so a restart's job next to GuildCreate never quarantines or logs one rejoin twice (the second run sees the first's action_log rows). Compare Discord's join time with a DB time only with a 30 s margin, since the two clocks differ.

**Another bot's role was judged per call, and only by its id.**
A bot giving two verification roles in two calls made each look like proof the member was verified already, and a bot role with View Channel on the verified-only channels skipped verification just the same. *Avoid:* note a bot's grants, wait 1.5 s, and judge one member at a time (`verify::given`); take back any added role that opens what a verification role opens and @everyone lacks (View Channel, or Send Messages where @everyone only reads, by base permission or overwrite) or holds Administrator. Check base permissions too, not only overwrites: the usual setup (@everyone without View at server level, the verified role with it) let any "Member" autorole verify. A bot's member overwrite opening such a channel is removed, and GuildCreate reads the audit log for grants made while Brim was down (`given::catch_up`). Every role change used to cost a user and a member read in the member bucket: a per-guild candidate set (60 s), a cached bot flag and a remembered verified member now come first. A cache that skips a security check must be cleared by the events that change its answer, and must not be fed by the actor it guards against. The candidate set is dropped in `invalidate_guild` and on role and channel audit events; a member counts as verified only from a verification role no bot gave just now (`on_overwrite` noted any role, so a bot giving the role and an overwrite together made its later grants skip for 10 minutes), and an un-verify forgets them. `catch_up` reads removals too (Brim's own take-back costs no member read), skips a member that fails, waits out a raid or lock and retries a failed audit read in a heavy job (`verify_catch_up`), and logs when it hits its 500-entry cap. A human was exempt for being human, so Discord's Manage Roles alone could verify anyone; exemptions come only from permits and Owner/EO/TA, so a verification role given by a human without a verify or quarantine permit is taken back too, logged, nobody punished, live and in `catch_up` for grants made while Brim was down. A cache filled by slow reads must not outlive an invalidation that landed during the reads (a per-guild generation in `GATES`; a gate read across a forget is not stored); Brim's own lock edits only shorten the gate to 3 s and its own slowmode keeps it, so a lock over every channel does not rebuild it per joiner, while another actor's edit still drops it at once. A permit-less human cannot give an unverified member a channel-opening role either, and a failed permit lookup takes the role back (fail closed, staff told). `catch_up` retries a run that skipped an actor or member, takes a per-guild lock, runs beside the heavy worker (spawned, like `requarantine_missed`, so neither holds back `panic_start` or `nuke_revert`), and tells staff about its 500-entry cap only after a downtime it may have cut (`bot_seen`, written every minute). A gateway event names no actor, so softening every one let an attacker's edit live 3 s on the old gate; `invalidate_guild` forgets again; only a gateway event about a role or channel Brim marked just before editing it softens (`given::mark_own(gid, id)` in lock, unlock and panic; scoped to the object since a guild-wide mark let anyone open the window by triggering auto slowmode, which needs no mark), and the audit arm drops the gate before anti-nuke runs. A read across a soften is stored with the short life. During a channel lock the gate judges the lock snapshot's saved overwrites (`lockdown::unlocked_channels`), so the Send gate holds while the lock lasts; panic's saved @everyone overwrites are put back the same way (`panic::unexempted`). A self-retrying job counts its failed tries in the payload and gives up after 5 with a staff log; a spawned job's row is finished only on Ok, so the lease and attempt limit apply to errors. A failed lookup or removal is tried once more, then staff hear of it. The hold steps aside only where it acts (a join quarantine, or Quarantine Hold on); a Quarantine Whitelist actor is judged here. Not covered: a channel opened only by two roles together, a member overwrite given while Brim was down, grants older than the newest 500 role changes.

**Renaming the bot would have made a second Discord AutoMod rule.**
The bot finds its own keyword rule in Discord by its name ("TommyBot keywords"). With only the name changed, the next sync finds no rule, makes a new one in every server, and the old rule keeps blocking the old word list. *Avoid:* anything the bot finds by a name it gave (Discord AutoMod rules, channels, roles, webhooks) also matches the old name after a rename (`discord_api::is_keyword_rule`), and the sync writes the new name. Prefer a stored id over a name. Old exported files keep their internal ids (message kit `tommybot-messages`) so they still import.
## 4. Parsing and limits

**The message cache grew with the number of servers.**
No total cap, no count of map and queue memory, and text kept for servers with no message log (300 servers: about 5 GB). *Avoid:* an in-memory store per server also has a total cap (`cache::MAX_TOTAL_BYTES`, 512 MB; over it the biggest server loses its oldest first), counts per-entry overhead (`OVERHEAD`), and holds data only where a feature reads it (`Logger::handle` caches only while a message edit or delete log is set; an edit while it is off drops the cached copy, which would be stale later).

**Parsed words lost their typed form or their limit.**
`note remove 12345m` was read as a duration and `012345` as 12345, so those note ids could not be removed. `sanitize` could cure a 32-character name to more than 32 (㎏ -> kg), which Discord refuses. `COMMAND -h` sent a help embed with no cooldown for members without a permit. *Avoid:* an id that is free text takes the raw word (`modcmds::word_after`); cut a cured name to 32 characters after curing; every reply path for members without a permit passes `cooled_down`.

**A member without a permit could make the bot search for and answer hundreds of names.**
`commands::run` looked up each name and replied per miss before any permit check, and automod waits for that loop. *Avoid:* check the invoker's permit perm before any lookup, look up at most `MAX_NAMES` (10) names, give the missed names one reply.

**MCP `logs_tail` filtered before it masked, and read whole files.**
`contains` matched the raw line and `redact` ran after, so `contains: "postgres://user:a"` revealed a masked password one character at a time. Each hourly file was read whole. *Avoid:* mask first, then filter (`tools::tail`); read at most the last 64 MB of a file (`read_end`) and say in the answer that older lines were not read. That notice is a line too, so the stop test is `>= n` plus a truncate.

**A new range clamped stored values the old code allowed, and the dashboard named the wrong used value.**
The first webhook multiplier range was 1-10, so a stored 0.5 (allowed by `heat_ok`) read as 1.0. `verification.time_limit_ms` and `filters.nsfw_threshold` are clamped on read but were not in `limits::CLAMPED_ON_READ`, so `stored_outside` showed a stored 5000 without "(used as 10000)". *Avoid:* a range's lower end is not above the lowest value the old validation took; a path clamped on read goes into `CLAMPED_ON_READ`.

**A stored prefix with a space fell back to `t!`.**
Reading any prefix that failed `prefix_ok` as `t!` broke a stored `"tb "`, which works (the bot strips it and splits the rest by whitespace); the API also refused it on every save of the section. *Avoid:* only a blank stored prefix reads as `t!`; `api::validate` checks the prefix only when it changed.

**Numbers that switched their feature off at 0 had no range, and one range was never checked.**
Verification time limit 0 kicked every joiner; strike expiry or reoffend window 0 reset every strike to 1; `auto_lockdown.strikes` 0 locked the server on anyone's first strike; `panic.duration_secs` above `i64::MAX` wrapped and ended panic at once; `warn_base_points` 0 made warns add nothing; a huge webhook multiplier tripped every webhook message. Import, MCP and the API took them, since `validate` only parses the type. `filters.nsfw_threshold` had a range only in `for_path`'s match arm, which the form shows but `check` never walks. *Avoid:* a number at a fixed path goes into `limits::FIXED` (save, import, MCP, prefix panel and `stored_outside` all use it), a float into `float_for_path` and `ranged_paths`; a value an old config can hold outside the range is also clamped on read (`VerifyCfg::time_limit_ms`, `HeatCfg::clamped`). `stored_outside` lists every section that has a range.

**An empty prefix made every message a command.**
The dashboard API, import and MCP saved `""` or `" !"`; with `""`, `strip_prefix` matched every message. *Avoid:* `sections::prefix_ok` (1-32 characters, no whitespace) in `api::validate` and the panel; `MiscCfg` reads a blank stored prefix as `t!`.

**The Join Gate spammer card took the Rename action.**
`spammer` had no `no_rename`, and the test loop listed six filters, not seven. *Avoid:* every account filter except the username filter has `#[serde(deserialize_with = "no_rename")]`, and the test lists every filter.

**The backups section had no range check.**
`max_images` had no upper bound (automatic backups have no other cleanup), and an `interval_hours` above `i32::MAX` wrapped negative, so a backup ran every tick. *Avoid:* `limits::FIXED` has `backups.max_images` (1-50) and `backups.interval_hours` (1-168), and `due` converts with `try_from` instead of `as i32`. A number a section saves gets a `FIXED` range.

**Ticket snippets and the blacklist had no count cap.**
Holders of the weak Manage Tickets permit could add entries without end, and each add writes a full config version. *Avoid:* one rule in core (`sections::tickets_cap_problem`, `MAX_SNIPPETS` 100, `MAX_BLOCKED` 1000), run by the bot's ticket commands on the locked config inside `settings::save` (two adds at once cannot both pass) and by the dashboard's `validate` (save, import, rollback). Refuse only a new entry: lists stored above the cap keep working and can be edited or shortened. The dashboard shows "N of cap" from the schema (`cap`), not a UI copy.

**A huge duration wrapped negative.**
`duration::parse` took any i64, so `/ban duration:106751991167300d` made `now + d` wrap and the ban ended at once. *Avoid:* the shared parser caps every duration at a year (`duration::MAX_SECS`, the filter action duration's cap; the 28-day timeout cap would refuse the renewed long timeouts), and `too_long` lets each caller say so (`duration::TOO_LONG`) instead of reading the word as a name. A lock keeps its own 30-day cap. Refusals also name the missing permit ("you lack the lockdown permit"), and the ticket blacklist refuses role and channel mentions (`user_ids`).

**A saved message could hold control characters.**
`embeds::limits` checked lengths and shapes, not characters, so U+0001 (the preview's placeholder marker) could be saved through the API or MCP and forge preview HTML. *Avoid:* `limits` refuses any C0 control character or DEL other than `\n`, `\r` and `\t` in every text of the message and names the path (`embeds/1/author/name`).

**A member's text closed the log's code block or spoiler.**
`diff` broke a fence with `replace("```", ...)`, which leaves a real fence in a run of 4, 5 or 7 backticks, so a message could close its diff block and forge lines (a fake "Responsible") in the staff log. The report logs had the same bug with `replace("||", ...)`. *Avoid:* break every character of the delimiter (`` ` `` and `|`), not the delimiter string; the test tries runs of 3 to 8 backticks.

**A ticket member could write a staff line into the transcript.**
`history` put message text into `[time] Name (Role): text` lines without touching newlines, so a DM holding `\n[2026-11-03 09:10:00] Mod (Staff): ...` became a staff line in the transcript and on the dashboard page. *Avoid:* `tickets::staff::line` indents every continuation line, and the dashboard parses only lines that start with `[`.

**A log embed could pass 6000 characters, and a change to a new permission showed an empty "Changes".**
`Log::build` did not count the author line toward Discord's 6000 total, so the log was refused and lost. `perm_diff` used `from_bits_truncate`, so a change to only unknown bits (bit 48) gave no names. *Avoid:* every text part of an embed counts toward the total; `perm_names` names unknown bits as `Bit N`.

**A lock could run for years.**
No upper bound on lock duration (`999999d`). *Avoid:* `lock_marked` caps the duration at 30 days (`MAX_LOCK_SECS`).

**Id 0 parsed as a target.**
The prefix parser and `info` accepted `0` and `<@0>`, which reached Discord as a request for a user that cannot exist. *Avoid:* both parsers drop id 0. A test covers users, roles and channels.

**`info` showed a member's moderation details to everyone.**
`user_info` printed cases, notes and flags for any caller. *Avoid:* for a caller with no permit asking about someone else, show only account age, "In Server" and the join date.

**A warn whose auto action failed said nothing.**
`apply_dm` dropped the error from `apply_warn_actions`, so the reply said the warn worked while the timeout or kick never happened. *Avoid:* log the error and add "Warn saved, but the auto action failed" to the reply.

**`/warn` gave 1 point, the chat command the server's base points.**
The slash parser filled a missing `points` with 1, while `t!warn`, confirmations, reports and automod use `misc.warn_base_points` (default 5). *Avoid:* an optional value that has a server setting stays `None` until the call site reads the setting.

**Byte slicing panics on non-ASCII.**
Duration parsing sliced off the last character by byte and panicked on `5å`; converting large values to milliseconds overflowed. *Avoid:* split on `char` boundaries (`chars`, `char_indices`), use checked or saturating maths. Never `&s[..n]` on user input.

**`Id::new` panics on 0.**
Join Raid read its Warned Roles (strings in the config) with `r.parse.ok.map(Id::new)`, so a stored `"0"` (import or hand edit) panicked the join task before the raid log and punishments. A parsed number is not a valid snowflake. *Avoid:* turn parsed or stored numbers into ids with `Id::new_checked` and skip `None`. Both raid triggers use `joingate::warned_roles`; `verify::guild_of` (guild from a button's custom id) also uses `new_checked`. Other `Id::new` calls take ids Discord or the database already gave.

**Limits drifted between places.**
The prefix panels, the dashboard and the save path each had their own bounds, and an out-of-range value was changed silently. *Avoid:* all bounds live in `core::limits` and are used everywhere. Refuse a value out of range with a message.

**Heat max and decay had no range outside the dashboard.**
Only the dashboard's `heat_ok` checked them, and it let decay 0 through. The `heat` prefix panel took any number (`4 1000` saved a decay of 10, so `(1 - 10)^t` made every heat NaN; `3 0` made every message trip), and the bot used the values as read. Decay 0 meant heat never cooled, so an auto slowmode channel was never calm. Panic mode's `heat_decay` had no range either. *Avoid:* `HEAT_MAX` and `HEAT_DECAY` live in `automod::heat` and go through `core::limits::float_for_path` (dashboard, import and MCP saves, the prefix panel's `Kind::Num` rows), and the bot clamps when it reads (`HeatCfg::clamped` in `AutomodCfg`'s deserializer, the panic decay in `add_heat`). A number the bot divides by, raises to a power or waits on gets a range in `limits` and a clamp on read.

**A URL checked by prefix lets look-alike hosts through.**
MCP OAuth registration took any `https://` redirect and checked localhost with `starts_with("http://localhost")`, which `http://localhost.evil.com` passes. A stranger could register their site, send the owner a login link and get a token for everything the MCP reads. *Avoid:* parse the URL (`reqwest::Url`) and compare scheme and host exactly against an allow list. OAuth redirects go only to the exact callbacks in `MCP_OAUTH_REDIRECTS` or the owner's own computer.

**A refresh token that issues a fresh one with a full lifetime never expires.**
Each MCP refresh gave a new 30-day refresh token, so a stolen one could be renewed forever (only rotating `MCP_OAUTH_SECRET` stopped it). *Avoid:* carry the login time (`auth`) through every refresh and cap the new token at login time plus the limit.

**A zero quietly switched a protection off.**
Strike cap, multiplier, strike durations, mention lock count and window, the mention hour cap (0 stays allowed: any mention trips), panic window and heat trigger, Join Raid count, span and ID matches, ban purge days and warn points had ranges only in the prefix panels, so the dashboard, import and MCP could save `panic.window_secs = 0` or `mention_window_ms = 0` and the feature never fired. *Avoid:* the ranges live in `core::limits` (`FIXED`, `for_path`), the prefix panels use the same constants, and rollback runs the same `validate`, `limits::check` and `web_mode_error` as a save. `panic.executors` 0 stays "off" on purpose (its dashboard field says "(0 = off)"). Zeros stored before the limits stay as they are (no clamp on read); Overview's security score lists them (`limits::stored_outside`, row "Settings outside their range") so the owner sees which protection is off.

**The dashboard validated a section with a smaller type than the bot reads.**
`verification` was checked as `VerifyCfg`, but the bot parses `VerificationCfg` (also `channel`, `roles`), so a bad role id was saved and the bot read the whole section as default (off). *Avoid:* a section type both sides parse lives in `brim_core::sections` and the dashboard validates with that exact type.

**A range check refused a whole rollback.**
Rollback ran the save's `limits::check`, so one old value outside today's range (`joinraid.count` 800) blocked the rollback a cleanup after a hacked admin needs. *Avoid:* a rollback calls `limits::clamp` first (moves a number to the nearest end of its range, returns `section.path: old -> new`); the rollback logs and returns those (`clamped`, shown in the toast). Saves and imports still refuse, so a user typing a value learns the range. A non-number is still refused.

**A lower limit makes old values look broken.**
Auto slowmode's maximum dropped from 6 hours to 2 minutes. A server with an older 21600 got the Overview warning "Settings outside their range" (which says 0 switches the protection off), although the bot reads that card clamped and the value works at 120. *Avoid:* `limits::stored_outside` marks values the bot clamps on read (`CLAMPED_ON_READ`) with `(used as N)`. When a card gets a `clamped` on read, add its prefix there; when a range shrinks, check what Overview and the form say about older values.

**Image decoding had a per-message limit but no global one.**
The QR check read up to 3 images per message on blocking threads with no cap across messages, so a flood could run hundreds of 128 MB decodes at once. *Avoid:* a global semaphore (`qr::DECODE`, 2 slots) taken after the download (a slow host holds up no other server) and moved into the blocking task. A flood in one server can still slow QR checks in others; that is the heat system's job, and skipping the check when busy would be a bypass. A deleted message must not keep its place in the queue: MessageDelete, bulk deletes and channel or thread deletes mark it (`qr::deleted`, before any await in `events::run`), and no download or decode starts for it; a decode already running finishes and its codes still count, so a scam deleted at once is still acted on. The profile image decode already has a limit; the NSFW scan runs in a fixed set of workers.

**A full process-wide map was cleared whole.**
Heat, ping windows, reaction-spam windows, scam-hit counts, live captchas, the spammer-flag cache, trigger cooldowns, anti-nuke heat, fight and take-back counts, and the "already acted on this message" sets were cleared whole past their cap, so a flood in one server wiped every other server's detection state (heat restarted, live captchas went invalid, a message could be punished twice). *Avoid:* past the cap, after dropping idle entries, `caps::drop_biggest` drops only the server (or rule) holding the most; maps keyed by message id drop their older half (`caps::newer_half`; by an id that is a snowflake, never a 0 placeholder). A restart counts the size this hour's log file already has. Caches that only cost a lookup (levels, thread parents) may still clear whole.

**A feed item without an id got a new random id at every read.**
The feed parser makes a random UUID for an RSS item with no `<guid>`, so the seen list never matched and the item was posted at every check. *Avoid:* `feed::parse` sets its own id (the item's first link, else its title); a test checks that an item with no guid has its link as its id.

**A fetch of an address staff typed is a request into the internal network.**
A plain client would fetch `https://169.254.169.254/` or a name that resolves to 10.x. *Avoid:* `feed::client` resolves through `PublicOnly` (each DNS answer checked by `is_public`), has no proxy (a proxy resolves the name itself, past that check), allows https on port 443 only, no IP literals and no user name in the address, checks every redirect hop with the same rules (`feed::safe`, at most 3), and stops at 10 s and 2 MB. Errors name the cause in plain words, never the inner address.

**A feed poller lost, doubled and stuck items.**
The poller saved the ETag before it handled the feeds, so a failed post was hidden by the next 304; a database write after Discord took the post failed the post, so the item went out again; a 400 from Discord broke the feed and the same item failed at every Check now; Check now on a paused feed posted; a good check never turned a broken feed on; a click during a check was wiped. *Avoid:* save conditional-request headers only when every feed on that address finished; bookkeeping after an outside side effect (a post, a DM) never fails that action; remember (skip) an item Discord refuses with 400 and still break the feed; a paused feed only does a test post; clear a request flag only if it is still the value the run answered. `is_public` also refuses `::/96`, site-local `fec0::/10`, `64:ff9b:1::/48` and Teredo `2001::/32`. Automod must not trust every message from a Brim webhook (anyone with Manage Webhooks can read its token and post past automod): it skips only the message ids Brim got back from its own sends (`feeds::sent_by_us`, which waits up to 5 s while a send in that channel is under way) and checks every other post and every edit.

**Hourly log files had no size cap.**
A storm of errors could grow one hour's file to gigabytes on the volume Postgres shares. *Avoid:* `log::Capped` stops each hourly file at 100 MB with one notice line (stdout, capped by Docker, still has every line); 24 files keep the total bounded.

**An entry's link fields were never filled, and an empty one made Discord refuse the message.**
`botmsg::render` fills text only, never `url` or `icon_url` (so member text cannot become a link), so a feed entry with `{image}` as its picture or `{link}` as its title link sent the literal placeholder; a template link whose variable was empty (a GitHub event with no page) sent `"url": ""`, which Discord refuses for the whole message (the feed then stops). *Avoid:* feed entries fill their link fields with the feed's link variables only (`fill_links`), and anything then not an http(s) link is emptied; `render` drops every empty embed, author, footer, image and thumbnail link for all entries.
## 5. Database and migrations

- **One flag held "staff switched it off" and "waits for its open time".** The open job set `forms.open` on again at `opens_at`, also for a form staff had switched off later. *Avoid:* a job that turns a thing on checks that a person did not turn it off; `forms::update` drops `opens_at` when the form is saved off.
- **An "on" default for a switch that acts on members would start removing roles unseen.** Config sections use `#[serde(default)]` with the struct's `Default`, so a new default reaches every saved config that lacks the key at the next bot start, and every server with no saved config (`GuildCfg::default`). For `misc.enforce_permits` that takes dangerous roles from staff without a permit, also on a just-added server. *Avoid:* the bot default stays off; "on by default" lives where a person confirms: the wizard card presets it on (`Card::preset`), Overview shows a row with a Fix while it is not saved on, and every save that turns it on asks first with the live list from `/enforce-permits` (`form::ask_enforce`). That list uses the bot's rule (`antinuke::enforce_permits`): not a bot, no level in `permits::holders`, a role below Brim that is not managed and holds `DANGEROUS_PERMS` with a Role lock's saved bits added. Use a migration for a default (as for `always_dm`, 2300; the verification fail action, 4000) only where the change cannot take something from members.
- **A lookup by Discord message id had no index.** `message_sends` is read by `posted_id` on every press of a saved message's menu or button (`msgmenu`, the sticky cleanup), but only `embed_id`, `guild_id` and `webhook_id` were indexed. *Avoid:* a table read from an interaction gets an index on the id the interaction carries, in the migration that adds the read (4039 adds `message_sends_posted`).
- **A wrong "left" mark would have purged a server the bot is still in.** `on_ready` marks every server missing from READY as left, and the hourly purge deleted its data 30 days later without asking Discord. A sharded bot's READY does not list every server. *Avoid:* `welcome::purge_removed` asks Discord (GET guild) about every marked server each hour: an answer clears the mark, 404 or 403 with code 50001 purges once due, any other error (Cloudflare 403, 5xx) waits an hour (test: `a_wrongly_marked_server_is_not_purged`).

**A config read error skipped an audit entry's guards and count.**
`on_audit_as` used `app.cfg(..)?`, and the cache is empty after each save, `relink` and a listener reconnect. The entry was marked handled with no count and no `action_log` row. *Avoid:* `App::cfg_or_last` falls back to the last config loaded (kept through `forget`, set only by a read that is not stale), else after one retry returns Err with the defaults: the guards undo with nobody punished, the action is saved with `counted` 0 (`log_uncounted`) so `/revert` can undo it, and staff are told. The nuke run and its later passes use the same fallback.

**A restore into a new Postgres stopped on the MCP role.**
`pg_dump` writes the grants to `tommybot_mcp` but not the role or its `ALTER ROLE ... SET`, so `restore-test.sh` (`ON_ERROR_STOP=1`) failed, and a real restore lost the read-only setting; the migration that makes the role never runs again. *Avoid:* anything a migration makes outside the schema (roles, role settings) is made again by `db::set_mcp_password` on every start with `MCP_DB_PASSWORD` set (settings and connection limit too), and a restore creates the role first. restore-test.sh runs the exact DEPLOY.md restore (schema replaced in one transaction) and checks the role's grants.

**A ticket lookup ran for every message in any category.**
`ticket_here` queried `tickets` for every plain message in a channel with a parent, also on servers without panels. *Avoid:* return before the query when the channel is not in the ticket category and the server has no panels.

**Member lookups on scheduled_jobs scanned the table, and fake report ids collided.**
Every member update with "Remove Main Roles on TO" on ran a `payload->>'user'` DELETE with no index. Native AutoMod reports used seconds times 1000 plus a random byte as the message id, so blocks in one second hit the unique key and were lost. *Avoid:* migration 4006 indexes `(guild_id, kind, payload->>'user')`; a made-up key uses milliseconds plus 20 random bits.

**Hourly prunes and the anti-nuke seed scanned whole tables, and the first prune deleted months in one statement.**
The `action_log` and `mod_actions` prunes and `seed_windows` filter on `created_at` alone, but both indexes lead with `guild_id`. Unban and appeal checks filter `action_log` by guild and target, the dashboard bell `appeals` by user: no index either. *Avoid:* a query that filters on a column no index leads with gets one (migration 4009, plain `CREATE INDEX` because sqlx runs each migration in a transaction); a prune of a large table deletes in batches (`welcome::prune_batched`, 10,000 rows a statement).

**Rows pruned only on their next use outlived the promised time.**
`verify_ips` was pruned only when someone verified on the web and `verify_timeouts` only on the next write for the same server, so a quiet server kept user ids past the 30 days and 1 day PRIVACY.md states. *Avoid:* every retention PRIVACY.md promises has a global prune in `welcome::purge_left`'s hourly list.

**Stored rows outlived the one use they had, and a "salted" hash could be reversed.**
`verify_ips` was kept 30 days while the alt check reads only the last day, and its hash was SHA-256 of the OAuth client secret, the server and the IP: the secret sits in .env and in the MCP container, and all IPv4 addresses can be tried, so the addresses could be recovered. Every NSFW scan, clean or not, kept the member, channel, message and the model's scores. Moderation records (cases, notes, reports, appeals, tickets) and config history had no time limit.
