Root cause: retention was picked from the feature, not from what the reading query needs, and a pseudonym was keyed with a secret that has other uses. *Avoid:* keep a row only as long as the longest window its readers use, and store only the columns they need (a clean image needs its hash, not who posted it); key a hash that must not be reversed with a key no other service, file or dump holds (`verify::ip_hash` makes one at start, in memory); a record type with no natural end gets a per-server limit (`retention` section, `bot::retention`), and a sweep never deletes an open item, a running timed action, the current config or the row a number sequence is read from (`MAX(case_no)`, `MAX(number)`).

**A data delete kept a member's live warn and then cleared its points anyway.**
Owner rule: warns stay until they expire. `PLACES` keeps a warn that still counts, but `ANONYMIZE` then set `user_id = 0` on every case the delete left, and `warn_points` reads by `user_id`, so an approved `/mydata delete` wiped the warn points at once. Nothing removed the kept warn later. Root cause: "kept" was written for the delete but not for the step that strips the person from what is left, and a row kept "until it ends" had no one to end it. *Avoid:* every step of a person-level delete uses the same keep condition (`CASE_KEEP` and the same live test in `ANONYMIZE`); a row kept until it ends names what removes it: `persondata::sweep_kept` runs hourly from the scheduler and finishes done delete requests for rows made before the request, never later ones: an ended warn, a handled report or appeal, a closed ticket. It reads every done request, with no time window (a window of one warn life would leave a warn that a restored backup brings back); migration 4040 adds the indexes by person it needs. Tickets lose the person only when closed, and the "Not NSFW" rows are the server's.

**A first draft of the data delete removed the person's dashboard 2FA, open items and the newest case.**
With the authenticator row gone, whoever holds the Discord account could enrol a new one by DM code; deleting the newest case let its number be given to another member. Root cause: the delete list came from "every table with the user id", not from what each row is for. *Avoid:* a person-level delete (`core::persondata`) never deletes a credential, an open item (report, appeal, ticket), a running timed action or the row a number sequence is read from; those are kept or lose the person (`ANONYMIZE`). An export leaves out other people's ids and text that can name them (report reasons, which servers reported a raider).

**PRIVACY.md said staff see what their Discord permissions allow.**
Only permits give access to Brim data; Discord permissions give none. *Avoid:* the notice uses the same words as the code's rules ("permit", "Discord permission"), and is read again when access rules change.

**One server whose purge failed stopped the purge of every server after it.**
`db::purge_left` used `?` in its loop, so a lock timeout on one server kept the others' data. *Avoid:* a loop over independent items logs a failed one and goes on.

**Case and ticket numbers took the config lock.**
`cases::create` and `tickets::claim_number` locked `pg_advisory_xact_lock(guild_id)`, the same key as the config saves (`settings::locked`, dashboard `store.rs`), so they queued behind each other, and a numbering call inside a config transaction would deadlock. *Avoid:* the bare guild id is only the config lock; every other lock uses its own key, `hashtextextended('name:' || id, 0)`.

**Removed Raider List entries and their evidence were never deleted.**
`expire` deleted rejected and old pending entries but not `removed` ones, so evidence (reporter ids and notes) stayed for good, while PRIVACY.md said evidence goes with its entry. *Avoid:* every status has an end; a removed entry goes after 30 days once no server has a list action left to lift (the lift reads the row).

**PRIVACY.md promised retention the code does not do.**
The Message Builder row said an unused avatar goes "after 1 day", but `msghooks::gc` runs only when that server uploads or deletes; the Yes/No row said 10 seconds where `confirm.rs` deletes rows older than 70 seconds. *Avoid:* a retention line names the trigger and the age the DELETE uses (read the query, not the constant's name).

**PRIVACY.md missed tables and said message text is never stored.**
Reports (300 characters of the message), appeals, raid log, staff lists, sticky roles, lock updates, Message Builder, MCP and update records had no row; `verify_ips` (salted IP hashes, 30 days) had none; `guild_owners` said "deleted on leave" (it is the 30-day purge); optional third parties (Cloudflare, the AI connector, off-site dumps) were not named. *Avoid:* a new table with user ids, user text or anything about a user gets its row in PRIVACY.md (what, why, how long), and a new outside service its line under "Optional services", in the same change.

**A 5-second job scanned the whole cases table.**
`jobs::expire_cases` runs on every scheduler tick (5 s) with no index for `active AND expires_at < now`. *Avoid:* a job that runs often gets a partial index that matches its WHERE (migration 4004, `cases_expiring`).

**Tables with personal data had no cleanup.**
Expired `dash_session` rows (with the Discord access token) and every `action_log` row stayed forever, while PRIVACY.md promised sessions expire and did not list `action_log`. *Avoid:* a new table that stores user ids gets a retention line in PRIVACY.md and, if time-limited, a DELETE in the hourly list in `welcome::purge_left`. Sessions go when expired, `action_log` after 90 days (owner rule), except Brim's own `quarantine` rows of a live quarantine, which `joingate::missed` reads to spot rejoins. An action older than 90 days can no longer be undone from the dashboard.

**Two PRs used the same migration number.**
Git merges them without a conflict, then sqlx fails every test with "duplicate key _sqlx_migrations_pkey". Two merged PRs that each added `4049_*.sql` also stopped every start: a fresh database failed on the second insert into `_sqlx_migrations`, and a database that had one refused to start ("previously applied but has been modified"). *Avoid:* parallel work gets its own number range; the coordinator gives each builder its number; before merging several PRs and after rebasing on main, `ls crates/core/migrations` and look for duplicate prefixes. A test fails on two files with one number (`db::tests::migration_numbers_are_unique`).

**Old migrations are frozen.**
sqlx stores a checksum, so editing an applied migration breaks every existing database. *Avoid:* never edit an old migration, not even a comment. Write a new one.

**A column that replaces another goes stale after a rollback.**
The new `embeds.message` replaces `embed`, and the new code still writes `embed` so the previous image works after a rollback. But the previous image edits only `embed`, so after a roll forward the readers took the old `message` and the edit was lost. *Avoid:* a trigger clears the new column when an update changes only the old one (`embeds_stale_message`), and readers fall back to the old column (`embeds::MSG`). Drop the trigger with the old column.

**A default change must not overwrite user choices.**
*Avoid:* a migration that changes a default only moves rows that still hold the old default (600 → 60). Test it from an empty DB and from the old schema with data.

**A new "last activity" column backfilled from an old time makes a new idle rule act at once.**
Backfilling `last_activity_at` from `opened_at` would have let auto-close shut every old open ticket when a server first turned it on, though staff wrote in them yesterday. *Avoid:* when a column starts tracking something not tracked before, backfill live rows with `now` (unknown counts as fresh), and only finished rows with the old time.

**A new migration file was not picked up.**
`sqlx::migrate!` embeds the migrations at compile time, and adding a file does not make cargo rebuild `brim-core`; tests fail with "relation mcp_... does not exist". *Avoid:* after adding a migration, `touch crates/core/src/db.rs` before testing (CI builds clean). The same happens after `git merge` brings in a changed `RaidCfg`: touch `crates/automod/src/join.rs`. If a test fails right after a merge on code you did not touch, touch the changed crate's file and run it again before you debug.

**Parallel worktrees shared one target and one DB.**
Cargo mixed crates from different worktrees, one branch's migration broke the others' tests, and four targets (about 7 GB each) filled the disk. *Avoid:* one `CARGO_TARGET_DIR` and one database per worktree (`su postgres -c "createdb X"`; `createdb -h localhost` hangs on a password prompt). Delete finished targets.

**Saving a note without a change was stored as an edit.**
`notes::edit` always wrote a `note_edits` row and both callers logged a Modify, so Save twice gave a "x → x" history line and a log. *Avoid:* an edit that changes nothing writes nothing (`old.text <> $3` in the SQL) and the callers skip the log when `old == text`.

**A row was found by its id alone, without its server.**
Many statements read or changed a row only by a global id (ticket channel, appeal, report, sticky, message rule, webhook, slowmode channel, raid code, confirm nonce). Every id came from a server-scoped step, so nothing leaked, but one later change (an unchecked id from a button, job payload or event) would let one server touch another's row. Root cause: the server was checked once, earlier and often in another function. *Avoid:* a statement keyed by an id that arrives from outside the function (parameter, job payload, button id, event) also says `guild_id = $n`; pass the server down (event `guild_id`, the job's `guild_id`, the handler's path). Not needed when the same function just read, locked or inserted that row with its server, or for sweeps over all servers (retention, the job queue, the message-rule tick).

**Any database session can NOTIFY, also a read-only one.**
Postgres has no grant for NOTIFY, and `default_transaction_read_only` does not stop it, so the MCP role (or any other) could send `config_changed` and make the bot redo each guild's save work (rule sync, ticket category, Raider List scan). *Avoid:* the listener always drops the cached copy (cheap; a purge notice names no version), but runs the save work only for a version that is saved and newer than the last it handled (seeded at start). Compare version numbers, not `created_at` (the transaction's start, so a later version can carry an earlier time). Treat a notice as a hint to read the database, never as the change itself. A new role that must not write rows gets no INSERT grant; a channel it must not use needs a check on the listening side.

- **A person's data deletion rule that lived only in the code.** The proofs and edit history of a case the deletion keeps (the newest, a running timed action) were deleted by two statements in `persondata::ANONYMIZE`. An older image (after a rollback) runs its own list, which does not know the new tables, so the member's message copy stayed. *Avoid:* a table that hangs on another table's row gets its deletion rule in the migration (ON DELETE CASCADE, or a trigger such as `case_forget_person`), so every image obeys it.
- **The copy trigger for an old column only added.** The trigger that copies `cases.proof` written by an older image into `case_proofs` fired only for a new value, so a proof the older image cleared or replaced came back after the next update. *Avoid:* a trigger that mirrors an old column handles replace and clear as well as set, and has a test for each.
- **The dashboard's database role could write what only the bot may set.** The role had INSERT and UPDATE on every table. SQL run as the dashboard could set `case_proofs.verified` (the bot's own copy of a member's message), change `reports.content` (the bot copies it as verified proof when a moderator acts), point a case at another member (`cases.user_id`, `kind`), and rewrite history and log rows. *Avoid:* a table the bot writes and the dashboard only reads, marks or adds lines to is narrowed in `db::set_dashboard_role`: revoke table-level INSERT and UPDATE and grant only the columns the dashboard's routes change (`cases`: reason, active, proof; `reports`: status, handled_by, handled_at; `case_proofs`: INSERT of the staff columns), and write the staff statement with only those columns. Before narrowing, grep every dashboard and core statement on the table, row locks too: `SELECT ... FOR UPDATE` needs an UPDATE right on at least one column (the proof removal locked a `case_proofs` row and would have failed with "permission denied"). `dashboard_role_has_rows_only` connects as the role, checks each refusal by its error code (42501) and runs what the dashboard does do. The dashboard's unit tests and the browser test connect as the owner, so they do not see a missing right: run the browser test once with the dashboard connected as `tommybot_dashboard` after a grant change.

**Any role could switch the erasure log off for its own deletes.**
`note_erasure` and `log_erasure` skipped logging whenever the setting `brim.erasing` (before: `tommybot.erasing`) was `on`, and any session may set a custom setting, so the dashboard's role could `SET LOCAL brim.erasing = on` and delete Extra Owners, permits, a rescue key or 2FA without a log line; a later restore brought them back. Root cause: a setting says nothing about who set it. *Avoid:* a skip that only the owner may grant needs a proof only the owner can write. Migration 4056: `purge_guild` and `restore_erasures` call `brim_owner.erasing_on`, which writes the transaction id (and its start time, so a row restored from another database cannot match) into `brim_owner.erasing`, a schema no other role may use (not `public`: an older image grants every table there again); `brim_owner.erasing(guild)` checks flag AND row, and the row names the purged server (NULL only for a restore): the dashboard may call `purge_guild` itself, and the log triggers are deferred to commit, so a purge of an unused server id in the same transaction would otherwise hide its other deletes. Postgres 16 already refuses a non-superuser role to keep the flag as its own `ALTER ROLE ... SET` default, but a role can keep other defaults (the MCP role could turn its read-only default off): `db::set_dashboard_role` and `set_mcp_password` clear them (`RESET ALL`, also per database) at every start. No other function reads a session setting for a decision.

**Role names written into SQL broke with the rename.**
Migrations 4035 and 4043 compared `current_user` with the literal `'tommybot_dashboard'`, and every MCP grant named `tommybot_mcp`, so a server on the Brim names (`brim_dashboard`, `brim_mcp`) would have lost the dashboard's row limits and the MCP role all its views. Root cause: the role name was a constant in SQL, the code and the compose file at once. *Avoid:* the code uses the Brim names only, and `db::connect` stops at start on a login under the old names (before any migration or role change), the bot grants the MCP views at start (`set_mcp_password`), and SQL that must know the dashboard's role names both (migration 4056). A new check on the dashboard's role in SQL names both names.

**The dashboard role's limits were gone after a rollback.**
`db::set_dashboard_role` narrows the role at every bot start, but an older bot image runs its own list (`GRANT SELECT, INSERT, UPDATE, DELETE ON ALL TABLES`, no revokes), so a rollback gave the role every row right again. Root cause: a limit that only the newest code sets does not survive older code; grants are state that every image rewrites. *Avoid:* a limit that must hold on every image lives in a migration (an older image runs no down step): migration 4043 `dashboard_role_limit` row triggers refuse (42501) for `current_user = 'tommybot_dashboard'` what the column grants refuse. A trigger checks values, not the columns a statement names (the #357 image names every `case_proofs` column with `verified = false`). A grant that leaves a column out also refuses a chosen value for it: the staff proof's `id` and `added_at` must be the defaults (a chosen `id` would take the sequence's next values and block the bot's verified proofs). A trigger function that guards a role sets `search_path = pg_catalog, pg_temp`, or the caller puts its own `pg_temp` operators in front of the check. The same holds for history and log lines (`case_edits`, `note_edits`, `mod_actions`, `raider_evidence`): "no UPDATE" alone still let the role DELETE a line and INSERT it again with the same id and any text or time. Append-only means INSERT on the named columns only (no `id`, no time) and no DELETE; a case's or note's delete removes its lines through the foreign key, which runs as the owner. A new dashboard write to one of these tables needs its column in BOTH the grant and the trigger's argument list; `dashboard_role_has_rows_only` grants everything back as an older image does and runs the refusals again against real rows (a row trigger does not fire for `WHERE false`).

**A per-member path must hold for 100,000 members.**
A giveaway can get 100,000 entries, and a button press can come from 10,000 members in a minute. *Avoid:* a press is one small indexed write (unique on giveaway + user, so a double press is a no-op) and a private answer, never a message edit; the shown count is refreshed by a job at most once a minute (`giveaways::queue_refresh` also skips the database for 60 s per server). Never load all rows to pick from them: the database draws (`ORDER BY -ln(1 - random()) / weight LIMIT n`, a weighted sample without repeats), and only the picked candidates are checked with Discord, with a cap on the checks. A change of rules does not re-check every entry; the draw re-checks the winners. Every query on a per-press path needs an index on its `WHERE` (migration 4061 adds `raid_log (guild_id, user_id)`). The load test `a_giveaway_with_100000_entries` measures it.

**A saved draw was never announced after a crash.**
The end job saved the winners, then posted them and queued the DMs. A restart between the two left winners nobody was told about, and the retry saw the draw as done. *Avoid:* the save of a result queues its follow-up jobs (announcement, one DM per winner) in the same transaction (`giveaways::save_draw`). A DM job checks that the member is still a winner, and DM jobs run spawned (`scheduler::SPAWNED`), so 100 DMs do not hold back unbans. A cancel drops the end job only when it changed the row, so it never takes the retry from a running draw.
## 6. Dashboard

**One dashboard 2FA step-up unlocked the 2FA-gated actions in every server.**
`dash_session.step_up_until` had no server, so `require(.., true)` and the bot's `fresh_2fa` (whitelist save, adding a Trusted Admin, main roles, backup clear, delete and load) took a step-up done anywhere. *Avoid:* the step-up stores its server (`step_up_guild`, migration 4012). The prompt sends the server of the page it opened on. `Sess::stepped_up_for(guild)` and `fresh_2fa(app, user, gid)` accept it there only. `/api/me` returns `step_up_guild` for pages that check first (`stepped_up_here`). Account pages (profile, update, MCP tokens, Raider List review) keep `stepped_up`. An MCP confirm's code counts for the change's server. Test fixtures that set `step_up_until` also set `step_up_guild`.

**A Message Builder rule kept running after its saver lost the level to make it.**
A schedule, trigger or sticky message is saved by an Owner, Extra Owner or Trusted Admin, but the runner never asked again. *Avoid:* `msgrules::saver_may_run` reads the level of `updated_by` before every schedule run, trigger answer and sticky repost (`sticky_msgs` for stickies). Below Admin it turns the rule off with the reason in `issue` (shown on the card) and logs once (the update matches `enabled`, so a second run changes nothing). A save by someone with the level sets `updated_by` and `issue = null`. An unread level fails the run and pauses nothing. No migration.

**A Trusted Admin could hand a permit role with the anti-nuke or quarantine whitelist (or View Mod Stats) to an alt in Discord.**
In a server whose permits are not listed by member, `guard_permit_roles` let every immune actor through, while the dashboard's `keep_exemptions` kept those permits Owner/EO only. *Avoid:* the bot twin asks the actor's level. Owner and Extra Owner pass. A Trusted Admin's grant of such a role (`owner_only_roles`) is taken back and logged, nobody punished. This includes roles with dangerous permissions, which `guard_perms` spares for a Trusted Admin. A bot path is never weaker than its dashboard twin.

**"Is this held member staff" always read no for a quarantined member.**
`top_permit` skips anyone in `quarantines`, so `resolve` gave no permit. A permit holder could release, or give roles to, quarantined staff whose permit came only from roles. *Avoid:* `antinuke::held_staff` reads the saved roles' permits (or the member's own permit rows once migrated) and dangerous permissions. `unquarantine`, the quarantine hold and the Raider List's release on removal all use it. Below Extra Owner, the hold's `quarantine` flag and whitelist spare the actor only for a held member who is not staff.

**A Trusted Admin could give the quarantine role itself dangerous permissions or a channel override.**
This reached every quarantined member, staff too, around the staff release rule. *Avoid:* `guard_perms` asks for Owner or Extra Owner when the role (or role override) is the quarantine role. A Trusted Admin's change is undone and logged, never punished.

**An anti-nuke revert of a role removal gave back every removed role.**
`Inverse::AddRoles` gave even Administrator to a member quarantined since, and one deleted role stopped the rest. *Avoid:* the bot and the dashboard give back no role with dangerous permissions or a permit, and nothing to a quarantined member (left to staff, as the step text says). They try every role before they report the last error. The dashboard also reads the role lock snapshots, since a lock strips the bits that make a role dangerous.

**A schedule's claim moved `next_at` before the send, so an error after it lost a one-time schedule silently.**
*Avoid:* an unread saver level after the claim sets the rule's `issue` with the reason. Nothing is paused.

**Only the browser checked the typed server name before a delete.**
`purge` checked Owner level, 2FA and the physical owner, but a direct call skipped the name PRIVACY.md promises. *Avoid:* a confirmation that the notice or the UI names (a typed name, a code) is also checked on the server, after the access checks so a refused user still gets 403.

**The rescue authenticator secret sat in plain text next to the key's hash.**
A database read alone gave the second factor of a rescue claim. *Avoid:* a secret that only the key holder needs is sealed with the key (`ops::seal`, opened at claim time). The row keeps only the hash. Older rows stay plain until the owner makes a new key.

**Log lines served to the AI connector kept login codes, state and session ids.**
Request spans logged the full URI, so `/auth/callback?code=...&state=...` and member search text went into the files `logs_tail` reads. `redact` masked only tokens and URLs. *Avoid:* request spans log the path, never the query. A new secret kind that can reach a log line gets a pattern in `mcp::tools::SECRETS` and a case in `masks_secrets`.

**A rescue claim from outside the server used up the key for nothing.**
The claim made the claimer an Extra Owner, which `api::level_of` counts only for a member. Owner rule: a lost account must be recoverable from a new account that nobody let in, so there is no member rule, and the claim moves the Owner level itself (next entry). *Avoid:* a grant that only counts under a condition is checked against that condition before anything single-use is spent. Here the grant (bot owner) has no member condition, and `let_in` unbans the account and makes a one-use invite.

**Owner level came from Discord ownership alone, so a lost owner account could not be replaced.**
A rescue claim moves Brim's Owner level. `rescue_owners` (migration 4048) names the claimer and the Discord owner at claim time. `permits::bot_owner` gives the Owner level to the claimer. The Discord owner account is paused until the claimer gives it back (`rescue_give_back`). The claim moves with Discord ownership (`on_owner` updates `discord_owner`) and ends only on a give-back or when ownership lands on the claimer (a sale needs a give-back first). Ending it when Discord ownership moved was wrong: a thief holding the old account handed the server to an alt and had the bot back at once. *Avoid:* never decide trust from `GuildInfo::owner` or Discord's `owner_id` directly. Levels go through `permits::resolve`; `level_members`/`member_roles`/`unentitled_roles` map in SQL; the bot uses `App::bot_owner` (owner's DMs, owner skips); the dashboard uses `api::bot_owner`/`owner_state`. Discord facts (the owner cannot be acted on, has every permission: `target_above_bot`, `can_invite`, the rank in `hierarchy`) keep the Discord owner. `guild_owners` holds the bot owner, so a claim and a give-back reach `on_owner` (queued `owner_check`) as a handover: the previous holder's Extra Owners, Trusted Admins and strong permits are paused, with Keep on Overview. A server without Brim answers like a wrong key and is not counted; code tries count on the key row only. The column is `server_id`, so `purge_guild` keeps the row and cannot give the bot back. `on_owner` appends the previous lists when the bot goes back to the owner the last change paused (else a give-back right after a claim loses the paused owner's Extra Owners). `owner_check` inserts skip only a job that is not leased: a running one may have read the state before the claim. shortcut: the erasure log does not record a give-back, so a database restore from before it brings the claim back; add `rescue_owners` to migration 4035's lists (and `restore_erasures`) if that matters.

**A rescue code checked without using up the key could be replayed.**
The rescue claim needs no replay guard because a right code deletes the key. The Secure my account press checks the same key and code but keeps the key, so the same code worked again within its 30 s (and in a claim). *Avoid:* any second use of a secret that is not used up stores the last code step in the write itself (`rescue_key.last_step`, migration 4050, `where last_step is null or last_step < $step`). Every path that checks the code (claim and press) refuses a step at or below it and counts it as a wrong code on the shared `code_fails`. The press also refuses a key made before the server's last change of owner, even inside the 7 days a claim allows. The press has its own limit (`PRESSES`): counting presses in the claim's per-user limit let whoever held only the Discord account (a claim needs no step-up) send 6 claims every 10 minutes and keep the owner's button refused. A new Rescue Key clears `last_step`. The alert's link is checked (https, parses, 512 characters at most), because Discord refuses the whole DM for a bad button link and the bot does not retry a 4xx.

**The rescue key outlived an ownership change for good.**
`claim_key` never looked at `owner_handovers`, so a previous owner could claim their key and become Extra Owner in the new owner's server at any time. A fix keyed on the one `owner_handovers` row per guild was wrong: its `at` moves on every change, so a second change brought an expired key back or gave an old key 7 more days. *Avoid:* a key made before the last handover (`owner_handovers.at > rescue_key.created_at`) works for `RESCUE_GRACE_DAYS` (7) and is then refused (`permits::rescue_expired_sql`, built from the constant; one SQL for the claim, the prompt and the bot's job). `on_owner` deletes, in its transaction, a key made before the previous handover, so only the handing-over owner's key gets the 7 days, once, and sets `at = clock_timestamp` after the config lock. `rescue_regenerate` checks the owner and the 7 days and inserts under the same lock, and refuses inside the 7 days with the date. Then the physical owner gets a full-page prompt (`page_access` `rescue_prompt`, `RescuePrompt`; a new key refetches the access) and a DM from the `rescue_expired` job that `on_owner` queues. Test claim, prompt and regenerate inside and after the 7 days, a key made after the handover, and a second handover (bot `a_second_owner_change_...`). No migration.

**Dashboard twins of bot actions were weaker: appeal blacklist and pinging messages.**
The appeal blacklist add and remove routes needed only an Editor, with no `appeals` permit and no panic mod lock, while the bot's Deny + Blacklist button needs both. Send and Edit of a built message checked only the webhook case, so an Editor could send a Trusted Admin's @everyone message again and again. Appeal answers went into the staff log as raw markdown. *Avoid:* a route uses its bot twin's checks (`reviewer` and `mod_lock_guard`; the page access follows the route). Send and Edit refuse below Admin when the allowed mentions name anyone (`pings_members`: `parse`, `users` or `roles` not empty), the same bar as saving. User text in an embed goes through `msgvars::escape`.

**Error texts named the wrong cause, and two config log lines had no version.**
`users_only` told an owner to "try again" for an id Discord does not know (`Ok(None)`), a failed `/users/@me` at login showed raw JSON, and every 2FA setup DM failure said "allow DMs". The Discord AutoMod rule routes and the word migration committed a version but logged without it. A second tab turning Tickets on made a second category and log channel. *Avoid:* match each failure kind (404 user, Discord 403 on a DM, others) to its own text. A route that commits a version logs it (`audit_version`). A setup that makes Discord objects before its commit holds a try-lock (`tickets_setup:`) and reads the version again under it.

**The MCP tokens page said "No tokens yet." when the list did not load.**
The owner could decide there was nothing to revoke while live tokens existed. *Avoid:* a list keeps a `failed` signal from its load. The empty fallback shows "Could not load ..." with Retry when it is set.

**Dashboard mod actions skipped panic mode's mod command lock.**
Appeal deny, dismiss and deny + blacklist, case edits, note add, edit, delete and sweep, and report dismiss and reopen ran during the lock, while their bot twins go through `commands::gated`. Only the appeal accept checked it. *Avoid:* every dashboard route whose bot twin runs `gated` calls `appeals::mod_lock_guard` (Owner, Extra Owner and Trusted Admin pass). The command rate limit of `gated` is still not mirrored (no dashboard rate limits yet).

**Reopening or dismissing a report on the dashboard erased its action.**
`report_status` set `action = null` and cleared `handled_by` with no trace. *Avoid:* an `actioned` report keeps its action and handler (the route refuses it). A dismiss or reopen writes a `mod_actions` row (`report_dismiss`, `report_reopen`).

**"Delete server data" deleted what a lock, panic mode or quarantine gives back.**
`purge_guild` empties `lock_snapshots`, `quarantines.saved_roles`, the `panic_end` and revert jobs, and `guild_owners`, while the bot stays in the server. *Avoid:* `api::purge` refuses while any `lock_snapshots` row exists (also one that is unlocking), while `recovery::no_backup_now` is true, or while a staff or automatic quarantine (not `on_join`) exists. It writes the checked owner back into `guild_owners`, so the next transfer still runs the D47 reset.

**The MCP DM showed `"pings": null` for a save that keeps @everyone.**
`Save.pings` reads a JSON null as missing (keep the stored pings), but the preview filled in the stored value only for a missing key. *Avoid:* the preview treats a null like a missing key wherever the route does. Check how serde reads `null` for each `Option` field the DM shows.

**An MCP change stuck in `running` kept its body.**
A restart between the claim and the final update left the row `running` with its body, and expired bodies were cleared only on the next MCP call. *Avoid:* `mcp::service` and the bot's hourly prune (`welcome::purge_left`) close `running` rows older than 15 minutes and expire pending ones, both with `body = null`.

**A thread passed the built-message channel check.**
`embeds::bot_channel` compared a thread's parent only with the ticket category, so a schedule or sticky could post in a thread of a log, appeal, verification or ticket channel. *Avoid:* pass `embeds::parents` (the parent and, for a thread, its parent) and compare each with the bot's own channels and the ticket category.

**A schedule edit kept `waiting_once`.**
The dashboard save reset `due_messages` and `issue` but not `waiting_once`, so after a "once" run waited out a pause, the first run of the new schedule was skipped with a wrong reason. *Avoid:* a save resets every run-state column the bot's enable path resets.

**Login and captcha failures showed raw JSON or "internal error".**
A cancel on Discord's consent screen (no `code`), a second login tab (state mismatch) and a reused code answered with a deserializer message, JSON or a 500. A Turnstile outage showed "internal error". *Avoid:* `auth::callback` answers these with a short page and a login link (`login_failed`). `verify::turnstile_ok` maps a send or parse failure to "The captcha check could not be reached".

**Auto Setup deleted the server's own channels and roles that had a matching name.**
`drop_named` deleted every text channel named like a log channel (`#mod-logs`, `#reports`, `#appeals`, ...) or `#verify-here` that the config did not use, and `quarantine_role` deleted every unused role named Quarantine, before it made a new one. The code cannot tell a leftover of an earlier Brim setup from a channel or role the server made itself, and anti-nuke ignores the bot's own deletes. *Avoid:* a setup never deletes a channel or role it did not make in this run. It takes over the oldest unused one with that name (`oldest_unused`). A log channel is moved into its category and its overwrites are replaced in full by the category's current ones (only adding denies left an old allow or Manage Permissions reading the logs). `#verify-here` gets the overwrites a new one would. Their messages stay. A Quarantine role must pass the same `unfit` check as a picked one (not @everyone, not managed, below the bot). No member may hold it (`ops::held_roles`; a held one would lock its members out with no quarantine row, case or release). It is stripped of its permissions. Other same-named ones are left alone.

**Auto Setup lost a save made during its Discord calls.**
Auto Setup decided "no custom permits yet" from its first read and then replaced `permits.custom` with the two default permits, so permits saved during its 10 or more seconds of Discord calls were gone. A save in that time also made its final `commit` return 409 after the roles and channels were made, and the level roles were written with no config version. *Avoid:* decide from the read in the final transaction (`no_custom`). Write the level roles together with a `bump`, under the config lock and only for levels nobody changed since the first read (`new_levels`). Put the setup's changed fields onto the newest version under the config lock (`put_changes`) instead of refusing the whole run.

**Message Builder Send and Delete had weaker checks than Edit and Save.**
Send (`publish`) did not refuse Brim's log, ticket, verification and appeal channels, which edit, the schedule and trigger rules and the Builder's new channel refuse, so an Editor could post a fake log line as the bot. Delete needed only Editor, with no `emergency_gate`, so an Editor could delete a message that pings, sends as a webhook or runs by itself (which only a Trusted Admin may save), with its rules and every copy, also during a lockdown. *Avoid:* Send asks `bot_channel` like edit. Delete reads the row `for update`, calls `emergency_gate` and the same `trusted_only` check as save. Same class as "A second way to change something had a weaker check than the main way".

**A new automated send went out as "manual" and skipped every per-minute cap.**
`/tag` runs called `send_copy` with no rule and no answer, so `send_copy` filed them as `manual` (cap 0), and only the per-member limit held them back: ten permit holders could post 60 messages a minute. *Avoid:* every path that sends without a person at the dashboard has a server-wide cap. `/tag` checks it in the bot's queue insert (10 a minute per server), next to the per-member and per-channel limits.

**Lockdown Apply Fix applied fixes the user never saw.**
`fix` ran every automatic fix the check found at click time, so a role or channel change after the page loaded could add a role-permission change nobody reviewed. *Avoid:* the page sends the ids of the automatic fixes it showed (`shown`). `chosen` refuses with 409 when the check now finds a different set.

**Overview read the member list once per row.**
`members_without` (up to 20 pages of members) ran once for each of the two rows with the same arguments, and the Channel Setup draft check read the guild, roles and channels again. *Avoid:* read the members once per request, and pass the score's `Live` to `channels::overview_check` (`world_of`).

**Auto Setup stays at Trusted Admin level.**
It sets `antinuke.quarantine_role`, and `lockdown.main_roles` while Monitor Public Roles is on, which Save puts behind the Owner bar. This is on purpose (owner rule: "if Auto Setup may do it, a Trusted Admin may"). *Avoid:* do not raise Auto Setup or its score fixes to Owner without asking the owner.

**"Setup channel" left no config-change log line and posted a second panel.**
`verify_channel` committed a version without `log_change`, and posted the verify panel on every press, also into the channel that was already set. *Avoid:* every route that commits a version calls `log_change` with it. The panel is posted only into a new or taken-over channel.

**Dashboard fixes and Channel Setup pushes saved a locked state as the newest backup.**
The security-score fixes (`ops::backup`) and the Channel Setup push inserted a backup with no state check, while `backup::save`, backup create and delete refuse during a lock or panic mode. The newest backup is anti-nuke's restore source, so a later restore put the locked layout back. *Avoid:* these backups are skipped while `recovery::no_backup_now` is true (a lock, panic mode, or a nuke revert's guard job), and the log says "no backup" (`recovery::backup_named`).

**Dashboard lock buttons skipped the lock permits.**
Lock, unlock and lock update on the dashboard needed only Editor, so a member could lock a server type or lift a lock that chat would refuse them, also during panic mode's mod command lock. *Avoid:* below Trusted Admin, `may_lock` asks the same permits as chat (`lockdown` or `lock_updates`, plus `missing` for each kind, shared in `automod::lockdown`) and refuses while `mod_lock` is on. The per-permit command rate limit of `gated` is not mirrored (no dashboard rate limits yet).

**Seeing a ticket channel was enough to talk as staff, and any Viewer could read transcripts.**
A plain message in a ticket channel was relayed to the member by DM before any permit check (only the command branch checked), so anyone a wrong overwrite let into the channel could DM the member as staff. The transcript route needed only Viewer and served any bot file named `ticket_log_` in any channel of the server. *Avoid:* every relay path checks the same live rule (`tickets::commands::allowed`: a Manage Tickets permit; admin commands Owner/EO/TA). Access roles are removed (permits only): any holder of a plain Discord role got ticket staff power, and nothing guarded who gave that role. The default Admins and Moderators permits get Manage Tickets (Auto Setup, migration 3931). The transcript route needs `api::staff(.., &["tickets"])`, a channel the server's config ever named as its ticket log, a `cdn.discordapp.com` URL before and after redirects, and at most 25 MB, also while reading. Limit: config history keeps 200 versions, so a log channel older than that is refused until transcripts are recorded per ticket (phase B).

**Saving a Discord AutoMod rule turned off all its Brim actions.**
`set_bot_actions` took `&mut config["discord_automod"][key]` for `actions`, `scopes` and `locks`. serde_json's index inserts `null` for a missing key, so a rule with no mention scope or lock saved `"scopes": null, "locks": null`. The bot cannot read a map from `null`, put the whole section back to defaults, and every Brim action on Discord AutoMod rules in that server stopped. *Avoid:* never take `&mut v[key]` on JSON just to read or remove. Use `get_mut` and index only to write a value. The lenient map readers (`lenient::map`, `map_of_lists`) read `null` as empty, so servers that already stored a `null` recover.

**A refused Discord login token gave a 500.**
When Discord refused a session's OAuth token (authorization removed, or token expired), `/api/guilds` and the verify page's submit answered 500 "internal error", and the dashboard showed "Could not load servers" with no way back except logging out by hand. `Discord::user_guilds` turned every HTTP error into the generic internal error. *Avoid:* every call that uses the session's own token goes through `auth::user_guilds`, which deletes the session and answers 401 on a Discord 401. The UI and the verify page send a 401 to `/auth/login`.

See [crates/dashboard/ui/PITFALLS.md](crates/dashboard/ui/PITFALLS.md) for the two worst ones (#143): a `>` in a `view!` attribute ends the tag, and reading a signal after its page is gone crashes the whole dashboard. Also:

- **List closures that call `.get`** rebuild every child on each change and kill CSS transitions (#99). *Avoid:* read per element with a `Memo` and `class:`.
- **The UI crate is not in the cargo workspace.** `cargo clippy` at the root does not check it. *Avoid:* run `cargo clippy --target wasm32-unknown-unknown -- -D warnings` in `crates/dashboard/ui`.
- **The debug wasm is about 100 MB** and makes the page close silently in Playwright. *Avoid:* use `trunk build --release` for screenshots and the e2e sweep.
- **A refused save vanished with the toast** (8d72d50). *Avoid:* an error the user must fix stays up until it is fixed or clicked away.
- **Auto Setup's steps read their state from the toast text** (#151). The toast closes itself after 3 s, so "Running" and "Done" vanished and the steps never stayed green. *Avoid:* keep a run's state in its own signal and show the result in the page. The toast only announces it.
- **Check access before you look at the body.** `put_section` and `import` validated the input before the level check, so someone without access learned the shape of a section from the error. *Avoid:* call `require` first, then validate.
- **A section struct without `#[serde(default)]` breaks the first save.** `RaidCfg` had required fields, so turning Join Raid on for a server that never saved it was refused ("missing field `suspicious_only`"). *Avoid:* a config struct with a `Default` impl gets `#[serde(default)]` on the struct.
- **Pages everyone may open still need greyed write buttons.** Lockdown was `Full` for viewers, and History's Roll back, Overview's Fix buttons and system switches looked usable although the server refused. *Avoid:* grey the write buttons for levels that cannot write.
- **A number field without a schema default must be optional in the config struct.** The form saves an emptied field as its default, or `null` without one. Join Raid's `id_margin_ms` is a plain `i64`, so emptying it made every save fail. `schema::tests::number_fields_without_default_accept_null` checks every field, and `one_field_alone_deserializes` checks every section loads with one field set. Stepping an optional field back to 0 empties it: Join Raid's ID flag at 0 would flag every joiner in a raid. *Avoid:* give a page whose main action is a write `need(level)` in `access::page`, or disable the button with `Access::rank`.
- **A user choice kept only in the DOM is lost on reload.** The light/dark toggle only set `data-theme`, so a reload went back to dark. *Avoid:* save a choice the user makes in the UI (here in `localStorage`, read again in `main`).
- **Every bug from the deep test of 2026-11-10** is listed with its cause in `tests/e2e/dashboard-deep-test-2026-11-10.md` of the archived old repository thomkr01/TommyBot. The UI ones (hover contrast, double page load, copy button, null number fields, `.row label` on the Import button) are in the UI file, sections 3 to 7.
- **`tests/e2e/deep.js`** checks every write route for seven access levels, every page as every level, the owner's number fields, toggles, save and cancel, the server switcher, 2FA, Auto Setup, toasts, theme, mobile and hover contrast.
- **The backup view sorted channels by category id, not position.** `(parent or own id, kind, position)` put categories (and loose channels) in snowflake order, so the tree did not match Discord, and matching children by category name broke when two categories share a name. *Avoid:* sort by `(voice, position, id)` and group children by `parent_id` (sent as a string: snowflakes lose precision as JS numbers).
- **A dropdown option's `selected=` stops working once the user has picked** (#158). The attribute only sets the default, so after Cancel the dropdown still showed the old pick. *Avoid:* use `prop:selected=` on every `<option>`, and `prop:value`/`prop:checked` on inputs (done in `form.rs`, `embeds.rs`, `lockdown.rs`; no plain `selected=`/`checked=`/`value=` bindings are left). The e2e sweep picks a channel on Logging, presses Cancel and checks the dropdown. It waits for the page to load first, because a pick made before the config arrives is overwritten by it.
- **Role names from Discord already start with `@` for @everyone** (#158). The dashboard added its own `@` and showed `@@everyone`. *Avoid:* trim a leading `@` before adding one (done in `form::items` and the `md.rs` role mentions; `recovery.rs` and the wizard's ticket pings already did). The bot never writes a role name after `@`.
- **The toast looked like two pieces.** A coloured `border-left` on a rounded box curves at the corners, and the absolutely placed progress strip starts inside that border, so the two met as a hook. *Avoid:* one colour mark per box. Put strips and edges inside an `overflow:hidden` box with `left:0; right:0` and no border on that side. Also `cargo fmt` in `crates/dashboard/ui` reformats unrelated files; revert them before committing.

- **Editing or deleting a case left no trace.** Anyone with Manage Cases could rewrite or close a case and a trusted admin could delete one, with no record of who did it, so a staff member could hide their own history. `case_delete` also answered OK for a case that did not exist. *Avoid:* a change that can erase moderation history writes an append-only row (`cases::record`, kind `case_edit`/`case_delete`) in the same transaction, and a delete of nothing answers 404. Other erasing routes already keep history (`note_edits`, `handled_by`/`decided_by`; Revert Center and purge are logged).
- **An Admin-only check let every trusted admin through a permit-gated page.** `api::staff` and `access::page`'s `has` treat Admin as holding every permit, which is wrong for a permit meant to be owner-granted (View Mod Stats). *Avoid:* such a permit gets its own check (`access::mod_stats`) placed before `has`. `page_access` fetches permit perms for trusted admins too, and `keep_exemptions` stops non-owners from changing who holds it on save, rollback and import.
- **A missing `enabled` key was read as on.** Overview's switches guessed their own defaults and showed Anti Nuke and Join Raid on for a server that never saved them, while the bot, the security score and the pages read them as off. The settings pages ringed cards red only when `enabled` was literally `false`, so a never-switched-on system showed no "Not in use" ring. *Avoid:* read a missing switch as the bot does (the schema default, off for every system). Never keep a second copy of a default in the UI.
- **The 2FA check ran before the DM code.** `confirm` checked the TOTP code first, so anyone holding an MCP token could send wrong TOTP codes and lock the owner out of dashboard 2FA for 10 minutes. *Avoid:* when a request carries several secrets, check the one the caller must already hold (the DM code) first, and touch shared lockout counters only after it passes.
- **An action ran something other than what was reviewed.** Push ran the draft stored at that moment, which another editor (or a late save) could have changed after the preview. *Avoid:* every reply carries a version of what it shows (`updated_at`), the action sends it back, and a mismatch is refused with 409.
- **An approval showed less than it approved.** The MCP code DM cut the change at 1500 characters, so the end of a long change was approved unseen. Same class: `embed_post`'s DM showed only `{repost}` and not the embed it posts; a rollback's preview diffed the permits copy in the last config version instead of the live tables (a rescue-claimed Extra Owner was removed without showing); the embed could be edited between the DM and the code; a `target` on an action without `{t}` in its path was never checked but went into the DM's inline code, so backticks and a fake block could reshape it. *Avoid:* an approval shows all of what runs or refuses (propose says "split it"). Drop input an action does not use before it reaches the approval. Preview what the action really reads (the stored embed, live permits). Store a hash of what was shown and refuse at confirm (`stale`) when a fresh preview differs (migration 3100 `preview_hash`).
- **A check before a slow call did not hold for the write after it.** 2FA enrollment checked "not confirmed", sent the DM, then upserted the secret with no condition. A confirm during the DM had its secret replaced while staying confirmed, so a stolen session could end up holding the owner's 2FA. Confirm had the mirror race: it marked confirmed whatever secret was stored by then. *Avoid:* put the precondition in the write itself (`do update ... where not confirmed`, `update ... where secret = <the one checked>`) and treat 0 rows as refused. (The rescue-key regenerate replaces on purpose.)
- **A host allow list let any page on that host catch an OAuth code.** MCP OAuth took any path on a known client host, and a client id signed under that rule stays valid for 10 years. *Avoid:* compare host and path exactly (`REDIRECTS`), no query or port, and check the redirect again at `/authorize`, not only at registration.
- **An empty list switched an authorization rule off.** "Only owners may hide a channel from staff" compared against the staff roles. A server without roles on the Admins and Moderators permits had none, so nothing counted as hidden and anyone could do it. *Avoid:* decide what an empty list means for the rule (here: every newly limited channel hides it from staff) and say in the refusal how to fix it.
- **A filter meant for display also weakened the check.** Member overwrites were left out of the issue list so they would not make rows pulse, which also let a dangerous member overwrite through Push. *Avoid:* filter for display where it is shown. The list that decides keeps every row.
- **A picker's filter is not validation.** The UI left the quarantine and main roles out of the role picker, but the server took any role id, and the UI's own list still offered roles the server would refuse. *Avoid:* the server validates with the same rule (`Ctx::pickable`) and sends the allowed ids, and the UI offers only those.
- **The same rule written twice drifts.** The dashboard's Report button had its own copy of the bot's report checks and missed some: permit holders, a quarantined moderator's saved roles, a server that opted out, and putting a rejected entry back to pending. The permit rule drifted the same way: the bot used the member's highest-ranked permit only, the dashboard the union of all their permits. *Avoid:* a rule both processes enforce lives once in `brim_core` (`raiders::report`, `raiders::not_collectable`, `raiders::allow`); each side only looks up what only it can reach (Discord) and passes it in. Both sides read `permits::top_permit` (ties: the oldest permit). The appeal rules live only in the dashboard.
- **A body extractor answers before the handler's own check.** The "not through the tunnel" check sat inside the handler, after `Json(..)`, so a request without a JSON body got 415 instead of 404 and the route showed it exists. *Avoid:* a check that must refuse before anything else is a `route_layer` middleware, not the first line of the handler.

- **Writing into Discord JSON by position panicked after the decision was saved.** Appeals `close_log` added the Decision field with `embeds[0]["fields"].as_array_mut`. `v[0]` panics when `v` is not an array or is empty, and `v["k"]` write panics when `v` is a string, number, bool or array. A log message with no embed panicked after `decide` had committed, so the member never got the verdict DM. The UI embed editor's `set_at` also panicked on a stored `"image": "url"` and now replaces a non-object parent with `{}`. The Decision field could pass Discord's 1024 limit, which made Discord refuse the edit and left the buttons on the log. *Avoid:* to change JSON from outside (Discord replies, stored documents, user input), use `get_mut(i)` / `as_object_mut` / `entry(..)` and do nothing on a wrong shape (reading with `v["x"]` never panics). Side work like `close_log` must not stop what comes after it. The dashboard and the bot's button path cut the Decision field to 1024 and skip a 26th field.

- **The config-change log read the new config.** A save that cleared or moved `logging.config_changes` was announced only in the new channel, so clearing it hid itself. *Avoid:* a caller that saved a version passes it to `log_change`, which then also posts to that version's predecessor's channel. Events that saved nothing post to the current channel only, or moving the log would not stop them reaching the old one.
- **The lockdown check looked only at main-role overwrites.** An allow on @everyone, another role or a member beats the lock too. *Avoid:* `tshoot` reports every overwrite that allows writing, except bots' managed roles, the bot itself and ticket channels. Only main-role and @everyone ones get the automatic fix, since a staff channel's allow is often on purpose. Apply Fix once took the write allow off @everyone in every channel, which closes a channel everyone is meant to write in, and the bot cannot undo it. So an @everyone allow beside set main roles is listed with its fix marked `opt_in`, and Apply Fix runs it only for the ids the user ticked (`picked`). Main-role allows are fixed as before, and with no main roles set @everyone is the main role.
- **A dashboard action skipped panic mode's mod command lock.** Accepting an appeal unbanned during panic mode while the bot refused `t!unban`, and the dashboard's own Discord client bypassed the `commands::gated` rate limit. *Avoid:* a dashboard path that does what a mod command does checks the lock (`appeals::mod_lock`, from the pending `panic_end` job; only owners, Extra Owners and Trusted Admins pass) and runs as a bot job through the command's own code, not a copy. The accept is the bot job `appeal_accept` -> `appeals::accept_job`, which runs the log button's `decide` (permit, panic lock, rate limit (a breach quarantines), lift, undo, verdict). The dashboard keeps its checks only to answer early, and a refusal is posted in the appeal log. `panic::start` saves the job (without unlock) before its slow category calls, rewrites its payload after each opened category (`end_payload`) so a crash leaves a job that closes what was opened, and deletes it when the start is undone.
- **"Loaded" meant any IP list.** `IpLists::loaded` was true with only the Tor list, so VPN and hosting addresses passed silently. *Avoid:* the refresh warns for each enabled list that is still empty.
- **The NSFW live lane had no per-member cap.** One member's flood filled the shared live lane. *Avoid:* at most 4 images per member wait live (`PER_MEMBER_LIVE`). The rest go to the backlog.
- **A bot path had a lower bar than its dashboard twin.** `t!backups load` and `clear` needed only the Custom backups permit while the dashboard asked Admin + 2FA, and `load` ran during a lock (it put back the locked permissions). The dashboard's `backups` section and `lockdown.main_roles` were Editor level although they steer anti-nuke's revert, panic's restore and what the monitors guard. *Avoid:* a change that steers anti-nuke takes anti-nuke's bar (Owner/EO + fresh 2FA) on every path: the bot command (`commands::backup_cmd`), the bot settings panel (`main_roles` while a monitor is on), `put_section`, rollback and import (`api::main_roles_bar`). A restore refuses during a lock or panic in `backup::load`, which the dashboard's job goes through too. Deleting one backup is a clear done in steps (ten deletes empty the store), so `t!backups delete`, `/backups delete` and the dashboard's delete have the same bar, and they and `clear` refuse during a lock or panic (`backup::locked_or_panic`, `recovery::busy`). The Backups settings form is read-only below Owner.

**A deleted main role made "Move to main roles" a dead end.**
The Security score fix refused to run with "a main role no longer exists" when one saved main role was deleted in Discord, without naming the role or a way forward. Saved role lists keep ids of deleted roles (nothing cleans them), and the fix required every id to exist. *Avoid:* skip saved roles that no longer exist and act on the rest. When none is left, say where to pick them again (Lockdown > Main roles). Other dashboard fixes and the lockdown plan already work from the live role list.

**A second way to change something had a weaker check than the main way.**
Root cause: a rule was put on the path that was built first, not on the state it protects. Cases: (1) Chat config edits (`t!` settings rows, `t!logging`, `t!pingrole`) took any typed channel or role id, while the dashboard's `check_ids` refuses ids of other servers. (2) The hold whitelist flag `quarantine` on a permit equals the Owner-only quarantine whitelist, but `antinuke_exempt` and the Permits page lock left it out, so a Trusted Admin could grant it. (3) Backup create needed only Editor and the backups permit, worked during a lock or panic, and deleted backups past Max Images, while backup delete needs Owner or Extra Owner, 2FA and no lock or panic: ten creates were a clear. *Avoid:* when you add or review a path that changes some state, list every other path to the same state (dashboard, chat, slash, MCP, jobs) and give it the same check. Fixes: chat `resolve`, the two ticket commands and `t!whitelist` check ids against this server (removing takes an id as given); `quarantine` is in `antinuke_exempt` and the UI `owner_only`; backup create is Owner or Extra Owner (no 2FA) on the dashboard and in chat; `backup::save` refuses every backup during a lock, panic or nuke (also when the queued job runs, and checked after its snapshot, since panic can start during the reads); only automatic backups delete the oldest, and they skip a lock (its overwrites would become the restore source); a rollback runs `check_ids` and `check_held_roles` on every section it changes. MCP writes and Auto Setup need no change.
- **A Message Builder access refusal answered 400.** The "pings members" and "sends as a webhook" refusals for staff below Trusted Admin were `BadRequest`, so the access matrix could not tell them from bad input. **Rule:** a refusal because of who the caller is answers 403 (`ApiError::Forbidden`). 400 is only for input that is wrong for everyone. The matrix in `tests/e2e/deep.js` runs in CI, and any answer other than 401/403 to a refused user fails it.

- **A server error said only "internal error".** Fix All showed "Staff cannot use their channels (internal error)"; the cause (Discord 404 on a permission overwrite) was only in the dashboard log, with nothing that tied that log line to the toast. **Rule:** `ApiError::Internal` answers "Server error, code XXXXXXXX. The cause is in the dashboard log." (a Discord cause `internal_text` recognises: its plain text plus " (code XXXXXXXX)") and logs the full cause with `error_id=XXXXXXXX` inside the request span (method, path, server id), so `logs_tail` (dashboard, contains the code) finds the line. The code is random per error and carries nothing internal. Errors with a cause the user can act on keep their own text (`BadRequest`, `Forbidden`, `TooMany`, `Busy`). A log field named `code=` after `?`/`&`, or a JSON `"code"`, is masked by `logs_tail`, so the field is `error_id`.

- **A dashboard change was recorded only in a Discord channel.** `log_change` posted the line to the config-changes log channel and kept nothing, so a server with no such channel (or a deleted one) had no record of who changed what. *Avoid:* `log_change` takes who made the change (`by`, 0 for Brim itself) and stores every line in `dashboard_audit` before it posts (migration 4031, 90 days, removed with the server's data). The dashboard role may insert but not update those rows. A new record of who did what is stored first and posted second.

- **A Tickets save refused "<id> is not a channel or role of this server" for an id the page had loaded.** The bot clears `tickets.category` when its category is deleted. A page loaded before that still held the old id, and `put_section` ran `check_ids` (id not in the stored config, not in the server) before the version check, so the stale page got a refusal instead of "reload". *Avoid:* `put_section` answers `Conflict` before any other check when `expected_version` is not the newest. Every section page goes through it, so no page can write an id the stored config dropped. A check that compares new with stored values runs only after the version is current.

**Sessions, public links and the edge trusted too much.**
- A session lasted 7 days whether used or not, kept the same id through a 2FA step-up, and stored the Discord OAuth token as plain text. *Avoid:* `dash_session.last_seen_at` (migration 4032) ends a login after 24 hours without use (refreshed at most every 5 minutes). A step-up moves the session to a new id and leaves the old id 30 s without the step-up (`totp_verify`), so a copied cookie never gains it. The token is stored with `auth::seal_token` (key stream from the client secret and the session id). The cookie is always Secure: the dashboard refuses to start with an `http://` URL other than localhost.
- `CF-Connecting-IP` was believed from any sender: a container or a host process could pass web verification's alt-account and VPN checks with a made-up address. *Avoid:* `auth::edge_only` keeps the header only on a connection from the `cloudflared` container (DNS name, looked up again after 30 s). New dashboard code that needs the visitor's address reads that header after this layer. The MCP server reads it raw, for its login rate limit only.
- The rate limiter keyed only on the `sid` cookie, so no cookie or a random one per request was never limited. *Avoid:* every route also takes from a per-address bucket (4x the session budget), `brim_core::ratelimit`. The MCP login routes have their own (`limit_login`).
- Rescue claims: 5 per user in memory only, a key without an authenticator worked alone, and every failed claim posted a log line. *Avoid:* wrong codes count on the key row (10 an hour, then the key waits; a wrong key cannot raise it, so nobody without the key can lock the owner out; no per-server lockout, by owner rule). Keys without a secret are refused and the owner is asked for a new one. Failed claims post once per server per hour with the count of the hour before (`claim_failed`).
- Viewers read every holder of Extra Owner, Trusted Admin and each permit, also in history diffs, and could export it. *Avoid:* `api::hide_holders` on every route that returns permits to a Viewer (`get_config`, `diff_versions`). The Overview score leaves out the ownership-change rows for Viewers (they name paused and kept holders). Check any new route that names permit holders. Export is for Editor and up. Session and address buckets share one map, so their keys carry a prefix (`sid:`, `ip:`): a cookie `sid=ip:<address>` drained another address's bucket.
- The MCP login limit (first 20 at once) failed `crates/mcp/tests/oauth.rs`: test requests carry no `CF-Connecting-IP`, so they all share one bucket, and the local run's output filter hid the failure. *Avoid:* check a new limit on a route with tests against the number of requests those tests send. Read the whole `test result` list of every crate, not the first failure only.
- Raid links never expired and uploaded pictures were served by their hash from any server. *Avoid:* raid pages and `raid_log` rows end after 30 days (hourly sweep). A new upload gets a random link (`media.token`). Older uploads keep their hash link so posted messages keep their picture.
- **A saved log or command reply without `{text}` dropped the bot's own text.** `{text}` was only an important variable, and `events::frame_over` laid the saved frame over the log, so a frame with no `{text}`, or with it in a second embed, content or a link, sent only the frame and lost the log details or command answer. *Avoid:* every entry whose template has `{text}` gets it as `Def::required` (with `Def::what`). `botmsg::missing_text` looks for it only where every sender shows it (the first embed's title, description, fields, footer and author text, since `bot_embed` and frames send the first embed only). The dashboard save and kit import refuse such a copy (`botmsgs::problem`), a backup load leaves it out (`msgbackup::bot_messages_to_load`), and `botmsg::render` sends the default for a stored copy without it (copies from before the check, or brought back by Undo). The UI notice (`ui/src/botmsgs.rs` `missing`) repeats the rule for display only. MCP has no bot message write.
- **Short replies were fixed sentences in code, outside the Bot messages page.** Verification side replies and `/tag` answers were `Ok("...".into)` strings and `format!`. *Avoid:* a short reply is an entry `reply.<area>.<name>` (one message = one entry, default = today's sentence as message text, values as named variables), and every sender calls `reply::say(app, gid, key, vars)` (text, embeds, `edited`). Channel sends use `commands::say_said`; interaction replies put the same text and embeds in the response. A command's reply goes through its `cmd.<command>` frame only while the server has no edited version of the entry (`commands::send_reply(.., frame)`). `botmsg::uses_stored` is the one test of "is the edited version used" for `render` and `say`. A test scans the source for `"reply.` ids (unknown key = panic at send time) and fails on an entry no code sends. A member-typed value (a tag name) is only a variable, filled in one pass, never searched for more variables. Still fixed text, for later packages: the owner/Extra Owner verify answers, the DM-failed, "Check your DMs" and "Verification done." lines (`verify.rs`), the dashboard's tag result text, `Slash commands are disabled` in `slash.rs` and `general.rs` (same words as `reply.tag.slash_off`, to merge into one entry in P7). Not live tested.
- **Ticket side replies were `error("...")` embeds in code.** Panel button answers, staff relay errors, the closing notice and the DM-ticket refusals were literals, so a server could not reword them. *Avoid:* each is an entry `reply.ticket.<name>` (table `TICKET_REPLIES` in `botmsg.rs`: one embed, red for an error, blue for a notice, default = the old sentence), sent with `tickets::say_key` (channel) or `reply::say` (interaction answer, `panel::answer`). A refusal passed up as `&'static str` (`gate`, `open`, `refusal`) carries the entry key, and the caller says it. Only the words changed: every permit, blacklist, pause, lockdown, quarantine and cap check runs as before and picks the same key. `workflow::summary` returns `None` for a non-ticket channel and the caller says `reply.ticket.not_ticket` (one entry for the two old copies). Panel ("this server") and DM ("that server") refusals stay separate entries: they talk to the member from different places. Ticket text not converted yet has an owner package in the plan (section 12 on tommybot-data). Not live tested.
- **Lock notices and short lock replies were literals in `lockdown.rs`.** The "The server is locked" notice, the "Lockdown Updates" post and the lock command's short answers could not be reworded. *Avoid:* entries `reply.lock.*` (`botmsg::lock_replies`). The two notices are embeds whose `{text}` is the update list the bot builds (`lockdown::updates_text`, empty until staff send one, so `{text}` is required but may render empty). The rest are message text. `lockdown::update` returns `reply::Said`. `fail_key` builds the "Lock Result:" reply from an entry: an edited version with an embed replaces the result embed, text only goes in the usual embed. Permit checks and `commands::gated` are unchanged. The notice default is byte-equal to the old one (test `lock_notice_numbers_updates_past_the_dropped_ones`). Lock result, loading and overview embeds and `LOCK_TYPES` belong to later packages (plan section 13). Not live tested.
- **The confirm prompt, Undo button and menu answers were literals.** "Do you want to proceed?" and its lines, the Undo refusals and the Message Builder menu's "Slow down" and "no longer in use" could not be reworded. *Avoid:* entries `reply.confirm.*`, `reply.undo.*`, `reply.menu.*` (`botmsg::confirm_and_button_replies`). The prompt is an embed whose required `{text}` is the target list the bot builds (`{seconds}` optional). The rest is message text sent with `reply::say`. A sentence inside a bigger result ("Command failed", "Failed to ban", the lost ticket permit in `closeall`) uses `reply::say_line`, which falls back to the default when an edited version has no text. Code-built text that is not an entry (`modcmds::run` results, `cmdcfg::button` refusals) becomes a `Said` with `From<String>`. Only the words changed: in `undo::handle` the no-permit stop still comes before any case lookup and before `Acting::claim`; `press` keeps the command settings check, the `not_current` check and `modcmds::run`; the click-time `tickets` permit re-check in `confirm.rs` is unchanged (test `a_press_needs_the_permit_of_its_command`). The prompt default is byte-equal to the old one (test `ten_second_question`). Left as is: the Undo reason "Undo of case #N" and the Action Log line on the mod log (package D1). Not live tested.
**The bot update alert went out titled "Raider List".**
Every bot owner DM was a `raider_notify` job sent in the editable `dm.raider_notify` entry, so the update and MCP token alerts carried the Raider List title and an edit could blank them. *Avoid:* the update alert and the MCP token made/revoked alerts are fixed text in code (`raiders::alert_owner`, `fixed: "update"` or `"mcp"` in the job), each in its own embed ("Bot update", "MCP token") with the mention and user ID of who did it. Never editable entries, not on the Bot messages page (owner rule, for security). Later "all embeds editable" packages must keep it. A bot build without `fixed` sends the job `text` in the old entry; the update that installs this change shows the old title once. Raider List confirm/verify alerts stay in `dm.raider_notify`; panic, join raid and voice raid alerts stay in the editable `dm.owner_alert`.
- **Report, case, note, whitelist and catch channel replies were literals.** *Avoid:* entries `reply.report.*`, `reply.case.*`, `reply.note.*`, `reply.whitelist.*`, `reply.catch.warning` (`botmsg::report_case_replies`). Report answers are `Said` (`reports::tell`, ephemeral); mod command results use `reply::say_line` (`modcmds::say`) and still pass the command frame. One sentence, one entry (`reply.undo.no_case` is merged into `reply.case.no_case`). Limits in note, reason and proof replies are written in the default; a test ties them to `NOTE_MIN`, `NOTE_MAX`, `CASE_TEXT_MAX`. Traps: (1) the Close case button compares the reply with `modcmds::closed_text` (async, same entry per server); never compare a reply with a hard-coded sentence. (2) The catch channel skips posting when its warning is already there, found by embed title; the check also takes the edited title or text, and the old "Catch channel" title still counts. `cmd.whitelist` is locked, so its replies are locked entries until the security package opens them with guards. Only words changed: report permit, `gated`, `cmdcfg::button`, log channel, `permits::check`, whitelist 2FA/permit checks run as before. Still code: report and AutoMod report logs, their Action Log lines, User Info embeds, `reply::case_text`, note card, proof list, Interactive Whitelist embeds, "Unknown case type", whitelist usage line, Report Message form title.
- **Ticket command results, staff notices and the paused DM were literals.** *Avoid:* entries `reply.ticket.*` (`botmsg::TICKET_REPLIES`, one embed each, red for a refusal), `reply.closeall.*` and `ticket.paused` (`{reason}`; server footer and time added by code). `workflow` results are a `Line` (entry key and values) said in the server's words later; `Ok(None)` means nothing more to say. A sentence built from parts is one entry per case (`command_only_on`/`_off`, `note_no_log_deleted`/`_kept`, `closeall.partly_anon`), never a variable holding English words. Ticket log lines (`workflow::log_update`) keep their own text. Still code: ticket permit refusals, "not set up yet", panel ticket relay refusal, usage lines of `reply`, `snippetadd`, `reassign`, the `tickets_cap_problem` cap text (shared with the dashboard save), the DM type pick's "This ticket is closed." when no server is known.
- **Errors, usage lines and permit refusals were literals.** *Avoid:* entries in `botmsg::error_replies` (`reply.error.*`, `reply.settings.*`, `reply.invite.*`, `reply.appeal.*`, `reply.nsfw.*`, `reply.raider.*`) and `reply.ticket.*` rows. A parser keeps returning its text; the send point puts it in `reply.error.wrong_use` with `reply::say_wrong` (`reply::UNKNOWN_COMMAND` has its own entry); a usage line goes in `{text}` of `reply.error.usage` or `reply.ticket.usage`. `build` marks `{text}` required only when the first embed shows it, so a message-text entry with `{text}` got no required placeholder and an edit without it dropped the usage line. A message-text entry sets `required` and `what` itself (as feed posts do); the scan test checks it.
- **Security texts could not be reworded, and a long author name made Discord refuse a whole message.** *Avoid:* anti-nuke, panic, join raid, whitelist, and the owner security, invite back, rescue key, MCP decision and 2FA code DMs are entries with `Def::security` (group "Security"): only Owner and Extra Owners save, revert, undo or import them (`botmsgs::may_edit`), Revert all leaves them, each change is an audit line with old and new version (`change_line`). A required placeholder counts only as plain text in the first embed's title or description, within its first 10 lines (`botmsg::fact_places`, `visible`: not in a spoiler, link target, `<...>` or code block language tag; footer, author, field do not count). An edit cannot hold control, zero-width or text direction characters (`hides_facts`). Logs need `{title}` and `{text}`, rescue DM `{name}` and `{page}`, 2FA DM `{code}`. `render` sends the default when an edit's own words push a fact past Discord's limits (`keeps_facts` looks only in the same places; a spare copy in a footer spoiler does not count; a value too long for the default too keeps the edit). Never accept a placeholder anywhere `shown` looks or search every string at send time (`{title}` in the author line, or a footer-spoiler copy, would pass); `frame_over` must not drop a frame's author. 2FA and MCP decision DMs stay per server; the 2FA DM is sent with no server, so a server's edit never shows. The dashboard's `Discord::dm` reads a bot-wide entry's copy under guild 0. `botmsg::limit` cuts title, description, footer, fields and the author name (256): an author over 256 made Discord refuse the whole message.
- **DMs with no server were literals, and the data request DM was locked.** DM ticket commands (usage, confirmation on/off, "No server found", server menu, "request expired", "cancelled", "wait a few seconds", "message was deleted", "This ticket is closed.") are sent before a server is chosen, so no server's edit applies. *Avoid:* bot-wide entries `reply.dm.*` (`botmsg::outside_replies`, group "Outside a server (bot owner)"), listed in `botmsg::BOT_WIDE`, one copy under guild 0 that only the bot owner changes; senders call `reply::say_wide` (`tickets::say_dm` for a channel, `say_wide_line` for text in the menu). A test ties the group to `BOT_WIDE`. Outside DMs that state facts about the recipient (`dm.data_request`, `dm.raider_notify`) are `Def::strict`: P8 fact rules apply (`{text}` as plain text in the first embed's title or description, no hiding characters, default sent when an edit loses it), without the Security chip or Owner/EO rule. `msgbackup` and kit import skip outside entries. Left: the dashboard shows an outside entry as editable to a server Admin, who gets the server's refusal on save.
- **A security text changed without a 2FA code, and a backup load replaced security texts without a log line.** *Avoid:* `botmsgs::may_edit` asks `require(..., step_up = true)` for a security text (save, revert, undo); the kit import checks it separately (it does not use `may_edit`) when the applied part holds a security text; a preview needs none. Every new route that writes `bot_messages` must go through `may_edit` or make the same check. The backup load needs 2FA (dashboard `recovery::load`, command `fresh_2fa`) and writes one `dashboard_audit` line per security text whose copy changed (`botmsg::change_line`, shared with the dashboard), under the user who asked, in the load's transaction. The bot does not post these lines to the config change log channel; the Backup Loaded server log covers the load.

- **Moderation logs and command answers built their facts in code; a fields-only log had no place for a frame.** *Avoid:* the 13 keys in `botmsg::FACTS` (`log.mod_command.*`, `reply.mod.*`: case log, case edits, proof, note logs, "Case Deleted", "Old Cases Removed", punishment result, several-member result, short case line) are the whole message with named placeholders (`{case}`, `{kind}`, `{member}`, `{moderator}`, `{duration}`, `{reason}`, `{changes}`, `{when}` ...), `Def::details`, `Def::strict` (P8 fact rules, default sent when an edit loses a fact, old and new version in the audit line), not Security. A log names its entry with `Log::entry` and its values with `Log::var` (a later value replaces an earlier one); `events::framed_with` lays an entry of facts over the whole log (fields replace; a frame still adds its fields). Code builds each log itself until a server edits the entry; `mod_texts_tests` lays the default beside every builder. An older frame around `{text}` keeps working: `{text}` holds every fact (`missing_text`, the editor's `missing`).
