Facts may stay in fields where the default has them (`fact_strings`). `render` drops a field with an empty value (Discord refuses the whole message for one). Answers: `ModTexts::load` reads the three `reply.mod.*` copies; `result_embeds_with`/`summary_with` use them; the `Applied.text` each command builds stays code, because the result embed and confirm path read it back (`Case #N: kind <@id>` first line, then notes). A variable an entry lists but a sender forgets shows as literal `{name}`; the case log sets every purge variable to empty. An edited answer is still wrapped by the command's `cmd.<command>` frame; short replies that become one whole entry (`say_said`) are the exception. Still code: the "Add Reason" pointer embed, the purge note in the channel.
- **Server event logs from the audit log built their facts in code, and the values never reached the sender.** *Avoid:* the types in `botmsg::AUDIT_LOGS` (member, role, channel, server, invite, webhook, emoji, sticker, event, thread, pin) are entries of facts (`is_facts`) with one template (`audit_entries`): `{by}`, `{channel}`, `{target}`, `{when}`, `{reason}`, `{changes}` (changes, "Not Shown" line and extra information as one block, which `render::audit` cuts from the lines after the reason). `render::audit` returned a finished `Embed`, so `Log::var` values were lost through `Entry`, `LogMessage`, `send_event_log`; now `Entry` and `LogMessage` carry `vars`, `events::send_entry` passes them to `framed_with`, and every builder sets every placeholder, empty ones too. A test in `render.rs` lays the default beside the built log (overwrite, update with a reason and no executor, webhook, prune). A variable name means the same in every area. `missing_text` and `keeps_facts` accept `{text}` wherever the old frame rules did (D1 entries too); counting an old frame only when `{text}` is in title or description silently skipped frames with `{text}` in footer, author or a field. An empty audit reason is no reason (the template drops a bare label).
- **The gateway and bot-made event logs built their facts in code, and two senders dropped a log's values.** *Avoid:* entries of facts in `botmsg::EVENT_LOGS` (message edit/delete/bulk delete, poll, publish, member join/leave, voice, event sign-up, boost level, automatic thread archive, Member Renamed, Timeout Ended, user name/avatar; group, title, lines `Label|value` or a line as written). `event_entries` builds each template, takes every placeholder a line shows as a variable, and every one shown outside a link target, except `{when}`, as required. A placeholder not in `FACT_VARS` panics when the list is built. Builders use `Log::fact(label, var, value)` (leaves an empty line out, still sets the variable) and return a `Log`, not an `Embed`, so `Entry`, `HeldLeave`, `HeldArchive` keep the values until `LogMessage` (`Log::parts`). `send_log_rows` and `send_log_pinging` must pass the values (as `send_event_log` does) or an edited entry shows `{name}`. Tests: `Logger::handle`, `flush`, `handle_voice` check every log of facts against its default (`reads_as_default`). A line whose only value is an empty code span (`` `{case}` ``) was kept as a bare label; `fill_text` drops it.

- **Automod, Discord AutoMod rule, Join Gate and verification logs built their facts in code.** Logs whose title holds a name (`*alex has been warned!*`, `alex's Verification Result:`) or share a title (five "Verification" logs, two "Rule Not Approved" logs) fell into one entry, so one edit had to fit different texts. *Avoid:* each is its own entry of facts in `botmsg::FACTS` (`botmsg::d3_entries`), as D1: the builder names its entry with `Log::entry` when the title cannot, sets every placeholder with `Log::var` (empty ones too); `d3_texts_tests` lays every default beside the builder. A sentence that differs per call (`taken_log`) is one entry per sentence (`given::BOT_TAKEN`, `OFFLINE_TAKEN`, `CHANNEL_TAKEN`), not a `{what}` placeholder. Filter-log message content is in a spoiler, so `{content}` cannot be required there; member, filter and channel are. A log with its own entry must skip a typed entry of facts with the same title ("could not read the rule" took "Rule Not Approved"'s edit); a typed frame (old "Verification") still applies. Each `FACT_VARS` name has one meaning for all entries (Join Gate refusal is `{refused}`; `{failed}` is D1's count). An old frame that kept `{text}` only in a footer, field or spoiler is judged by the fact rules and the default is sent. Still code: panic "Server Locked" log in `automod.rs`, join raid logs in `joingate.rs`, `users_only.rs`, `verify/deleted.rs` (anti-nuke log; D4), "NSFW Image Found" (`nsfw.rs`), "Webhooks Deleted" (`webhooks.rs`).
- **The general command answers built their facts in code, and a page turn dropped the command's saved frame.** Page buttons (`settings::handle`, kinds `about`, `ginfo`, `cmds`, `cases.*`, `tkblock`, `tksnip`, settings panels) rebuilt the page without the `cmd.<command>` frame, so an edited frame vanished on page two. *Avoid:* answers of `info`, `roleinfo`, `serverinfo`, `about`, `ping`, `invites` are entries of facts in `botmsg::ANSWERS` (`answer_entries`, one embed per message: a page is its own entry); code passes values only, through `reply::answer`, which fills every unset placeholder with empty, so no raw `{name}` reaches Discord. One entry holds every variant of its answer, because a bare `**Label:**` line whose placeholders are all empty is dropped; a line of free words with an empty placeholder keeps its words (an edit should put an optional value behind a label). Values in a field carry their own code style (an empty field must be blank to be dropped); values in a line do not. `answer_texts_tests` keeps the former builders as reference, compares every default, moves every placeholder and checks that a copy losing the required fact sends the default. A page turn lays the command's frame like page one (`settings::page_command`). Titles holding a name use the name's placeholder (`{member.username}'s Info:`), not `{title}`. Still code: plain refusals of `info` and `invites`, link maker refusals, flag block words (`Spam`, `Managed`, `No Avatar`).

- **Lockdown, auto slowmode, backup and scheduled action logs and the lock command answers built their words in code.** *Avoid:* logs are entries of facts as D1 (`botmsg::d5_entries`); the startup-check "Scheduled Action Failed" has its own entry so it cannot take the job give-up's edit (same title). Lock answers ("Brim Lockdown:", channel locked/unlocked, "Lock Result:", "Active lockdown modes:") are entries of facts (`botmsg::lock_answers`, `reply.lock.*`): each builder returns its embed with its entry and values (`lockdown::Entry`), and `lockdown::own` swaps in the server's edited version only when there is one (`reply::say(..).edited`); code keeps building the default and `d5_texts_tests` lays each default beside it. `Reply.text` stays code, like `Applied.text`. A short reply sentence a server edited with an embed still replaces the whole "Lock Result:" embed (P4 rule); without one the "Lock Result:" entry frames it.

- **Ticket, report, appeal and catch channel logs built their facts in code, and the dashboard posted its logs without any edited version.** `Discord::post_log` sent `log.build` as it was, so the dashboard's appeal log and "Lockdown Update" ignored a server's edit. *Avoid:* entries of facts as D1-D5 (`botmsg::d7_entries`, test `d7_texts_tests`, the dashboard's in `appeals.rs`) for New Ticket, Ticket Closed, the five "Ticket Updated" sentences (one entry each), message report log (headline is the author line), "Discord AutoMod Report", "Appeal Not Accepted", appeal log, two catch channel logs. `events::send_private` (ticket logs) takes a `Log` and passes its entry and values, as `send_log` does; `framed_log` does the same for report logs. `over`, `bot_embed`, `parts` live in `tommybot_logging::overlay` so the dashboard lays an edit over its logs the same way (`discord::framed`: own entry, then the group; the copy's fields are added, so the appellant's answers stay). Link fields are never filled from placeholders, so a copy's author line keeps the log's icon unless the copy sets one. Footers, the ticket closer's author line and the report thumbnail stay as built unless an edit sets its own. A Ticket Closed "Changes" list whose first line was over 1024 bytes made an empty field (Discord refuses the whole log); the field is left out. Still code: permit sync logs go to the anti-nuke log (D4).
- **Security logs built their facts in code, and an entry of facts with `{text}` let a frame skip every fact rule.** *Avoid:* one entry per text in `botmsg::SECURITY_LOGS` (anti-nuke, panic, join raid, permit sync, verification channel, log channel, NSFW, webhook spam; group Security, so Owner/EO, 2FA and backup audit rules of P8 and #416 apply); every placeholder shown is required; the title stays `{title}`. Senders call `overlay::security_log(title, key, values)`, which renders the default, so sent log and default cannot drift; complex builders (`antinuke::report_log`, `panic::report`, `joingate::trigger_log`) name their entry with `Log::entry` and `fact`/`var`. `d4_texts_tests` keeps each old builder beside the new one. `over` fills any unset placeholder with empty. A default over 10 lines (nuke report) may keep its facts up to its own length (`fact_places`). Rule: a security entry of facts never offers `{text}` (a test checks every one): `is_frame` takes any copy showing `{text}` as an old frame and skips `hides_facts` and every required fact, so `||{text}||` under a calm title would pass; the frame exception is only for copies saved before an entry had facts. NSFW and webhook spam logs are security entries in the ordinary automod group: a security log never takes a non-security group's frame (`framed_with` must not fall back to the `log.auto_mod` frame any Admin may edit). Old copies saved on those two entries before D4 lack the required facts, so the default is sent.
- **The security command panels could only be framed, and the whitelist menu and result skipped every edit.** `t!antinuke`, `t!ancaps`, `t!anpanic`, `t!antinukerestore`, `t!joinraid` built their rows in code; the whitelist menu and saved result were posted with `create_message` and the interaction answer, so not even `cmd.whitelist` reached them. *Avoid:* each panel is one entry of facts in `botmsg::PANELS` (`reply.panel.<command>`, group Security, so Owner/EO, 2FA and the audit line apply); whitelist answers are `reply.whitelist.prompt` and `reply.whitelist.saved`. Every value and row number is a required placeholder: the number is its own placeholder named as the code (`{3}` fills `[3]`, `{4a}` fills `[A]`), because people type it to change the setting; the on/off sign after Status (`{toggle}`) is required too. No `{text}` (D4 rule). A panel may put all rows in one list: `fact_places` counts as many lines as the default has in all. `settings::panel_vars` fills the entry from the same rows `panel` reads; `panel_texts_tests` keeps `PANELS` equal to `settings::maps` and each default equal to `panel`; a new row in a security panel needs its line in `PANELS`. The `cmd.<command>` frame still lies over the panel. A reply a command sends outside the reply helpers gets no edit: send every answer through an entry or `command_reply`. A security entry offers no placeholder whose value a server sets freely: `{prefix}` may be `||` or three backticks, and the save check reads the template before filling, so `{prefix}{status}{prefix}` would hide a fact; the footer gets the prefix after filling (`settings::with_prefix`). `limit` must drop the last fields until an embed fits Discord's 6000 total (it cut only the description, so an edited fields-only copy was refused and nothing posted); a security text then sends its default.
- **The other settings panels and the reply after a setting changed were still code text.** *Avoid:* `t!statics`, `t!heat`, `t!hfilters`, `t!joingate`, `t!verification`, `t!misc`, `t!raiderlist` are entries of facts in `botmsg::SETTINGS_PANELS` (group Settings panels, same rules as `PANELS`: every row number, value and on/off sign required); the changed reply ("✅ [7B] Name: value") is `reply.settings.changed` with `{row}` and `{value}` required. A security panel's setting (join raid), a setting only Owner and Extra Owners may change (misc, main roles) and the Trusted Admins line use the Security copy `reply.settings.changed_security` (`settings::changed_key`), so a Trusted Admin cannot reword the answer (misc must not get the general copy). The Security copy is sent without any `cmd.<command>` frame (`send_said`): the Trusted Admins line answers `t!statics`, and `cmd.statics` is not a security text, so a Trusted Admin could put the list in a spoiler there. A paged panel (`t!hfilters`) is filled whole, then cut into pages of its cards (`settings::page_of`); a page turn uses the same entry, never `panel`. The whole filled panel goes through `limit`, so a paged panel must stay under Discord's 6000 in all (the test compares every page with `panel` and fails if fields were dropped). The misc Prefix row is the one panel value a server sets freely (`{prefix}`); it is not a security text, so it stays.
- **The settings error replies were code text.** *Avoid:* `settings::edit_for`, `resolve` and `no_such_row` return a `Refusal` (entry plus values), never a sentence; `Refusal::say` sends the server's copy, `Refusal::text` is the default (tests compare it with the old text for every row). Entries (not a switch, not a list, not valid, out of range, takes one of, not a role/channel of this server, nothing found, ambiguous, no such row, "Not saved: ...", 2FA replies, main roles and Trusted Admins refusals, "Only the person who opened this panel can use it") are in `botmsg::SETTINGS_ERRORS` (each with a Security copy `<key>_security`, chosen by `settings::changed_key` as for the changed reply, or, when no row was found, by whether the panel has any such row) and `botmsg::SECURITY_ERRORS` (always Security). What went wrong is a key variable (`{row}`, `{typed}`, `{min}`/`{max}`, `{options}`, `{reason}`); no entry offers `{text}`. A Security copy is sent without the `cmd.<command>` frame (`send_said`). `settings::save` keeps its `String` refusal for other callers; the settings command uses `save_with`, whose "Not saved" is an entry. `needs_2fa` is one entry for settings, whitelist and backups replies (`needs_2fa_line` where the reply is only text). Bot-not-user refusals (`users_only::refuse`) are security entries, one per list.
- **A security refusal went through a command frame a Trusted Admin may edit.** The backups command's owner-only, 2FA and "not while locked or in panic" refusals and the revert command's owner refusals were plain strings sent through `cmd.backups` / `cmd.revert`: `||{text}||` plus a fake Confirm 2FA link would hide the real refusal from the owner. Root cause: the frame decision looked only at `Said::edited`, not at whether the text is a security text, and the slash path framed every answer. *Avoid:* such refusals are Security entries (`botmsg::SECURITY_ERRORS`), and `reply::Said::security` (set from the entry's group) keeps them out of every `cmd.<command>` frame: prefix `commands::say_said`, slash `run`'s `frame` flag (`slash::unframed`), settings `send_said`. A command answer that must not be framed returns a `Said`, never a `String`. The load-while-locked refusal is also checked in `backup_cmd` (`reply.backups.locked_load`); `backup::load`'s own string is for its other callers. Still open (needs a decision): backups load/delete/clear results and permit and panic-lock refusals (`gated`) use the command frame; the held-staff release refusal goes through the moderation result entry; `t!tshoot lockdown` and Raider List report owner-only refusals are ordinary entries.
- **The dashboard's Configuration Changed log had no Bot messages entry.** `api::log_change` posted it with `post_log`, which knows no entries; general command answers (help, dashboard, invite, config), invite link replies and report User Info embeds were also code. Root cause: the log is not a `LogKind`, so per-kind frames in `botmsg::logs` never covered it, and the D packages listed areas, not every sender. *Avoid:* a dashboard log goes through `discord::framed` with its own entry (`log.config_changes`) and `post_embed_with`; a command answer is an entry rendered with `reply::answer` or `reply::say`, never a `Log` or `format!` in the handler. A refusal helper returns an entry key and values (`invite_cmds::Reply`); tests render the default to compare with the old text. When adding a sender, check every `Log::new` and `post_log` in the bot and dashboard.

**A public endpoint takes GitHub and GitLab events from the whole internet.**
`POST /hooks/feed/<id>` needs no login. *Avoid:* only the address limit comes before the signature (a per-feed limit there lets anyone without the secret use up a feed's turns, and GitHub does not retry a refused delivery), the per-feed limit after it. Body stops at 1 MB; the signature is checked over the raw body before anything is parsed (GitHub: HMAC-SHA256 with `verify_slice`; GitLab: the token, compared in constant time). Unknown, paused or stopped feeds and a wrong or missing signature all answer a bare 404, so nobody learns which feeds exist. The secret is 32 random bytes, never in a list, log or error, shown once (add and "Make new secret", which kills the old one at once). A repeated delivery id, or the same signed body under a new id (the signature does not cover the id), is ignored for 7 days (`feed_events` unique keys). The bot posts queued events every 5 s, but a failure for now counts once a minute, so a short Discord outage does not stop the feed after 25 s. GitLab's confidential issues and notes never post. Event text is untrusted: the same checks as RSS items (scam, NSFW, the server's invite, link and word filters), and only the ping role is allowed as mention. GitHub's default webhook content type is a form: refused with a 400 saying to pick application/json, after the signature.

**A route read data that its page hides.**
The Appeal Blacklist page is hidden without the `appeals` permit perm (`access::page`), but its list route needed only Viewer, so any Viewer could read the user ids by the API. The page rule and the route check are written apart. *Avoid:* a route needs at least what its page needs (`api::staff` with the same permit perm), for every route of a hidden page. Test: `the_blacklist_needs_the_appeals_permit_perm`.

**The 2FA lockouts lived in memory.**
The 5 wrong codes in 10 minutes, the code replay guard, and the 5 tries in 10 minutes for a rescue claim and for Secure my account were kept in the dashboard process; a restart reset them and a just-used code worked again. *Avoid:* a security counter lives in the database: `dash_fails` (counted by `auth::tries` under an advisory lock, so parallel tries can not pass the limit) and `dash_totp.last_step` (migration 4058). Test: `totp_locks_after_five_misses_and_refuses_replay`.

**The dashboard had no script rule (Content-Security-Policy).**
Only `frame-ancestors` was set; the UI writes message previews and emoji as raw HTML, so one escape gap would run a script with the viewer's session. *Avoid:* every response gets `script-src 'self' 'wasm-unsafe-eval'` plus the hash of each inline script of the built `index.html` (`script_csp`, made at start). A server-made page (verify, raid report) gives its scripts a new nonce (`nonce_csp`) and has no inline event handler (`onclick=`): set the handler from the script. A new inline script in `index.html` needs nothing more; a new script host must go in the rule.

**The verify page's button stayed dead after a lost connection, and the raid page was wider than a phone.**
The verify script caught a bad answer but not a failed `fetch`: with no connection the button stayed disabled with no text. A 32-character name with no space ran out of the raid report page at 375 px. Server-made pages were tested only on the good path at desktop width. *Avoid:* a script on such a page wraps `fetch` in `try`, shows a message and frees the button; the page body has `overflow-wrap:anywhere`. Only the verify page and the raid report page are server-made with a script or a list.

**Logout left the `sid` cookie in the browser.**
`jar.remove(Cookie::from("sid"))` sends `sid=; Max-Age=0` with no path, so the browser applied it to `/auth` and kept the cookie set with `Path=/`. Same at the login callback for `oauth_state` and `login_next`, and at Secure my account. The session row was deleted, so no access remained. *Avoid:* a removal cookie must carry the same path as the cookie it removes: `jar.remove(auth::cookie(name, String::new(), st.secure))`, the same builder that set it; a test checks the `Set-Cookie` of a removal.
## 7. Build and deploy

**Every push to a PR branch ran the whole CI twice.**
`ci.yml` had `push:` with no branch filter plus `pull_request:`, so each commit on a PR branch started every job (check, supply-chain, dashboard-ui, e2e, about 20 minutes) once per trigger. *Avoid:* `push` is limited to `branches: [main]` (the Images workflow still follows main's CI run); the PR gets its four required checks from `pull_request` under the same job names. A new workflow with both triggers needs the same filter. A branch with no PR gets no CI.

**The updater trusted the digests in a queued row.**
Only the dashboard checked GitHub's signed build record before it queued an `update_runs` row, and the dashboard's database role can insert such a row. A dashboard or database break-in could make the updater (the one container with the Docker socket) install any image in the repository. Root cause: a check by the writer of a request was trusted by its reader. *Avoid:* the side that acts checks again itself: `updater.sh` `attested` asks GitHub for the record of each digest and matches image name, digest, repository, workflow and `main` in SQL (psql is the only JSON tool there; the answer goes in with `\set` from a file). It fails closed: no answer or no record installs nothing. `ATTEST_URL` exists only for the e2e test's stub. The updater image changes only with the server command, so a change to `updater.sh` needs that command (the Update page warns).

**The hardening block covered only the two services that hold the bot token.**
`x-hardening` (`cap_drop: [ALL]`, `no-new-privileges`) was added to bot and dashboard only, so the MCP server, Postgres and the tunnel kept Docker's default capabilities. *Avoid:* every new service gets `*hardening`. Postgres needs `CHOWN, DAC_OVERRIDE, FOWNER, SETGID, SETUID` back for its entrypoint (without `DAC_OVERRIDE` it cannot read its own data folder at start). The updater gets only `no-new-privileges`: its Docker socket is root on the host anyway, and dropping capabilities could break the dump into a `./pre-update` owned by another user. A change to `docker-compose.yml` reaches the server only with the server command.

**A compose secret from `.env` cannot go into a `read_only` container.**
Compose copies a secret with `environment:` into the container at start and refuses a read-only one ("`file` is the sole supported option"). A `file:` secret is a bind mount, which the updater cannot recreate: it runs compose with the project at `/project`, a path the host does not have. *Avoid:* the bot token is an `environment:` secret and the bot and dashboard are not `read_only` (they drop all capabilities instead). Try a compose change on a real Docker daemon (`dockerd` runs in the cloud container) before you write it into DEPLOY.md. A variable set in the shell wins over `.env`, also for secrets.

**An update could not be undone with its migrations, and a restore could half drop the database.**
The updater took no dump before the new image ran its migrations, so a rollback restored only the old images (not, for example, the column migration 3989 dropped). The DEPLOY.md restore piped into psql with no stop on error and no transaction, after the dump's DROPs. With a pinned tag the update's `up -d` kept the old container, whose restart count failed the health check. *Avoid:* the updater dumps with the server's own `pg_dump` (a newer pg_dump writes settings an older server refuses) into `./pre-update`, after the pull and the same-version check, so a run that changes nothing never rotates out the dump from before a migration. It stops before any restart when `/backups` is not a mount (new image, old compose file), when free space is under twice the database, or when the dump fails or passes its timeout (run inside the container, so it stops pg_dump itself). A restore checks the file (`gunzip -t`), drops and makes `public` again, and runs in `--single-transaction` with `ON_ERROR_STOP=1`; the bot starts only after it succeeded (`&&`). Every `up -d` the updater health checks uses `--force-recreate`.

**The Update page showed an older build as the newest.**
GitHub's `status=success` filter on the Images runs list left out a finished green run for over 15 minutes (the unfiltered list had it), so the page could neither show nor install the new build. *Avoid:* never trust GitHub's `status=` filter for something that must be current: list the runs unfiltered and check `conclusion == "success"` in our code (`update::runs_to_builds`, at most the 10 GHCR keeps). When the page is up to date on a commit the list does not have yet, "Newest" shows the running commit, never an older one.

**A failed cleanup of old image versions blocked every new image.**
The `delete-package-versions` step in each image job failed with "delete version API failed. Package not found" after it had deleted some old versions (most likely a child manifest GHCR had already removed with its index, left from builds before `provenance: false`). The updater job failed, the matrix cancelled the other three, `latest` was skipped, and the Update button had nothing new for hours. *Avoid:* housekeeping steps in the Images workflow run with `continue-on-error: true`; only build, push, attest and the `latest` move may fail a run. After a merge, check the Images run's jobs, not only that `latest` has not moved yet.

**The new updater's first heartbeat failed on the old schema.**
The updater waits only for Postgres to be healthy, not for the bot, which runs the migrations, so its first heartbeat wrote `updater_seen.sha` before migration 4014 added it (`ERROR: column "sha" ... does not exist`). *Avoid:* a container that does not run the migrations itself waits at start until the tables and columns it writes exist (`updater.sh` polls `select ... limit 0`); `tests/e2e/updater.sh` starts the updater before the schema and fails on any ERROR line.

**A Postgres healthcheck passed before Postgres was up.**
On a fresh volume the postgres image first runs a temporary server that listens only on its socket, runs the init scripts, then stops it and starts the real one. `pg_isready` over the socket passed on the temporary server, so the next `psql` hit the gap. *Avoid:* every Postgres healthcheck (CI services, `docker-compose.yml`, e2e compose files) uses `pg_isready -h 127.0.0.1`; the temporary server does not listen on TCP.

**The approved-build Update had four holes.**
(1) An attestation looked up by digest alone also matched the same bytes copied into another package (the updater's image under `tommybot-bot`). (2) A stopped MCP container (`ps -a`) was started by an update and failed its health check, so every update rolled back. (3) A build whose dashboard still queued untargeted runs could be installed, and then no update from the page worked again. (4) The Images `gate` used a job-level `if`, so a run whose jobs were all skipped (a failed CI, or a fork's pull request from a branch named main) could count as a successful build in the dashboard's list. *Avoid:* `update::attests` reads the in-toto statement: subject name `ghcr.io/thomkr01/tommybot-<img>` with the digest, and the workflow `images.yml` on `refs/heads/main` of this repository (under its old name `thomkr01/TommyBot` or new name `thomkr01/Brim`, exact strings: GitHub records the name as spelled at build time). The updater adds mcp only when it runs (`ps -q --status running`), and refuses a dashboard image without `org.brim.update=digest` or `org.tommybot.update=digest` (images carry both, since an updater from before the rename reads only the old one). Workflow conditions that decide what counts as published are steps that cancel the run, never a job-level `if`. The updater checks `target_sha` and digests by regex in SQL (Postgres `$` is end of string; `grep -x` matches any line). An old updater's heartbeat keeps a stale `sha`, so read it only with a fresh `sha_at`.

**Every move of main became an installable build.**
Each push to `main` built four images (about 35 minutes) and the Update page offered each one: many half-step versions, no named version. *Avoid:* only a release is built and offered: a tag `vMAJOR.MINOR.PATCH` on a commit of `main`. Keep these rules when you change it. (1) `images.yml` stays a `workflow_run` of CI (filter `branches: ['v[0-9]*']`; for a tag push the CI run's `head_branch` is the tag name). A `workflow_run` run always uses the file as it is on `main`, so its signed build record names `images.yml` on `refs/heads/main`, which `update::attests` and `updater.sh` ask for. A `push: tags` trigger would run the tagged commit's own copy, which a branch commit can change. (2) The gate checks, as steps that cancel the run: CI passed on a push of this repository by its owner, and its `e2e` job passed (e2e runs for tags, on request and on a push that changed the dashboard or the browser test; a skipped job counts as passed, so the gate reads the job's own conclusion) (write access alone could tag an old commit as the highest); the tag is `v` plus three numbers; the tag (peeled for an annotated tag) names the commit CI checked; and `compare/<sha>...<main's sha>` is `ahead` or `identical` (main's sha from `ls-remote refs/heads/main`, never the name `main`: a tag named `main` would win). (3) `latest` moves only when the release is higher (`sort -V`) than the version label of the current `:latest`, so a re-run or a late older release never moves it back; it is not compared with the tags, since a higher tag that never built would hold it back for good. It is copied from the `:<version>` tag, not `:<sha>`, which two releases on one commit share. The package cleanup keeps 15 versions, more than the 10 releases listed, since a re-run adds a version. (4) The release goes into the image as the label `org.opencontainers.image.version`, part of the signed digest; the updater refuses an image without a release label, so an attested build of `main` from before releases is not installed. (5) The dashboard lists only runs titled `Release <version> at <sha>`, sorted by version, and looks up digests by the version tag, since two releases on one commit share the sha tag. (6) `image-cache.yml` builds the release while CI runs on the tag, only to fill the build cache: no push, no login, no attestation, only `contents: read`. It checks the parts of the gate known before CI ends (owner, tag, on main), so the shared cache holds only the owner's code from main; Images still runs its whole gate before it publishes. An updater or Update page from before this does not know releases: the server command is needed once, after the first release is out.

**The Update button kept installing an older version.** (also: two image builds could finish out of order)
CI runs finish out of order, so the oldest commit's run finished last. The `images` concurrency group runs one at a time in the order CI *finished*, and a waiting run is replaced by the next one, so the old commit was built last and moved `:latest` back, and the Update button could install it on a database a newer migration had already changed. The Update page read the newest successful Images run's `head_sha`, which for a `workflow_run` run is main's tip when it was queued, not the commit it built: it showed the newest commit as published, and each update pulled the old image and said "Updated". *Avoid:* the Images `gate` job builds only when its commit is still main's tip, and otherwise cancels its own run (cancelled, not skipped, since a skipped job counts as success). Each image builds only its `:<sha>` tag, in its own concurrency group, so a newer commit's build cancels an older one. The `latest` job checks the tip again and then moves all four `:latest` tags together, in a group that is never cancelled, so the images never point at different commits. The run title is `Release <version> at <sha>`, and `update::published` reads the release and commit from it. The updater refuses a download that is the version already running. Never read the built commit from a `workflow_run` run's `head_sha`. An older commit's `:<sha>` tag may never be built; that is fine, since the updater's rollback uses its local `:rollback` tag.

**Images were pulled by a moving tag.**
`rust:1-bookworm`, `debian:bookworm-slim`, `docker:28-cli` and the backup script's `rclone/rclone` (`latest`) changed under the same name, so two builds or backups of one commit could run different code. *Avoid:* name an exact version that is at least two weeks old (`rclone/rclone:1.75.2`). Postgres keeps its major tag (`postgres:16`): an exact minor pin can be older than the one a server already runs, and the `-alpine` variant would change the collation library under the data volume. The rclone pin is moved by hand.

**Dependabot would offer a Postgres major upgrade.**
The docker-compose ecosystem proposes the newest `postgres` tag, so a merged bump plus `docker compose up -d` starts Postgres 18 on the version-16 data volume, which refuses to start. *Avoid:* the docker-compose entry ignores `postgres` `version-update:semver-major`; a major upgrade is a planned pg_upgrade.

**Images were published for commits that failed CI.**
`images.yml` ran on every push to main on its own, so a commit that failed clippy or tests became `:latest` and the Update page offered it. "Keep 3 versions" also counted the untagged attestation manifests, leaving fewer real builds to roll back to. *Avoid:* `images.yml` runs on `workflow_run` of CI on main and publishes only when that run was a push and succeeded, from its `head_sha`. It stays a separate workflow, and keeps its name, since deployed dashboards read `images.yml` runs (`update::LATEST_URL`). A job-level concurrency group on the matrix needs `${{ matrix.target }}` in its name: one shared group makes the 4 legs cancel each other. The package cleanup keeps 10 versions.

**CI ran unpinned upstream code with the default token scope.**
`pipx install graphifyy` and `npm i playwright` took the newest release on every run, and only `images.yml` set `permissions:`. *Avoid:* pin every installed tool to an exact version, and give each workflow `permissions: contents: read` (a job that pushes images asks for `packages: write` itself).

**The UI build took new dependency versions without a commit.**
The UI crate's `Cargo.lock` was gitignored and the Dockerfile ran `cargo generate-lockfile`, so a bad upstream patch release could change or break the dashboard. `cloudflared:latest` had the same problem. *Avoid:* `crates/dashboard/ui/Cargo.lock` is committed; the Dockerfile runs `cargo fetch --locked`, CI runs the UI checks `--locked`, and the release build is `--locked`. Third-party images in compose name a version. After a UI dependency change, commit the new lockfile.

**An update could stay "running" forever.**
`updater.sh` has no `set -e` (its loop must survive), so when Postgres was briefly unreachable while `finish` ran, the row stayed `running`, the dashboard polled forever and hid "Update now" until the updater restarted. *Avoid:* runs happen one at a time inside the loop, so the loop fails any row still `running` every 30 s with an error that says the end could not be recorded.

**The backup was world-readable and ran `.env` as shell code.**
`backup.sh` wrote the dump (2FA secrets, session tokens) with mode 644, sourced `.env` (a value with spaces, `&` or `$(` broke the script or ran code), left `*.tmp` files after a failed dump, and `rclone delete` removed every file older than 30 days on the remote, not only dumps. *Avoid:* `umask 077`; read only `BACKUP_DIR`, `RCLONE_REMOTE` and `RCLONE_CONFIG_DIR` from `.env` as text; a trap removes the temp file; the remote delete has `--include '*.sql.gz'`.

**GitHub Actions were pinned by tag.**
A `uses:` tag (`@v7`, `@stable`) can be moved by its owner to other code; `images.yml` can push the images the updater installs. *Avoid:* `uses:` names a full commit SHA with the tag as a comment (Dependabot updates both). `dtolnay/rust-toolchain` then needs `toolchain: stable`, since it reads the toolchain from its ref name.

**Docs and config said what the code does not do.**
Cargo.toml named a licence with no LICENSE file, compose did not pass `PHISHING_LIST_URL`, `NSFW_LIST_URL` and `NSFW_THREADS`, `METRICS_ADDR` did nothing under Docker, the CHANGELOG named Postgres 18 for the database, an unused workspace dependency stayed, and the invite feature carried the name of another Discord bot (now Invite Tracking). *Avoid:* a new env variable goes into `.env.example` and, if the container reads it, `docker-compose.yml`; check a feature name against existing bot products first.

**Trunk's prebuilt wasm-bindgen needs a newer glibc.**
On the arm64 VPS `trunk build` failed with `GLIBC_2.38 not found`. *Avoid:* the Dockerfile builds `wasm-bindgen-cli` itself at the version the UI lockfile resolves to. Don't hardcode the version: read it from the `wasm-bindgen` entry of `crates/dashboard/ui/Cargo.lock`, before the UI code is copied in, so the build cache keeps this step.

**containerd deletes an untagged image.**
Rollback by image id failed because the old image was already gone. *Avoid:* the updater keeps the previous version under a `:rollback` tag.

**A `git pull` in the old folder would start the bot on an empty database.**
With `name: brim` in docker-compose.yml, a server set up before the rename (`~/tommybot`, project `tommybot`) that pulls the new file gets new, empty volumes `brim_*`: the bot would start in every server with no settings, permits or whitelist. The Update page also told such a server to run `git pull`. *Avoid:* the compose file needs `BRIM_NAMES=1` in `.env` (`x-names`), so the old folder refuses to start (`git checkout HEAD@{1}` goes back); the bot and the dashboard also stop at start under the old names, so no Update page runs there. The move itself copies the volume and renames database and roles in the copy, so the old volumes stay as the way back.

**The updater does not update itself.**
*Avoid:* a change to `docker-compose.yml` or the updater needs `git pull && docker compose up -d` on the VPS once.

**No logs without `RUST_LOG=info`.**
The bot prints nothing useful at the default level. *Avoid:* `live.sh` and compose set it.

**A new RustSec advisory turns the `audit` job red.**
*Avoid:* fix with `cargo update -p <crate>`. A new dependency can also pull a crate back to an older, vulnerable version (tract 0.21 pinned `time` below 0.3.42): after adding a dependency, diff `Cargo.lock` for downgrades. Ignore an advisory in `.cargo/audit.toml` only with a reason, as for RUSTSEC-2023-0071 (rsa only via the unused sqlx-mysql).

**Kept image tags could not be pulled.**
Each build pushed an index plus an untagged attestation manifest (3 package versions), and the cleanup keeps the newest 10 versions by date, so it deleted the image manifest under a tag it kept: `tommybot-bot:0d89861` returned 404 for its image while the tag still showed. *Avoid:* `images.yml` builds with `provenance: false` (one manifest, one version per build), the signed provenance goes to GitHub's attestation store (`attest-build-provenance`, not pushed to the package), and `imagetools create --prefer-index=false` so `:latest` is the same manifest as the sha tag. Before relying on an old tag, pull it or HEAD its manifest, not only the tag list.

**The patched twilight-model was outside every check.**
`vendor/twilight-model` is a path crate, so Cargo.lock has no checksum for it and cargo audit skips it; nothing showed whether its bytes were still the upstream release plus our one change. *Avoid:* `scripts/check-vendor.sh` (CI supply-chain job) rebuilds it from the crates.io release in `vendor/twilight-model.lock` plus `vendor/twilight-model.patch` and fails on any other difference, and CI runs `cargo audit -f vendor/twilight-model.lock`. On a twilight upgrade, update both files. A cargo audit that ran only when Cargo files changed, in its own workflow, did not stop an image either: it is a CI job, and Images publish only after CI passed.

**DEPLOY.md named a menu that does not exist.**
It said **MCP** in the top bar; the link is **MCP tokens** in the account menu. *Avoid:* when a guide names a button or menu, check its label and place in `crates/dashboard/ui/src/main.rs` (header) or the page itself. After a rename, grep every .md, CHANGELOG and README included, for the old label (a rename missed "new MCP page" in CHANGELOG.md).

**A header was trusted behind any proxy.**
Web verification reads the visitor's IP from `CF-Connecting-IP`, which only Cloudflare's tunnel sets for us; behind another proxy, or with the port open, a visitor sends their own and skips the alt and VPN checks. *Avoid:* DEPLOY.md states that only Cloudflare Tunnel is supported for web verification. A new header-based check names the one deployment it trusts.

**A rollback reported success without checking the old version.**
After a failed update the updater restarted the old images and wrote "running again" without a health check, so an old binary crash-looping on a newer migration was reported as fine. *Avoid:* the rollback recreates the containers (`--force-recreate`, so the restart count starts at 0), waits on the same `settled` check as the update and reports `failed` when the old version does not start. `tests/e2e/updater.sh` covers it with a marker file standing in for the migration. The old version must also run on the newer schema and config: `db::connect` sets `set_ignore_missing(true)` (so migrations must stay additive: never drop or rename what an older binary reads), and a key or enum value an older binary does not know drops only that entry (`brim_logging::lenient::{map, list}` on anti-nuke `limits`, logging `routes`/`events`/custom `events`, Raider List `categories`, Discord AutoMod `bot_actions`), never the whole section (anti-nuke off). A section that still fails falls back to defaults and DMs the owner once a day (`reply::fallback_alert`). A DM alone was missed with DMs closed, while the dashboard kept showing the section on: the alert also posts to the config-changes (or fallback) log channel, read from the raw config since `logging` may be the failed section, only when @everyone cannot read that channel (`events::public_with`, the log path's check without a config read); it posts nothing when `lockdown` (the main roles that check needs) is itself unreadable, and only to a channel of that server. The dashboard names such sections (`api::unread_sections`) in a red Overview row, a banner on the page and an off Quick Systems switch. *Avoid:* every enum-keyed map or enum list in a config section uses the lenient readers; a single enum field still fails its section, which is then announced. Writes still refuse such an entry (`sections::unreadable_entry` in the dashboard's `validate` and the bot's `check_section`), so only data a newer version wrote is ever skipped. That check first looked at the whole section, and the UI sends the stored section back, so after a downgrade every save of a section holding a newer entry was refused; it also checked only keys, so a bad value under a known key (`limits.Ban.enabled = "no"`, a route that is no id) was saved and then silently dropped; and `lenient::map` over the AutoMod `actions` dropped a whole rule for one unknown action. *Avoid:* `unreadable_entry(section, new, stored)` checks keys and values, and refuses only an entry that is not stored as it is (the rule `limits::check` uses with `old`); list items count as stored when the stored list holds them. A map of lists uses `lenient::map_of_lists`, which drops one item, not the entry.

**A new migration was missing from the test binary.**
`sqlx::migrate!` embeds `crates/core/migrations` at compile time, and core had no build script, so after adding a migration an incremental build kept the old list and the tests ran without it. *Avoid:* `crates/core/build.rs` reruns on a change in `migrations`.

**Each release compiled every Rust library again.**
The builder stage started with `COPY . .`, so any code change threw away the layer with the compiled libraries, and each image build took 8 to 10 minutes. *Avoid:* the Dockerfile copies only the manifests, the lock file and `vendor/` first, builds the libraries against empty stand-in crates, and then deletes every `brim*` artifact before the real code is copied, so no stand-in output can reach an image. A new workspace crate needs its own `COPY crates/<name>/Cargo.toml` line and a stand-in in that step; without it the build stops with a manifest error.

**The early image build ran beside the real one and saved nothing.**
CI on the tag ended in 7 minutes, the Image cache builds in 8 to 11.5, so Images started while the cache was still empty and compiled everything again. *Avoid:* each Images build job first waits (at most 20 minutes, and only waits) for the Image cache build job of the same release and target to end. The gate decides before that step; the wait never changes what is published.
## 8. Tests

**Two tests waited 30 seconds on a database that never answers.**
`owner_gets_a_token_others_do_not` (MCP) and `icon_revert_clears_a_new_icon_and_refuses_a_bad_hash` (dashboard) use `connect_lazy("postgres://x@127.0.0.1:1/x")`. A path that reads the database waits for the sqlx default acquire timeout (30 s) before it fails. *Avoid:* a pool that must never connect gets `PgPoolOptions::new().acquire_timeout(200 ms)` (all four such pools: dashboard `main.rs`, `ops.rs`, `sections.rs`, MCP `tests/oauth.rs`).

**A role setup that never committed passed every local run.**
`set_mcp_password` wrote the password outside its transaction, with no commit, so the role was rolled back. Locally the role still existed from earlier runs, because roles belong to the whole Postgres server, not to the dropped test database. CI (new server each run) failed. *Avoid:* test role setup on a fresh Postgres server (`docker run postgres:16`), not only a fresh database. Run every statement of a transaction through `&mut *tx` up to `commit`.

**A DB test overflowed the 2 MB test thread stack.**
`verify_lifts_only_a_join_quarantine_and_admin_roles_need_staff` awaited `modcmds::run` many times inline. In a debug build its async frames took about 1.7 MB, and a small growth in the called code tipped it over; this aborts the whole test binary. It is not a recursion, and not the live bot (spawned tasks keep futures on the heap). *Avoid:* a test that awaits a big command future (`modcmds::run`, `on_audit`) in a helper or loop boxes it: `Box::pin(..).await`. Check with `RUST_MIN_STACK=1048576` when a test grows.

- **The e2e sweep read the sidebar links before they rendered.** `dashboard.js` waited for more than 10 `#/` links of any kind; the header's links met that before the sidebar's module links existed, so `links[0]` was undefined. *Avoid:* wait for the exact element the next line reads (here a `#/<id>/module/` link), never a count of something broader or a fixed sleep.
- **Two DB tests shared a guild id and one purged the other's rows.** A new test in `cases.rs` took `5_100_000_000 + pid`, the base `db.rs` uses for a `purge_guild` test, so its cases vanished mid-test about one run in four. The same for a shared helper offset: in `core/src/giveaways.rs` two tests passed `db(7)` and one deleted the other's giveaway. *Avoid:* before a DB test picks a guild id base, search the crate for that number; a test that passes alone and fails in the full run has a neighbour writing its rows. List offsets in use (`grep -o 'db([0-9]*)'`) before you take one.
- **Dashboard tests shared a guild id base and failed with a duplicate key.** `g3_handler_tests::setup(base)` uses `base + pid`; bases (`9_884`, `9_893`, `9_894`) were used by two or three tests of the same process, so parallel tests wrote the same `guild_config` row (`guild_config_pkey` violation). *Avoid:* a new `setup(base)` takes a base no other call uses; `grep -rhoE 'setup\(\s*[0-9_]{10,}' crates/dashboard/src | sort | uniq -d` must print nothing.
- **Two DB tests used the same guild id.** Both took `9_300_000_000 + pid`, ran at once in one process and saw each other's config versions, so `history_keeps_newest_200_versions` failed now and then (`6_100_000_000` was also used twice in the bot crate). *Avoid:* give each DB test its own base (`grep -rn '_000_000_000 +' crates/` before picking one) and delete its rows at the end.
- **Two test files gave `T::new` the same numbers.** `lifecycle_tests` and `quarantine_tests` both used 70-81; the number picks the guild ids, so parallel tests shared a guild and one `cleanup` could delete the other's config. *Avoid:* `T::new(n)` numbers are unique across all test files (h2 tests use 150-199; the base is `pid * 10_000 + n * 10`, so `n` stays below 1000); grep `T::new(` before picking one.
- **A changed MCP tool description reached the AI model unseen.** AI clients take new tool text without asking the user, and nothing in CI compared it. *Avoid:* `crates/mcp/tests/definitions.rs` pins the instructions and every tool definition in `definitions.json`; a change fails CI until the reviewed file is updated with `UPDATE_DEFINITIONS=1`.
- **A mock let a timeout succeed while member edits failed.** The timeout arm came before the `fail_member_edit` arm, so a test of a failed timeout passed against a mock kinder than Discord. *Avoid:* a failure flag arm comes before every arm for the same route.

**The DB tests skipped green and ran on any database.**
Without `DATABASE_URL` about 500 tests passed without running; with a production URL they deleted rows and changed the MCP role's password. *Avoid:* every DB test gets its URL from `db::test_url`: it panics in CI when the URL is missing, and refuses a database whose name does not end in `_test` unless `BRIM_TEST_DB=1` (CI sets it). `tests/e2e/run.sh` has the same check.

**Tests that could not fail.**
A native AutoMod test never approved its rule, so every hit stopped before the whitelist it tested; two tests asserted only "nothing happened"; a core test checked a drifted copy of antinuke SQL; the OAuth test allowed a refresh to move `exp`; two tests shared `T::new(601)`; one ordered two handlers with a 100 ms sleep. *Avoid:* an "it did not act" assert has a control in the same test that does act; never test a copy of production SQL; assert the exact value of a security limit; each `T::new` number is unique; wait for the event (a mock call), not a fixed time.

**The dashboard access matrix ran only by hand, and a wrong refusal only made a note.**
`deep.js`'s matrix ran with `DEEP=1` only, and a refused user who got 400/404/500 was a NOTE, so a route that validates before it checks access passed. *Avoid:* `run.sh` always runs `DEEP_ONLY=matrix`; a refused user must get 401 or 403, and a 5xx for an allowed user fails.

- **Defaults are off on new servers.** Heat filters and anti-nuke start off, so a live scenario must turn on what it tests.
- **Bots are exempt from a lot.** Webhooks owned by Brim or a verified bot are exempt, bot-authored commands are ignored and bots cannot press buttons. Coordinated spam, confirm buttons, appeals, reports, Join Gate and DM tickets need a second human account.
- **The live test must not use the hosted bot's token or server.** The cloud env had the VPS bot's `DISCORD_TOKEN` and its only guild as `TEST_GUILD_ID`, so `live.sh` and the VPS bot both react to every attack. *Avoid:* a separate test bot (own Discord application) on a separate test server, or stop the VPS bot (`docker compose stop bot`) for the run.
- **The live script shares the bot's token** and its rate limits. Cache channel and role lookups and wait longer instead of polling hard.
- **Make scenarios clean up after themselves** so they can run again (the old nuke scenario kicked the attacker bot every run).
- **Cloud env vars are read at session start.** A variable added during a session needs a new session.
- **An e2e step waited for any row, not the one it made.** The notes check waited for "a note is listed", which old notes already met; the reload then swapped the card under the fill, so Save sent the old text. *Avoid:* wait for the exact value the step created.
- **An e2e step waited for a dropdown, not its options.** The Logging Cancel check waited for the channel select, which shows only "none" until the channel list loads. *Avoid:* wait for the option the step will pick (`option:nth-child(2)`).
- **A mock was kinder than Discord, and a test passed only because of it.** The Ban Sync mock's ban PUT replaced the reason, but Discord keeps the first ban's reason (and writes no audit entry) when a banned user is banned again, so `a_synced_ban_replaces_a_shorter_local_temp_ban` passed. *Avoid:* when a live run shows Discord behaving differently, fix the mock first (the mock PUT, in `test_support.rs`, keeps an existing reason) and re-run the tests that touch it. The code fix is under "A second ban keeps the first ban's reason" (section 2).
- **A body-less DELETE was refused and a soft call hid it.** `live.py`'s `call` sends `Content-Type: application/json` on every request; `DELETE /guilds/{g}/bans/{u}` with no body got 400 (50109), and `soft=True` turned that into None, so cleanup left the user banned. *Avoid:* `call` sends `{}` on a DELETE without a body; a soft call is for an expected 404, not every 4xx.
- **`tests/e2e/run.sh` needs root Postgres, trunk and Playwright**; the gateway is not covered by the Discord mock.
- **A global cleanup in one test deleted another test's rows halfway through its setup.** `raiders::expire` deletes every old rejected or pending entry in the database. Test 55 created old entries first and their evidence after, so test 54's parallel `expire` could delete an entry before its evidence existed (count 2 instead of 1). *Avoid:* in a test that calls a job working on the whole table, create the rows such a parallel run could act on last, so every state it can see is complete.
- **Two tests ran the same global sweep at once and deadlocked.** `persondata::sweep_kept` finishes every done delete request in one transaction (cases, then reports, appeals and tickets, then cases again). The two datareq tests (978, 979) each called it and each held rows the other wanted (40P01). Production runs one sweep an hour, so only tests collided. *Avoid:* tests that call a job working on the whole table and holding locks across statements take turns on one static `tokio::sync::Mutex` (`datareq::tests::SWEEP`).
- **A filter lived in one caller, not in the shared lookup.** Roles Discord hands out were dropped only in `App::level`, so `permits::resolve` and `top_permit` called directly (enforce permits, Raider List, dehoist, mod commands, voice raid, the dashboard's permit lookup) still gave their permit. *Avoid:* put a rule about which roles count into `permits::top_permit` (here the `unpermittable_roles` table), which every reader goes through.
- **A "sent once" set shared the size-cap clear with the counts.** The owner DM count map was cleared at 10 000 entries, and with it the record of who was DMed, so a busy server could DM the owner again. The key also held the role set, so one actor giving other roles counted again. *Avoid:* keep "already done" apart from a cache that gets cleared, and key it by what the message is about (guild, actor).
- **A rate gate dropped the last event.** `on_rule_change` ignored a change less than 60 s after the last sync, so a quick second edit in Discord stayed. *Avoid:* a gate that drops events schedules one run at the window's end (`keyword_rule_sync` job, deduplicated per guild).
- **A compare folded more than Discord folds.** `rule_matches` lowercased regex patterns too, but Discord keeps their case, so a changed `(?i)` or letter case was not seen; repeats were not deduped. *Avoid:* fold only what Discord folds (keywords); sort and dedupe lists before comparing.
- **`false` passed for a known bot flag.** Callers that had the member passed `bot = false` to `permits::resolve`, so a bot still listed as TA got TA trust. *Avoid:* pass `member.user.bot` wherever the member is known; `false` only where only an id is known, with a comment.
- **A test row's level ignored what the call changes.** `deep.js` expected a Trusted Admin to roll back to History v1. v1 comes from `dashboard.js`, before `levels.sql` adds the TA and EO, so the rollback also changes both lists, and `api::rollback` needs an owner for the TA list and the Discord owner for Extra Owners (same rule as `put_permits`). The owner's rollback also wiped the TA, EO and permits for later phases. *Avoid:* a matrix row's level is the strictest rule the call touches with the fixture's data; a row that changes fixture data undoes it (the rollback row runs `levels.sql` again).
- **Numbers with no range at all.** `panic.executors`, `joinraid.active_ms` and `automod.heat.similarity_floor` took any value, so 999999999 executors or a similarity above 1 switched the feature off silently. *Avoid:* every number the bot acts on has a range in `core::limits` (`FIXED` or `float_for_path`, and `ranged_paths` for a new float); executors may be 0 (off). The bot clamps the similarity on read (`HeatCfg::clamped`), like heat max and decay. The heat weights (`heat::WEIGHTS`) go from 0 to the max heat in the same section (`limits::float_range`, clamped on read too; a negative weight cooled a spammer). A new weight goes in `WEIGHTS` and `weights_mut` (a test checks they match). A filter's "Action duration" (`filters.<key>.duration_secs`) is 0 (default) to 365 days (`FILTER_DURATION`).
- **A role carried a permit, so whoever handed out the role handed out the permit.** Manage Roles, a reaction-role bot or a level bot could give anyone a permit, and every guard (View Mod Stats) had to judge the giver. *Avoid:* a permit belongs to a Discord user id (`permit_members`, one permit per person); its roles are Brim's to give. `permit_sync` (GuildCreate, job `permit_sync` after a dashboard save) pages every member, and only after a complete paging: moves role holders to ids once per guild (`migrate_members`), forgets listed people who are gone, gives listed members their permit's roles and takes permit roles from everyone else, nobody punished (never managed, Discord-given or above-the-bot roles, never a quarantined member). Live, `guard_permit_roles` takes back a permit role given to anyone not listed, whoever gave it (Owner, EO and TA too), and `guard_permit_removal` gives a listed member's role back. A leaver loses permit, EO and TA (`permit_sync::on_leave`). Until a guild is migrated (`permits::members_migrated`) every lookup and guard reads the roles as before; new code that reads permits goes through `permits::top_permit`/`member_roles`, never `permit_roles` alone.
- **A permit by id outlived the quarantine and the leave.** When roles carried permits, a quarantine (strips roles) and a leave took the permit with them. By user id, a quarantined holder and a user no longer in the server still resolved to their permit in `top_permit`, `App::level` and the dashboard's `permit_perms`. *Avoid:* `permits::top_permit` gives no permit to a user with a `quarantines` row (both paths); `App::level` and `permit_perms` give nothing to a non-member (a fetch error stays an error, see "guild_member errors"), as `level_of` does for EO/TA; a dashboard save refuses a new permit member who is not in the server. When trust moves from a role to an id, list every way losing the role used to remove the trust and keep each one.
- **A missing key wiped data.** `write_permits` read a custom permit without `members` as "no members", so an old UI tab or older MCP client that saved a permit cleared everyone listed. *Avoid:* a new list field on a saved object keeps its stored value when the key is missing or `null`; only an explicit list, `[]` too, changes it.
- **A leaver's level was gone before the audit entry asked for it.** A kick or ban outside Brim is judged by the victim's level, but MemberRemove can run `permits::forget_member` before the MemberKick or MemberBanAdd audit entry comes in, and `App::level` gives a non-member nothing, so every kicked permit holder read as "no permit". *Avoid:* `permit_sync::on_leave` keeps the level a listed leaver held for 10 minutes (`permit_sync::left_level`, in memory) before it forgets them; `antinuke::guard_rank` reads that, else `permits::resolve` by id, and the live level only for a timeout. When code deletes trust on an event, check every later reader that judges the same event.
- **A loop breaker failed open.** After 5 reverts in 10 minutes for one (guild, actor, member), `fight_stopped` and `permit_fight_stopped` stopped every revert, so the bot that fought Brim won. *Avoid:* a breaker on a security revert slows it down (here at most one revert a minute, `FIGHT_SLOW`), never stops it; the alert stays once per window.
- **A leave and a later ban were read as one event.** `left_level` kept a leaver's level for 10 minutes, so a member who left by themselves and was banned by id later looked like a kicked or banned permit holder, and the actor was punished. *Avoid:* keep the leave time (wall clock) with the level; a kick or ban entry uses it only when the entry's snowflake time is not more than 5 seconds after the leave.
- **A bot-kept message took over messages staff posted.** `public::sync_post` edited every `ticket_panel_posts` row in #open-a-ticket, so a Publish (a chosen subset, or a menu) into that channel was rewritten to the bot's message on the next save, and with every panel off the old buttons stayed. *Avoid:* code that keeps its own message in sync touches only the rows it posted (`posted_by = app.me`), and removes its message when there is nothing left to show.
- **Two authenticator entries with one name.** The login 2FA and the rescue key QR both used the issuer "Brim", so the app showed two "Brim" entries; the owner typed the rescue code at step-up and got "code rejected" and then the lockout. *Avoid:* each secret a person scans gets its own issuer name ("Brim" and "Brim Rescue"), and the code prompt says which entry to use.
- **A new DM button answered after its REST work.** The member's ticket type menu in the DM saved the type, posted, logged and updated staff buttons, and only then answered Discord, so a slow API showed "This interaction failed". *Avoid:* every new button handler defers first and edits the answer at the end, also when it copies a flow (`staff_button` kindset) that already does.
- **A new text field's default reached servers that never chose it.** Giving `tickets.panel_text` the new default through serde would add that text on top of the own panels of every server whose config has no such key, including restored versions and imports. *Avoid:* a new field that changes what members see defaults to "nothing new" when missing; the new default is written together with the default it belongs to (`save_id` writes it with the default panel), and a migration moves only rows that are exactly the old default.
- **Security checks warned about a state the permit rules intend.** After permits went per Discord user ID, "Staff permit members" still warned when nobody was listed in the Admins or Moderators permit, but people are added only when needed. "Automod whitelist" read only the Whitelist page lists and told the owner to add "staff roles", although staff skip automod through their permit (Owner, EO and TA always; Admins and Moderators by the `automod` whitelist flag Auto Setup gives them). The wizard setup score had the same two points, and its notes said Discord permissions whitelist a member and left out the whitelisted permits. *Avoid:* when a trust rule changes, search the security score (`security.rs`), the wizard score and texts (`wizard.rs`) and the Overview links (`pages.rs`) for checks built on the old rule. A check about staff reads the permits, never the Whitelist page lists or roles.
- **`v["key"].as_array_mut` added `"key": null` to a body sent to Discord.** serde_json's `IndexMut` inserts a missing key as null, so filling variables in an embed without fields sent `"fields": null`. *Avoid:* read optional keys of JSON that leaves the process with `get_mut(..)`/`pointer_mut(..)`, never `v["key"]` on a `&mut`.
- **A value filled into a message pushed it past Discord's limits.** Variables and role mentions differ in length from their tokens, so a text that passed the save check could be refused at send time, and `{guild.icon}` with no icon was sent as an image link. *Avoid:* cut every filled text to its Discord limit after filling, cut member-chosen values to 100 characters, and leave out an image slot whose filled value is not an https link.
- **An edit in place sent the raw text.** The edit path of publish replaced the filled `content` with the saved text so it could clear old text, which posted `{guild.name}` as typed. *Avoid:* only default a missing key; never overwrite a filled body with the saved one.
- **Old copies answered from the newest version.** Menus on a copy posted before the Sent log (`mb:<embed>:<row>`) were answered from `embeds.published`, which every new Send overwrites. *Avoid:* a posted copy answers from its own stored body: by its send id, else by its Discord message id, and the newest version only when neither is stored.
- **One unreachable copy blocked the delete.** Delete removed up to 101 Discord copies inside an open transaction and stopped at the first 403, so earlier copies were gone, the message stayed, and every retry failed the same way. *Avoid:* commit the database change first, then remove the Discord side best effort and say what was left.
- **A Discord create in a request handler was not cancel-safe.** "+ New channel" ran Channel Setup's create inside the handler, so a closed tab could leave a made channel with no log line and the push lock held until rollback; a failing step after the create (its position) read as "not made" and a retry made a second channel. *Avoid:* run locked Discord work in `tokio::spawn` (like push); once the thing exists, later step failures are warnings.
- **A failed permission lookup greyed usable channels.** The channel picker used `member_roles(..).unwrap_or_default`, so a failed lookup of Brim's roles judged it by @everyone only and disabled channels it can post in. *Avoid:* when an input to a permission check is missing, skip the check (no reason shown); never compute it from a default.
- **A reply was written into the message opened after it.** `save` wrote `cur` after its awaits, so switching to another message mid-save put the first one's id and Sent state into the second. *Avoid:* an async reply checks that the same item is still open (an open counter) before it writes page state.
- **New migration not in the e2e build.** `sqlx::migrate!` embeds the folder at compile time and core has no build script, so an incremental build after adding a migration ran without it ("relation does not exist"). *Avoid:* touch `crates/core/src/db.rs` after adding a migration locally; CI builds clean.
- **A phase shipped without the plan's safety rules for it.** PR #229 went up without the plan's 4d.7 and 4d.8 rules (sends during panic, lockdown or a nuke case; no edits in log, ticket, verification or appeal channels; caps and banned categories for "+ New channel"), because the build read the phase line and not the safety sections. *Avoid:* before a phase PR, grep the plan's safety sections (4, 4b, 4c, 4d, 4e) for every route and feature the phase touches, and list each rule as built or deferred in the PR.
- **"Allow @everyone" let a filled-in name ping everyone.** `allowed_mentions.parse = ["everyone"]` applies to the text after variables are filled, so a server or display name containing "@everyone" pinged the whole server. *Avoid:* build pings only from what the author's own template contains; add "everyone" only when the template itself has @everyone or @here.
- **A rollback's save kept a permission stamp it did not earn.** `saved_level` said a Trusted Admin saved the message, but the previous image's save changes the text without touching that column, so an Editor's text could ping after the roll forward. *Avoid:* a stamp that grants something is valid only together with a marker the older image cannot keep in step (`saved_at = updated_at`); any write by older code then voids it.
- **A picture showed in the preview but not in Discord.** The browser loads a link that Discord's image proxy cannot (a site that answers Discord's fetch with 403), so the preview looked right and Discord silently showed nothing. The preview also drew the thumbnail top left (a `float` inside a flex column), not top right. *Avoid:* after a send or edit, read Discord's answer: an image slot with width 0 was not loaded, so say which slot and why. The preview uses the same layout classes as the editor (`.embed.th` + `.thumbslot`), never its own copy.
- **A picture Discord showed was reported as not loaded.** Discord answers a send before its image proxy has fetched a link it has not seen, so the answer had width 0 and the Sent log said "Discord could not load the big image" while Discord showed it seconds later (bot and webhook alike). *Avoid:* width 0 in the first answer is "not yet", not "failed": read the message again (after 2 s, then 3 s) before warning; keep the first answer only when it cannot be read back.
- **Two first sends made two webhooks in one channel.** The webhook was looked up, then made, with no lock, so two sends at once each made one and the row kept only the last. *Avoid:* "find or make" of an outside resource runs under a lock on its key (here `pg_advisory_xact_lock` per channel), and the made-again path names the stale id so a racing caller reuses the fresh one.
- **A gone webhook looked like a gone message.** Discord answers 404 for both "Unknown Webhook" (10015) and "Unknown Message" (10008). Treating every 404 as "copy deleted" hid live copies from the Sent log, and Delete left them up. *Avoid:* read Discord's error `code`, not only the status; delete a copy as the bot when its webhook is gone, and keep a webhook while copies it posted are still up.
- **Webhook messages lost their buttons.** Discord ignores `components` on webhook execute and edit unless the request has `with_components=true`. *Avoid:* every webhook execute and edit path sends `with_components=true`.
- **A global header layer overwrote a route's stricter header.** `SetResponseHeaderLayer::overriding` for Content-Security-Policy replaced the `sandbox` policy that `/media` sets. *Avoid:* global security headers use `if_not_present` when a route may set a stricter value of its own.
- **A webhook token in a logged URL.** A webhook path holds its token, and reqwest errors print the URL. *Avoid:* map request errors with `without_url` for any path that holds a secret.
- **Brim's own cleanups deleted its own Message Builder webhooks.** The webhook spam cleanup deletes webhooks made in the last 10 minutes, and a backup restore with "delete webhooks" deletes every webhook not in the image; both hit the Message Builder's webhooks, and nobody could edit the copies they posted. *Avoid:* a bulk delete of a Discord resource skips the ones Brim depends on (`msghooks::ours`). A restore that cannot read that list deletes none.
- **A record of "who did it" was lost to a race.** The dashboard marked copies as gone when Discord answered "Unknown Webhook" before the audit log entry came; the entry then found nothing to do and wrote no log. *Avoid:* when two paths can notice the same event, the later one fills in what it adds (here `hook_gone_by` and the log) instead of returning early on "already done".
- **Saving a schedule vouched for text nobody checked.** The first build stamped the message as saved by the Trusted Admin on every rule save, so after a rollback an Editor's text (saved by the older image) got back the pings and webhook a Trusted Admin had set. *Avoid:* a schedule that is turned on vouches only for the version the page showed (`seen` = `embeds::VERSION`, refused on a mismatch or unsaved changes) and never for a save by an older image (`saved_at <> updated_at`); a rule that is off vouches for nothing.
- **A far date crashed the next-run math.** `NaiveDate` parses a year like `+262142`, and `local + Duration` panics on overflow, reachable by any Viewer through the preview route. *Avoid:* limit typed dates to a sane range when parsing, and use `checked_add_signed` in time math on user input.
- **The shortest repeat held per line, not per rule.** Ten daily times one minute apart, or "every 15" plus "every 16", passed the 15-minute minimum. *Avoid:* check the whole rule (`msgsched::too_close` over its next 400 runs) at save; the runner also skips a clock run less than 15 minutes after the last one (`last_clock`).
- **A restore sent a schedule at once.** Relinking a remade channel set `next_at = now`, which matched no line, so the runner picked any recent "once" line and could send it a second time. *Avoid:* a rule switched back on gets its real next time (`msgsched::next_of`); a "once" run is matched loosely only while marked as waiting out a pause (`waiting_once`).
- **A send-time check done only at save.** The schedule's channel was checked against log, ticket, verification and appeal channels only when saved; a later config change made the runner post there. *Avoid:* a check that guards a delayed action runs again right before the action.
- **A trigger answered a message automod removed.** Trigger answers were queued before `automod::on_message` ran, and the dashboard woke at once (NOTIFY), so a spam message with a trigger word got an answer before automod deleted it. *Avoid:* the Message Builder hook runs after automod in `events.rs`; the dashboard checks the message, member, timeout and quarantine again before it answers; a reply uses `fail_if_not_exists: true`.
- **The Sent log trim dropped answers still waiting to be deleted.** Keeping the last 100 copies removed rows whose `delete_at` was still ahead, so "delete after" never deleted those answers. *Avoid:* a row with pending work (`delete_at`) is never trimmed; trim only finished rows.
- **Regex cost had no per-server total.** Each pattern had a size cap, but 50 rules of 10 patterns and 50 words, kept compiled for every server, let one server load the shared bot. *Avoid:* one `RegexSet` per rule with `size_limit` and `dfa_size_limit`, and a server total (30 patterns, 300 words) checked at save under the server lock.
- **A batched counter lost counts.** Entries were marked written before the write, and the size cap cleared counts that were never stored. *Avoid:* mark a batch as written only after the write succeeds and only if the value did not change meanwhile; on overflow drop only stored entries, and read a missing count back from the database before counting on.
- **A JSON body that is not an object panicked a handler.** `spec["channels"] = ..` on a user-sent `Value` panics when it is a number or a string. *Avoid:* use `as_object_mut` (or a typed struct) before writing into JSON from a request.
- **A trigger answer skipped the rollback check that schedules have.** Schedules refuse a message an older Brim saved after a rollback (no rights check there), but trigger answers sent it. *Avoid:* every automated send (schedule, trigger, sticky) calls `msgrules::trusted` before it sends.
- **A sticky's repost pinged again each time.** `send_copy` forced no pings only for trigger answers, so a sticky with @everyone pinged on every repost. *Avoid:* every send that repeats by itself without a person pressing Send (trigger answers, sticky reposts) uses `allowed_mentions: {"parse": []}`.
- **A sticky's own new webhook counted as another bot.** The bot's cached list of Brim webhooks did not know a webhook made by the first send, so the copy it posted moved the sticky down again, in a loop, until the bot streak paused it. *Avoid:* a cache that decides "is this our own post" falls back to the database for an id it does not know (`message_webhooks`), and Brim's own user id never counts.
- **Two reposts of one channel mixed the sticky order.** Two dashboards during an update could repost one channel at once. *Avoid:* a repost holds `pg_try_advisory_lock` per channel on its own connection (released at the end, or with the connection if the process dies); a run that finds it taken goes back in the queue. A run caused only by bots reposts from the first sticky that follows bots and every one after it, so the order holds.
- **The webhook-deleted log gave one advice for every copy.** The "Message Builder Webhook Deleted" log said "send the message again, then delete the old copy in Discord" for all copies, but a sticky copy is replaced at its next repost and a trigger answer with a delete time is still deleted on time. *Avoid:* advice in a log or notice matches what happens to each kind of copy (kept, timed, sticky); check every source of `message_sends` when one text covers them all. A trigger answer whose timed delete failed was forgotten too: the claim clears `delete_at` first, so a failed delete writes a "delete it by hand" warning on its Sent log row. A sticky copy counts as sticky only while a sticky still holds it (`sticky_msgs.send_id`).
- **A sticky copy that could not be deleted was forgotten.** Moving or switching off a sticky cleared its stored copy id before the delete, so a failed delete left a copy nobody tracked. *Avoid:* a failed delete of a copy Brim posted is shown on the sticky (or in the answer) with "delete it by hand"; Brim only deletes copies by the id it stored.
- **A stored id was trusted at use because it was checked at save.** Log channels, the appeal channel, the dashboard's Lockdown Update post, the verification channel (anti-nuke invite back, verification retry invite), ticket categories and verification roles were checked to be in the server only when saved. An id saved before those checks (or written by another path) could post one server's logs or appeal answers into another server, make an invite there, or count another server's role as safe. *Avoid:* every send, invite, parent or role grant that uses a stored id checks at use that the object is in the same server: bot logs through `events::send` (error, no owner DM); dashboard logs through `Discord::post_log_with` (`private_channel_of`, which like the bot also refuses a channel a main role can read); invites and verification roles against `guild_info`; categories by the fetched channel's `guild_id`. A role or channel missing from the server's lists is not used.
