- **"The last server used" sent DMs to the wrong server's staff.** With confirmation off, a DM ticket message went to the last server the user wrote to, so a message with files meant for another server landed in the wrong ticket. *Avoid:* a DM goes straight on only when the user shares exactly one ticket server with Brim; with several, they pick the server for each message (`tickets::dm`). `ticket_users.last_guild` is no longer read or written.
- **A refused Member write left a channel hidden, and Overview then called it private.** The standard set wrote each main role's View, then @everyone's deny, and counted a refused write but went on. A channel whose Member write failed (429 or missing permission) lost @everyone's View with no main role let in, which `standard_overwrites` reads as private: Overview and the Fix skipped it, while members could not see it. *Avoid:* a deny that hides a channel from @everyone comes last and is skipped when any allow before it on that channel failed (as `info_access` does); test it with a refused role write.
- **A 429 retry waited too short and too long at once.** `Discord::send` clamped each Retry-After to 10 s and tried 3 times, so a longer limit failed after 30 s anyway, and the Overview reload after a Fix hung, then showed a 500 with the old list. *Avoid:* a retry waits the time Discord asks for, within a total budget (15 s); a longer wait returns the 429 at once, so the page says "try again in a minute". A best effort step (role order, catch channel) puts its failure in the step result, not only in the log, and keeps Discord's own reason for statuses that do not say it (a 400).
- **Auto Setup lost its "running" line.** The simpler Auto Setup page dropped the `<Working />` line, so a one-minute run showed nothing until the result. *Avoid:* every action that can take more than a few seconds shows the shared `Working` indicator while it runs.
- **A channel hidden from every member read as "private on purpose", so Overview and the Fix left it hidden.** Before #303 a run could write @everyone's View deny after a main role write failed (429). `standard_overwrites` called any channel with @everyone denied and no main role let in private, so the half-done channel never showed in Overview and no Fix or Auto Setup run repaired it; Administrator roles (bots) in Discord's access list made it look like someone was let in. *Avoid (owner rule):* a channel is public unless Channel Setup limited it or it is one of Brim's own layouts. Private means: @everyone denied, no main role let in, AND some overwrite other than a bot (managed) role allows View. Channel Setup's limited channels deny the main roles and always let the Brim member in (also on a channel that was hidden already, or a deleted staff role would leave it looking half done); the Logs, Staff and Staff Chat categories let the Brim member in; a jail lets the quarantine role in; logs, verification, catch, Information and tickets are skipped by id. Read only real overwrites, never Discord's access list (Administrator shows there). `private_overwrites`, `is_public`, `private_channel_of` and the lockdown "private" flag mean "hidden from @everyone" for log channels and display only; they stay as they are.

**The domain blocklists held one string per domain and grew with every refresh.**
`SiteList` kept about 1.14 million `String`s in a `HashSet`: about 100 MB, and each 12-hour refresh left freed fragments in glibc's heap (102 -> 178 -> 181 -> 213 MB over refreshes). Even a compact block grew to 110 MB without a trim, because glibc raises its mmap threshold after the first big free and keeps big parse buffers in the heap. *Avoid:* a list of this size is one compact structure (`fst::Set`, built from sorted unique keys on `spawn_blocking`), never a collection of small allocations; a periodic reload that frees big buffers calls `malloc_trim(0)` after the swap (`mem::release_freed_memory`). Not an allocator swap (mimalloc grew more). Numbers: tommybot-data `bot-memory-2026-11-18/methods.md`.

- **Channel Setup called every members' channel "Staff only".** `access_of` read "@everyone denied View" as limited, but with verification every members' channel denies @everyone and lets the main role in, so #general, the text and voice categories and the ticket category showed "Staff only" and the panel offered "Only these roles". Editing such a channel would have hidden it from members; picking "Everyone" also cleared @everyone's View deny, which let unverified members in. *Avoid:* limited means @everyone denied AND no main role let in (as `private_overwrites`); open with a main role is the standard set (main roles in, @everyone out). The row badge comes from the server (`tag_of`): none for a members' channel, "Unverified" for the verification channel, "Staff" for a staff-only one (the Owner and Trusted Admin level roles count as staff), a custom role's name, "Members read only" where the main roles cannot send (Information).
- **A loading message blinked instead of using `Working`.** The Update page's queued note had no moving indicator. *Avoid:* a loading message or status line uses the shared `Working` component; only an item that is itself loading (placeholder bar, running step) may pulse, through the one `.skel, .checks li.run` rule in `style.css`.
- **Loading dots blinked or froze with system animations off.** `.working` swapped the glide for an opacity pulse under reduced motion. *Avoid:* `Working` keeps its normal movement under reduced motion; only that component is exempt from the global reduced-motion rule.
- **Reduced-motion rules.** Animations are always on. There is no motion switch and no `data-motion`; a new rule must not stop animations for any setting. Only the hover lift may be removed.
- **The Wizard restarted at step 1 after a visit to another page.** The step and the Auto Setup picks lived only in signals of the open page. *Avoid:* state a person builds up over several steps is kept per server in `localStorage` (`wizard:<guild>`), cleared at the last step; never put a secret there. Unsaved edits inside a step's own cards are still lost.
- **The Update page's time left and bar only moved with the server answers.** Both were worked out from the server's whole-second step time once per poll, so a slow answer froze them and the native `<progress>` jumped. *Avoid:* a running timer counts on from the last answer with the page's own clock (250 ms tick) and the bar is a `.pbar` with a 1 s linear width transition.
- **Built-in DMs were plain text built in code, outside the Message Builder.** Each DM was a `format!` string sent as content, so nobody could restyle or reword it. *Avoid:* every built-in message has a key and a default template in `logging/src/botmsg.rs` (Message Builder format, with the variables it may use); code sends it through `reply::dm_msg` (bot) or `Discord::dm` (dashboard) with `(variable, value)` pairs, never a `format!` as the whole message. A server's saved copy (`bot_messages`) wins unless the template is `locked` (2FA code, rescue key, anti-nuke and panic alerts, invite-back): a locked key never reads a stored copy. An unknown key panics, so a test scans the source for `"dm.` keys. An optional line (Moderator, Duration) is a template line with only its variable: a line whose variables are all empty is dropped. Embeds from the builder carry no `type`; `reply::parts` adds `rich`. Mentions in an embed do not ping; a DM already notifies its receiver. Left on their own embeds for now: ticket relay and closed DMs, captcha DM, welcome DM to the adder, note card.

**The image-scan model held 103 MB, most of it freed heap and a second copy of its own bytes.**
Loading the model left ~37 MB of freed buffers in glibc's heap, each scan ~20 MB more, and the 24 MB `include_bytes!` copy stayed resident although tract keeps its own weights. Measured 103 -> 43 MB with both released. *Avoid:* a one-off big allocation (model load, a scan, a list refresh) is followed by `mem::release_freed_memory`; embedded data copied at startup is released with `mem::drop_file_pages` (it discards clean file pages only; a later read reloads them; tests: `mem::tests`, `nsfw::tests::bundled_model_is_the_pinned_one...`). Not an allocator swap (mimalloc 133 MB, jemalloc 98 MB), not unload-when-idle (26 MB more for ~150 ms on the next image), not a quantised or f16 model (8x slower, or does not run). Numbers: tommybot-data `optimise-2026-11-18/results.md`.

**The QR check decoded a reposted image again every time.**
A 12 MP photo costs ~250 ms and ~100 MB of transient memory to decode; a spam flood of the same file paid it per post. *Avoid:* a result cache for pure per-file work is keyed by the SHA-256 of the bytes (never a fast hash: a crafted collision would let a scam image reuse a clean image's result), holds only results that depend on the bytes alone (the QR decode reads nothing from the server's settings, so it is valid for every server), and is bounded in count and age (`qr::CODES`: 2,000 entries, 1 hour, 16 KB of text per entry; tests: `qr::tests`). The NSFW scan results already follow this (`Scanner::cache`).

**The updater image was the whole Postgres image, and Postgres ran with 100-connection defaults.**
The updater only needs `psql`: its image was 594 MB (amd64) instead of 183 MB. Postgres had `shared_buffers` 128 MB and `max_connections` 100 for a small database. *Avoid:* the updater copies `psql` and its libraries out of the pinned Postgres image onto plain Alpine of the same release (no package install; the `pgclient` stage in `Dockerfile`); a new `psql` library dependency shows as `not found` in `ldd /usr/local/bin/psql` inside the image, so check that after a Postgres image bump. `max_connections=40` in `docker-compose.yml` must stay above the worst case of all pools plus listeners (bot 10+2, dashboard 10+2, MCP 3, updater, dumps, admin about 33): add a new pool to the comment and the number when a service or a pool grows, or connections wait for the 30 s acquire timeout.

- **A deleted catch channel stayed set, and every save logged a failed warning post.** Nothing noticed a catch channel deleted in Discord: no Overview row and no fix, and `catch::post_notice` (run after each save) logged "404 Unknown Channel" each time. *Avoid:* a stored channel Brim depends on gets an Overview row when it is gone (`catch_gone_check`), with a fix that makes it again in the shape Auto Setup makes (`sections::make_catch`, shared with the catch setup button); background posts into a gone channel return quietly and leave the telling to that row.
- **A colour set by hand must survive Auto Setup.** Auto Setup and the Member role fix give the Member role green (`MEMBER_COLOUR`) when they make it, take one over, or find an earlier colourless one named Member (or the Custom name). *Avoid:* only a role without a colour (0) gets one; a main role with another name keeps its look. The staff roles are Brim's layout and always get their colours.
- **A restore brought back deleted servers and revoked grants, and dumps left the server readable.** A restore replaced the whole database with the dump, so every server purged and every Extra Owner, Trusted Admin, permit holder, rescue key, dashboard 2FA and MCP token removed after the dump came back; the off-site copy was a plain gzip; its rclone image was pinned by tag only; the same rclone key could delete every off-site copy. *Avoid:* a grant or server removal leaves an `erasure_log` line (migration 4035, deferred constraint triggers, so a delete-and-insert in one transaction such as a permits save logs nothing), and `scripts/restore.sh` carries the live log into the restored database and calls `restore_erasures`. A new table that holds a grant gets the trigger in that migration's list. Every daily dump, the 7 local copies too (the updater's `pre-update` dumps are not), is age-encrypted to `BACKUP_AGE_RECIPIENT` and the private key never sits on the server, so anything unattended that reads a dump (the monthly restore test) runs on the plain dump inside `backup.sh` before it is encrypted; the backup key has no delete right and the bucket's object lock keeps copies 30 days; images that scripts run come from `docker-compose.yml`, pinned by digest when they get a credential, so Dependabot updates them.
- **The backup scripts were not executable.** `backup.sh` and `restore-test.sh` had mode 644, so the crontab lines in DEPLOY.md (`~/tommybot/scripts/backup.sh`) failed with "Permission denied" and no dump was made. *Avoid:* a script DEPLOY.md runs by its path is committed with mode 755 (`git ls-files -s scripts` shows 100755).
- **The restore test left a plain copy of the database behind, and leaked rows into its log.** `restore-test.sh` removed its Postgres container with `docker rm -f`, but the image's data folder is an anonymous volume, so every run left the restored database (2FA secrets, session tokens, deleted data) unencrypted under `/var/lib/docker/volumes`. Its psql errors went to the log with DETAIL and CONTEXT lines, which quote rows. A typo in `BACKUP_AGE_RECIPIENT` made `age` fail inside the dump pipe, so no copy at all was made. *Avoid:* a throwaway container that holds data is removed with `docker rm -fv`; a log gets only psql's ERROR line; a key is checked (`age -r KEY < /dev/null`) before the dump, and a bad key falls back to the plain copy with a message.
- **A rename of variables missed a check on the old name's prefix.** The Message Builder read the server from Discord only when a used variable began with `server.`; after `{server.name}` became `{guild.name}`, no send filled it and members saw the raw text. *Avoid:* such a check is one function beside the names (`msgvars::needs_guild`), never a copied string; a rename greps for the old prefix as a string (`"server."`), not only the whole names. Variable names follow `botmsg::public_name` (Discord things keep Discord's API names, Brim's own are `function_name`); code passes values by code name, and `to_code`/`to_public` convert at the edge.
- **The code kept old Discord names behind a name map.** The first rename changed only the names members see: defaults still said `{server}`, code passed `("server", ..)`, and `public_name` mapped it to `{guild.name}`, so a reader took the old name for a missed rename. A hidden spot also matched the old name (the dashboard examples gave the real server name to `"server" | "name"`). *Avoid:* a Discord thing has Discord's name in the code too (`guild.name`, `channel.name`); only old copies on load are mapped (`OLD_DISCORD_NAMES`). A rename greps for the old name as a match arm or a list item (`"server" |`, `&["server"]`), not only as `{server}`.
- **A bot message copy could keep a Message Builder variable that it never fills.** `botmsg::unknown_vars` only counted `{a_z}` names, so a saved copy with `{guild.name}` or `{role:1}` passed the save check, and the member got the raw text: bot message templates fill only their own variables. *Avoid:* a check that refuses unknown variables reads every `{...}` name the senders could treat as a variable (letters, digits, `_`, `.`, `:`); a test holds one of each kind. A built-in message also cannot hold buttons (code adds them), an embed time or `<t:+N>` (only the Message Builder sender fills those): `dashboard/src/botmsgs.rs` `problem` refuses them.
- **Two tests in one binary used the same test server id.** Test ids are `<base> + std::process::id`, so two tests with the same base get the same id, and in a parallel run both insert `guild_config` version 1 (`duplicate key`, a random red check). Nine bases were used twice (dashboard `api.rs` 9_884_000_000, `mcp.rs`/`api.rs`, `ops.rs`/`api.rs`, `embeds.rs`/`update.rs`, `mcp.rs`/`channels.rs`; bot `lockdown.rs`/`tickets`, `antinuke.rs`/`slowmode.rs`, `antinuke.rs`/`joingate.rs`; core `sections.rs`/`tests/db.rs`). *Avoid:* grep a new base first (`grep -rn "<base>" crates`) and use it once per crate.
- **A bot message copy with an empty footer or author made Discord refuse the whole message.** `botmsg::render` dropped an all-empty embed and an empty description, but kept `{"footer": {"text": ""}}` or an author with no name. Discord answers 400 and the DM or ticket message is lost; the ticket footer code also saw "a footer" and left out the server footer. *Avoid:* after filling, `render` removes every embed part whose text is empty (description, footer, author); `render` is the only place that fills these templates (test: `tickets::msgs::tests`).
- **A command word can mean two commands, so a reply frame found by word alone hit the wrong one.** `r` is unquarantine for moderators and reply in a ticket; `c` is purge and close. Ticket aliases are not in `general::CMDS`, so the lookup fell to the moderation aliases and a ticket error was framed as `cmd.purge`. *Avoid:* `reply::command_key` takes the sender's family (`mod_first`): moderation senders use `prefix::alias`, ticket senders `ticket_alias`, general senders `general::lookup`; a test holds `r`/`c` for both. A frame goes on the first embed only. A reply over 5500 characters in total is sent as built (Discord counts all embeds together). A result that edits an earlier message (lock) must be framed too. Confirm questions stay unframed, so a frame without `{text}` cannot hide what is being confirmed. Check every `create_message`/`update_response` that answers a command.
- **A log group had one editable frame, so a server could not word one log type alone.** `log.<group>` framed every log of the group. Owner rule: one entry per log type, as for command replies. *Avoid:* a log type with a fixed title has its own entry `log.<group>.<title slug>` (`botmsg::LOG_TYPES`). `events::framed` takes the group key and finds the type by the embed's title: the type's saved copy wins, else the group's (`log.<group>`, where all older edits sit, so no migration), else the log as built. A log whose title holds a name or number stays on the group entry. A new log type is one title in `LOG_TYPES` (its group's list); a test checks the title is really sent (an audit title from its `LogEvent`, anything else as a literal in the source), so a typo cannot leave a dead entry. The group must be the kind the code sends that title with. Security groups (anti-nuke, panic, join raid) have no types, only the locked group entry. Kits and backups copy `bot_messages` by key and need no change. Callers of `framed`: `send`, `send_private`, `reports`.
- **A push that made roles read the quarantine role as above the bot.** The Roles tab renumbers every role after the draft, new ones included, but the safety check used Brim's live position, so with one new role the quarantine role right below the bot read as above it and every such push was refused. *Avoid:* check a renumbered role list with the bot position read from that same list (`roles::bot_top`), never `Live::bot_pos`.
- **Settings the score only warns about passed the "serious problem" check.** Verification level and content filter are `Warn` rows, and `layout::blocking` keeps only `Bad` ones, so a push could set both to off and save that as the correct layout. *Avoid:* compare a change to a guarded setting with live on its own (`roles::weaker`); a `Bad`-only filter is not a safety check for everything.
- **A role edit during a lock gave Send Messages back.** The Roles tab may set Send Messages; during a lock or panic mode that is the lock's, and Brim's own audit entries are not put back. *Avoid:* any editor that writes Send (channel overwrites or role permissions) reads `Ctx.locked` first (`roles::editable`).
- **A handler that awaited a whole mod command overflowed the test stack.** `undo::handle` awaited `modcmds::run` inline; that future is large, a test thread has 2 MB, and the test died with "stack overflow" instead of a failed assert. The mock also answered 404 to every interaction reply, so a handler that returns its error stopped before it acted. *Avoid:* a handler in the interaction chain boxes a large command future (`Box::pin(modcmds::run(..))`), which also keeps the gateway event future small. The mock in `test_support` takes interaction answers, follow-ups and edits.
- **An e2e step that fills a form right after `goto` can lose the fill.** The page rebuilds its inputs when its data arrives, so the user ID was empty when Add note was pressed and `POST /notes` answered 400 ("user ID must be a number"); it failed on some PRs only. *Avoid:* wait for `networkidle` after `goto` before filling, as for the dropdown pick above.
- **A per-command setting must hold at every way into the command.** Ways in: prefix form with aliases (some only in `prefix::alias`, such as `slock`), short ticket words (`r`, `c`, `s`), a slash form whose subcommand can be another command (`/ban remove` is unban; the Commands page listed it under ban, so unban had no slash form), right-click entries (Kick, "Purge 100 From Here"). A gate on one of them lets "who may use it" or "off" be passed through another. *Avoid:* `cmdcfg::prefix` (in `events`, before every prefix handler) and `cmdcfg::slash` (in `slash::handle`, before `run`) resolve every word and menu to its catalog command with `cmdcfg::canonical`; a new way into a command goes through one of the two. The gate only refuses; the command's own permit check still runs after it. Safety commands (`core::cmdcfg::SAFETY`) ignore off, narrowing and cooldown at save (`problem`) and at read (`of`), so a rollback or import cannot lock staff out. A server alias never replaces a built-in word (`cmdcfg::RESERVED`, which holds every word `commands::KNOWN` dispatches; `verifysetup` is in no catalog row). A sibling word that runs another command (`hackban`, `warns`, `cases modify`, `info -server`) also answers to that command's settings (`cmdcfg::also`). A button that runs a command (Warn, Timeout, Kick, Ban on a report; Ban, Kick, Quarantine on a Raider List alert; the undo buttons under a case) calls `cmdcfg::button`. `whitelist` in a ticket is unblock. A new word, sub-form or button that runs a command gets a row in one of these. The cooldown map stops taking entries when full of running cooldowns, so members without a permit cannot grow it.
- **A test with many calls of one large async function overflowed the stack.** `modcmds::run` holds every command's state in one future; two new arms made its debug frame large enough that an old test overflowed, and a new test with 11 call sites held 11 copies. *Avoid:* a new arm of a large `match` in an async fn goes into its own async fn called with `Box::pin`; a test that calls such a function many times calls it through one closure that returns `Box::pin(async move { .. })`.
- **The browser test ran into the dashboard's own request limit.** A session gets 40 API requests at once and 20 more each second (`auth.rs` BURST, RATE). `tests/e2e/dashboard.js` opens pages back to back; the case page steps added about 25 requests in under a second (each page load and change does `/config`, `/access`, the case and a member lookup), so on a fast run the next page (Logging) got 429 for `/config` and `/schema`, showed no channel dropdown and timed out. The page itself was not over the limit for a person, but it asked again for the name of a member who left, after every change. *Avoid:* the test counts its own API requests and `page.goto` waits exactly until the limit has room for a page load (no fixed sleep; the limit stays); new steps need nothing extra. A page that reloads data after a change does not repeat lookups that gave no answer (`asked` in `CasePage`).
- **A log built outside the logger skipped the event route and the ignore lists.** `events::send_log` looks the channel up by kind only (`target(kind, None)`) and never reads `ignored_users` or `ignored_roles`, so a log of an event (the timeout end) went to the kind's channel instead of the event's, and an ignored member was still logged. The dashboard cannot send a framed log at all (its `post_log` knows no Bot messages entries), so a dashboard log (case delete) would not be editable. *Avoid:* a bot log of one `LogEvent` goes through `events::send_event_log` (event route, custom types, ignore lists, frame); a dashboard or sweep log is queued as a job (`case_delete_log`, `old_cases_log`, like `case_log`) and sent by the bot with `send_log`. A new log type is one title in `botmsg::LOG_TYPES` that the code really sends (a test checks it); a new kind is also in `LOGS`, `LogKind::ignorable` (security-type kinds false) and the dashboard's `LOG_KINDS`. twilight reads only the audit change keys it knows, so AutoMod rule and app permission details arrive as `other` and the log says some settings are not shown. Check every `send_log*` caller and every `LogKind` match.
- **A noisy log type took the kind route or the general channel, so a vote or sign-up flooded a channel nobody chose for it.** `LogConfig::target` falls back from event to kind route to the general channel; that is wrong for poll votes and event sign-ups (one vote is one message), and Auto Setup gave every event the shared `discord-logs` channel. *Avoid:* list an event that must stay off until a person picks a channel in `LogEvent::quiet`, not in a `matches!` of its own; `target` then uses only its own event route or a custom type, Auto Setup (`sections::log_channels`) skips it, and the logger builds nothing for it without a route (`Logger::vote`, `Logger::signup`). Same package: (1) state that decides whether a gateway event is news (boost level, open threads, published messages, ended polls) lives in the `Logger`, in memory and bounded (`MAX_KEPT`, `MAX_THREADS`, `first_time`); the first sight of a guild only records it (`Logger::seed` at `GuildCreate`), so a restart logs nothing false. (2) A thread archive is held for `HOLD` and dropped when an audit `ThreadUpdate` for the same thread arrived within it; only an archive with no audit entry is Discord's own. (3) A stage audit entry's target is the stage instance, so `render::audit` leaves out the Target line and the Channel line names the stage. (4) An update of a message with no cached copy is never read as a publish (it would hide the edit log), so the cache also runs while only the publish log is on. (5) A bounded map evicts its oldest entry when full (a "stop inserting" cap leaves new scheduled events unnamed). Known limit: the new state events (boost level, thread open/archive) are applied after the config read, so two events of one thread or guild in the same instant can swap. (6) A poll's vote-count updates are never a "Message Edited" log; only the finalized one is, once. Check every `LogEvent::ALL` user (dashboard schema, Auto Setup, the botmsg title test), every `LogKind` match, `guild_of` in `events.rs`.

**A saved edit of a DM sent outside any server was never sent.** The raider notice DM was saved on the Bot messages page, but it is sent with no server, so the copy lookup read nothing and sent the default. The data request DM has the same path, but it is locked. *Avoid:* an entry sent outside a server is listed in `botmsg::BOT_WIDE`: its one copy is stored under guild 0 (`botmsg::copy_guild`), and only the bot owner may save it (`botmsgs::may_edit`). Every sender with no server reads that copy: `reply::dm_msg` with `None`, and `reply::frame_embeds` (async, takes the app). Callers: raider notices in `raiders/review.rs` and `raiders/tasks.rs`, data request in `datareq.rs`. Not live tested.
- **The landing page flashed after login, and some actions had to be pressed again after the 2FA code.** The web app drew the landing page while it still waited for `/api/me`. The 2FA prompt resumed an action by clicking its control again, which fails for a request sent after an await (Overview Fix all when the step-up ran out mid-run, config import) and for a control that closes or changes before the code (import Apply, Channel Setup push, Delete server data, Load backup, panic mode). *Avoid:* see `crates/dashboard/ui/PITFALLS.md` ("The landing page showed..." and "After the 2FA code..."): wait for `/api/me` before choosing a view, and use `run_resumable` for those requests.
- **The Update page showed no change lines because it only knew merge commits.** `update::changes` listed a pull request only from a two-parent "Merge pull request #N" commit, but main has used squash merges since #335 ("Title (#N)", one parent), so every list was empty ("Newest" showed a hash only, Install rows had no lines). *Avoid:* `update::commit_change` reads both forms; a build's lines come from one compare of the oldest to the newest build, split at each build's commit (`changes_per_build`), so the list costs one GitHub call per new pair. The oldest listed build has no lines (its base is not in the list). A finished update shows the lines of the installed build, not of every build it skipped. Same package: the Servers page takes `banner`, `owner` and `permissions` from Discord's `/users/@me/guilds` (no new scope), lists servers where the person may add the bot (owner, Manage Server or Administrator) as `addable`, and "Add to server" is `/auth/invite?guild_id=<digits>`, the existing invite with that server preselected (same scopes and permissions; a non-numeric id is ignored).
- **Channel Setup showed the Channels grid under the Roles and Server tabs.** `.cs-grid` sets `display:grid`, which beats the browser's `[hidden]` rule, so `hidden=` did nothing (same for any element with its own display rule, such as the role picker). *Avoid:* `style.css` ends with `[hidden] { display:none !important; }`; a new class with a `display` value needs no extra rule for `hidden`. The browser test switches the tabs and checks that the grid is gone.
- **Spacing was off the 4 px grid in 233 places.** Tokens such as `space-6`, `space-10`, `space-18` and raw 2, 3, 6, 10 or 14 px values were used for margin, padding and gap. *Avoid:* every spacing is 4 px or a multiple of 4 (see `design/README.md`); `tools/ui-sizes.mjs` fails on a spacing off the grid, in `style.css` and `bundle.css`. A border or ring width uses `--stroke-2`, not a space token. A control whose inner offset changes (the toggle knob: track 44x24, knob 16 at 4 px) gets its size changed with it. Not covered by the lint: `em` spacing and positions set from Rust (`left`, `top` of popovers).
- **A log that needs the old value needs stored member data, and stored member data has five ways out that are easy to miss.** Discord's member update carries only the new username, display name and avatar, so `profile_log` keeps the last seen ones in `profile_snapshot` (migration 4044). *Avoid:* wire a new table with member data, in the same PR, to all of: (1) written only while the feature that needs it is on (`profile_log::routed`); (2) deleted when the member leaves (`on_leave`); (3) a retention delete in `retention::sweep` (here a fixed 90 days with no setting, owner rule; a per-server limit would be a `RetentionCfg` field with a `delete_for` arm and a `limits::FIXED` range); (4) `persondata::PLACES`, so `/mydata` shows and deletes it and the dashboard counts it; (5) the dashboard role: a REVOKE in `db::set_dashboard_role`, the `dashboard_role_limit` trigger in the migration, and both lists in `dashboard_role_has_rows_only`. Also `PRIVACY.md`, the `/mydata delete` DM text and `T::cleanup`. `purge_guild` needs nothing: it finds every `guild_id` column. Never use `dashboard_role_limit` for DELETE: it returns NEW, which is NULL there, so the trigger would cancel every role's deletes. Show a member's name in a code block (`Log::diff`), never as plain embed text (a display name can hold a masked link). The read of the old row takes `FOR UPDATE` (events run in their own tasks; two updates at once would both log). The first sight of a member only stores, so the whole member list is read once a day, after a restart and within the hour after the logs are turned on (`profile_log::sweep`, last in the hourly loop, one request per 1,000 members in sequence): a quiet member's first change is then logged, a missed leave is deleted (only after a complete paging), and the 90 days count from `seen_at`. A sweep over every server takes `only` for its test, as `retention::sweep` does (else a test touches other tests' rows). Known limits: a change made while the bot was offline only updates the row, with no log; a server that turned the logs on less than an hour ago has no old values yet; the old avatar picture works only while Discord still serves that hash.
- **A ticket pinged and gave access to roles that no longer exist (`@unknown-role`).** Nothing removes a permit role row (`permit_roles`) when the Discord role is deleted or re-made, and the stored ids in `tickets.ping_roles` and a panel's `ping_roles` go stale the same way. `tickets::staff_roles` (channel overwrites, at creation and in the public-channel resync) and `panel::ping_staff_roles` (the staff ping of every ticket) read those rows as they were, so the intro showed two `@unknown-role` mentions and the overwrites carried dead ids. The dashboard's role picker lists only live roles, so a dead id stays in the saved value with no way to untick it (not changed: the picker's list can be stale, and dropping unlisted ids there could remove a valid new role; the bot skips dead ids instead). *Avoid:* every place that mentions or grants access to a stored role id first drops ids the server no longer has (`tickets::live_roles` / `live_ids`, which keep the list when the role lookup fails); the same for a new role mention anywhere else. The DB rows stay: `relink` re-points them when Auto Setup or the Overview Fix remakes a role. Checked: every `staff_roles`, `ping_staff_roles`, `ping_roles` and `<@&` in `tickets/`. Role pings in other modules read roles from config the dashboard validates at save, not from permit rows; not changed. Also: the ticket type is in the channel name (`name-type-number`, `tickets::kind_name`, set at creation for a panel type and in `workflow::set_kind` for every later change). Discord allows two renames per 10 minutes per channel: a refused or slow rename (5 s limit) is only logged and the type change stands. Nothing finds a ticket by its channel name (lookups use `channel_id`).
- **A role alone gave a permit before a guild's member migration.** `permits::top_permit` read `permit_roles` (a role holder had the permit) for every guild without a `permit_members_migrated` row, and four more readers had the same "unmigrated" branch (`antinuke::held_staff`, `raiders::not_collectable`, `automod::word_exempt_roles`, the Overview handover card). *Avoid:* a permit belongs to user ids only (`permit_members`); `top_permit` and `resolve` take no roles, and `permit_roles` is only what the bot hands to listed members (and what guards and pings read). A new reader of "who holds a permit" reads `permit_members`, never a role. Kept on purpose: `migrate_members` still converts a guild's role holders to listed members once, at the first sync (GuildCreate), so a server loses nothing; the ownership handover in an unmigrated guild still deletes the strong permits' role rows, so the migration cannot give them back later (`owner::on_owner`). `enforce_permits` waits in a guild whose first permit sync has not run (nobody is listed yet, so taking roles then would lose the staff for good). Until that sync, staff who hold a permit role and are not Owner/EO/TA resolve to no permit (anti-nuke whitelist, held-staff check and the raider list see them as members); the sync is queued at GuildCreate and retried. Not done: some functions still carry a `roles` argument that nothing reads (`App::cached_level`, `raiders::collect`, `joingate::immune_by_roles`); removing it is a separate cleanup. Tests that gave a permit through a role now list the user in `permit_members`; `permits_resolve_levels` asserts a user listed nowhere has none.
- **Permit role rows outlived their Discord role.** Admins and Moderators each listed an old and a new role id, the old pair deleted. Auto Setup and the Overview Fix never make permit roles; a person deleting one by hand, or a restore that was not relinked, leaves the row. *Avoid:* do not drop the row when the role is deleted, because an anti-nuke restore makes the role again with a new id and `relink` needs the old row to point it there. `deadroles::sweep` (hourly, in `welcome::purge_left`) starts `permit_roles.missing_since` (migration 4049) when it sees the role missing, clears it when the role is back, and drops the row after `DEAD_ROLE_DAYS` (7, one constant). Readers skip missing roles at once (`tickets::live_roles`, also the `ticket config` view). A saved permits copy from the dashboard rewrites the rows and so restarts the clock (a server that saves permits every week never drops a dead row; harmless, readers skip it). `relink` clears `missing_since` when it points a row at the restored role, or a sweep that read the roles just before the restore could drop the fixed row. A backup loaded more than 7 days after the role was deleted finds no row to relink: the permit has no role until staff set one. Not done: stored config lists (`tickets.ping_roles`, panel `ping_roles`, feed `ping_role`) keep a dead id until edited, readers skip it; `level_roles` rows are not swept (the owner sync would have to make the role again first).
- **#open-a-ticket showed a bare "Open a ticket" title over a "New panel" button.** The panel message text was written only together with the default panels; a server that pressed Add panel got an empty text, and the Add button named the first panel "New panel". Root cause: a default kept in config is written once, on one path, so every other path gets nothing. *Avoid:* a message's look is a Default messages entry (`ticket.panel`), so the default always applies until the server saves its own, on every path. The ticket greeting is the one config text the owner wanted on for every server: its serde and schema default is the default greeting and 4055 filled the empty ones; an empty greeting a server saves still sends none (the 2026-11-15 rule above still holds for other new fields). The first panel the Add button makes is labelled "Open a ticket". Migration 4055 moved each written panel text into an edited version of the entry, and the dashboard tells the bot (`pg_notify('ticket_panel')`) when Default messages change, so the posted message follows without a Tickets save.
- **A 2FA refusal showed a server state.** Pressing Verify in the "Confirm it's you" dialog before setting up an authenticator showed `totp not enrolled`; a wrong code showed `invalid code`; a second setup showed `totp already enrolled`. `ApiError::Forbidden` sends its text as is, and the dialog shows it word for word. *Avoid:* a refusal a person reads says what is wrong and what to do, in a sentence (`auth::WRONG_CODE`, `NOT_SET_UP`, `ALREADY_SET_UP`, `NOT_SET_UP_MCP`). Code that reacts to a refusal compares against the constant, never a copied string (`mcp.rs` counts a guess with `auth::WRONG_CODE`). Only the text changed: which codes pass and the lockout are the same. Checked: every `ApiError::Forbidden` in `auth.rs`, `mcp.rs`, `ops.rs` (the Rescue Key refusal "invalid rescue key or code" already says what is wrong and is kept, since `secure.rs` compares it).

**The dashboard showed "Discord is limiting requests" on normal page loads and after "Fix all".** One page load starts several handlers at once (`/score`, `/score/fix`, `/discord`, `/lockdown/check`), and each fetched `GET /guilds/{id}/channels` and `/roles` fresh, so the same requests went out side by side. On a 429 they all waited the same Retry-After and retried together, hit the limit again and ran out of tries. Discord's `X-RateLimit-Remaining: 0` was ignored, and the 30 s memo (`memo_get`) and a few raw calls skipped the 429 handling. *Avoid:* every bot request goes through `Discord::send`, which waits when Discord said a path's bucket is used up or sent a 429 (shared per "METHOD /path" and for a global limit, 15 s in all). GETs through `call`, `get_json` and `memo_get` share one request when asked at once (`shared_get`) and reuse an answer for 2 s. Every write clears all reused answers and stops a GET under way from storing its answer, so data read after a fix is fresh. Tests: `discord::tests::parallel_gets_share_one_request_and_a_write_clears_them`, `a_used_up_bucket_makes_the_next_request_wait`. Not live tested.

**A feed address had to be the feed file itself.** Staff paste a site's normal address, and the add failed with "this is not an RSS or Atom feed". *Avoid:* `feed::find` reads the page and takes the feed its `<link rel="alternate">` names. The found address goes through `check_url` and the same guarded client as a typed one (public names only, https on 443, redirect, time and size limits), one hop only.

**GitHub's public list of events leaves out most of each event.** A push in `/repos/<repo>/events` has no commits, and a pull request has no title, link or author. *Avoid:* `feedfollow::body` reads each part again from its own `url` (only `https://api.github.com/`), and a push from the compare API, so `feedhooks::github_post` gets the body the webhook sends. The list says `merged` and `created` where the webhook says `closed` and `submitted`. The hourly limit (60 without `GITHUB_TOKEN`) does not count toward stopping a feed, and an unchanged list (304) costs nothing.

**A feed log line showed "Feed: GitHub (``)".** A webhook feed stores no address (GitHub or GitLab calls Brim), and its repository name came only with the first event that posts, after the "Feed Connected" line was queued; `feed_label` printed the empty address in a code span. *Avoid:* take the name from every signed delivery, the ping too (`feedhooks::take`), and from the pasted link when the feed is added (`repo_of`, `gitlab_path`); `label_of` leaves out an empty address, and one equal to the name. The poster name of posts stays GitHub or GitLab. Test: `a_label_never_shows_an_empty_address`.

**Check now said nothing, and a new feed posted nothing to test with.** The first check only remembers items, and Check now on a webhook feed did nothing. *Avoid:* Send latest posts the newest item (website, YouTube) or the newest event of a switched-on kind (GitHub, followed or webhook: the public events list, at most 5 events read), even one posted before, under a new delivery key. One at a time per feed (refused while one waits), not counted as a failed check. GitLab webhook feeds and private repositories say how to test instead (GitLab's Test button, GitHub's Recent Deliveries > Redeliver).
## A module switch must reach every reader of its parts
- The `modules` section holds the master switches of Server Joins, Messages and Logging (a missing switch is on). `GuildCfg::parse` applies them: Server Joins off turns Join Gate, Join Raid (with voice raids), Verification and the Raider List off in memory; Logging off makes `LogConfig::target` drop event logs. Stored part settings stay as they are.
- Readers of raw config were missed: the Raider List SQL queries, the dashboard web captcha, the Overview health list, and the standard channel set gate (`config_gate`), which would hide channels from @everyone while no verification ran. A part write that turned the module on also fired on a save of a part that was already on.
- *Avoid:* code that reads a part's `enabled` from raw JSON or SQL also reads `modules.<module>` (`brim_core::sections::module_of`). A write turns the module on only when it turns the part on from off, and then needs the `modules` bar (Trusted Admin with 2FA).
- **The in-Discord settings panel (`joingate 1a ?on`) saved the part on but left Server Joins off.** *Avoid:* both writers use `module_to_turn_on`; the panel asks for the same bar (Owner, Extra Owner or Trusted Admin with a fresh 2FA).
- **The Moderation switch forced each moderation command off.** That also blocked the Raider List alert buttons (Server Joins) and the commands that lift an Anti Nuke or Auto Mod punishment (unban, untimeout, unwarn, the Undo buttons). *Avoid:* the Moderation switch is one check in the command gates (`cmdcfg::module_off`), with a kept list (`KEPT` plus `SAFETY`). A button of another module uses `cmdcfg::button_settings`, which skips that check.

## Ticket type buttons
- A panel with the type switch on posts one button per ticket type: `tk2:open:<panel>:<typehash>` (menu option value `<panel>:<typehash>`, form id gets the hash as a sixth part). *Avoid:* take the type only from a hash that matches a type in the current list and only while the panel shows more than one button (`button_types`); anything else gets `form_changed`. Never read the type name from the id.
- `panel_message`, `types_problem` and `button_types` are the one source for the posted message, the save check and the dashboard preview. The preview is drawn from `POST .../tickets/preview` (the page keeps no copy). Test: `the_preview_is_what_publish_posts`.
- A type name is the button label: at most 80 characters with the switch on. The save refuses more than 25 buttons on the panel message.
- `{ticket_type}` is empty for a ticket with no type (a DM ticket has one once the member picks it before the channel); every `tags` caller passes the type or "".
- Migration 4059 renames only the exact untouched 4057 panel. A panel with any other setting keeps its name.

- **The appeal page could show what the punishment DM hid.** A case reason of the bot's own (an automod filter, the Join Gate minimum account age) is left out of the DM when the owner hides it. Showing "the newest case reason" on the appeal page would tell a raider the gate's rule. The server name went to anyone who knew the id. *Avoid:* the page shows only staff cases' reasons (`mod_id` is not the bot) and the server only to someone punished there. A Discord log message that more than one path edits (member additions, staff questions, the decision) is edited one at a time (`appeals::patch_log`); an addition checks the appeal is still open, or it writes back an old copy without the Decision field.

- **One Discord 502 left the emoji set half replaced.** `tools/emojis.mjs upload --replace` deleted an emoji, then created it, and retried only 429. One 502 on a create stopped the run (and printed the whole HTML page) after some emojis were new and one was deleted. *Avoid:* every Discord call in a tool that changes many things retries 429 (with `retry_after`), 502, 503, 504 and network errors with a backoff, and prints a short error line, not the body. Draw the new image first, then delete, then create. A failure of one item must not stop the run: count replaced, created and failed, and print the exact rerun command for the failed names. Not tested against Discord with an error.

- **A purge without a member was logged as a case against the moderator.** A purge of a channel and a slowmode stored the moderator as the case's `user_id`, because the case needs a number there. The mod log showed the moderator as Target, their case list and count held the purge, and they could not change their own purge case ("it is about you"). *Avoid:* a case without a member stores `user_id = 0` (the value a data deletion uses for "no person"); `cases::create` then logs no `target_id`. Every place that shows or filters a case's member uses the one rule in `brim_core::cases`: `target()` in code, `TARGET` and `HAS_TARGET` in SQL. They also cover older rows (`user_id = mod_id`; a self-target is refused, so that pair always means no target). Used in: the mod log (`Target: None`), `t!cases`, the report member view, the dashboard Cases list, case page, edit guard and case delete log, and the MCP `cases`, `case` and case edit preview. A dashboard staff action on such a case records no target. Other kinds with no member (lock, lockdown) open no case.

- **A move of a job that the scheduler holds is lost.** The scheduler leases a job, runs it, then deletes it by id. An update of `run_at` on the leased row (a shift of the end time, the end of a pause) changes a row that is deleted a moment later, so the giveaway never ended. *Avoid:* a move updates only unleased rows (`locked_until IS NULL OR locked_until < now()`) and queues a new job when it moved none. The job checks under the row lock that it is still due (`giveaways::end_due`): when the end moved later or the giveaway is paused, it waits and makes sure one other pending job exists.
- **A draw or an entry during an attack.** `paused_at` existed but nothing set it, so a raid, lockdown, panic mode or nuke did not stop entries or the draw. *Avoid:* the Enter press and the end job read the live state (`giveaways::attacked`: raid detector, panic run, open anti-nuke case, server locks; a lock of one channel does not count) and set the pause at once; a round every 15 s pauses and resumes. A restored or remade role or channel moves into running giveaways (`giveaways::relink`), so a remade blocked role still keeps its members out.
- **A backup must not bring back a giveaway row.** Only the 90-day prune of ended giveaways deletes a row, and manual backups are kept for ever, so a load that restored rows would bring back a giveaway that already drew, and draw it again. *Avoid:* the rows stay in the database through a restore; only their channel and role ids move (`relink`). Panic and raids come back at start, but an open anti-nuke case is in memory only: the pause round also reads `nuke_incidents` before it resumes.
- **A winner could claim while the place was being drawn again.** The claim job read the expired place, then spent seconds on member checks; a Claim press in that time still saw an open deadline, said "You claimed", and the redraw then removed the place. *Avoid:* the job closes the claim in the same statement that reads it (`claim_due`, `claims_due`: `deadline = LEAST(deadline, now())`). Places that run out together are drawn again in one redraw (one post, not one per place). A new place number skips every place in use, also those handed out in the same save.
- **A winner role was taken away while a later win still held it.** A member who won the same winner role in two giveaways lost it when the first role time ended, and a second win while they still had the role queued no removal (the role counted as "had before"). *Avoid:* a due role removal skips while another removal for the same member and role waits in the future (`giveaways::role_off_waits`), and a win for a member who has the role adds its own removal when one waits. The member keeps the role until the latest role end time. The running job is due, so it never counts itself; a test that calls the job must make its row due first.
- **A repeat that starts a giveaway must claim its run and make the giveaway in one transaction.** The message schedule stores the next run first and sends after, so a crash between the two loses a run. *Avoid:* for a plan, the plan row lock (`FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED`), the new giveaway (`giveaways::create_in`, under the per-server lock that counts the limit) and the next run are one transaction: two runners, a restart or a late tick start it once, and a crash starts it never or once, not twice. A run more than an hour late is skipped with the reason on the plan (the last run of a plan has 6 hours); a plan over the limit of running giveaways skips the run, it does not queue. "Start after the last giveaway ends" keeps `held` and looks again every minute: one indexed read per waiting plan.
- **A plan runs with the rights of who saved it last.** Like message schedules, the runner reads the saver's permit (`giveaways`, or Trusted Admin and up) before each run and stops the plan with the reason when it is gone; an unreadable level only skips the pass. A template and a plan never keep an entry code (only a hash is stored, salted with the giveaway id): a giveaway made from them has none, and the form asks for a new one.
- **A runner that looks at few plans per pass, and marks a plan late by the clock, loses runs under load.** A pass looked at 8 plans, one at a time, and a run was skipped when over an hour late, so many plans due in the same minute were skipped only because the runner was busy. *Avoid:* a pass takes 200 plans in 8 lanes, and a run is late only by the time Brim was down (runner start minus run time). 10,000 due plans start once in about 8 s.
- **A plan must not start a giveaway in a server under attack.** The attack state is in the bot's memory, the plan runner is in the dashboard. *Avoid:* the bot's pause round (every 15 s) moves the plans due soon of an attacked server a minute on, with the reason on the plan; it starts once when the attack is over. Tests that call the pause round side by side use `pause_round`, not `sync_pauses`, whose one-round guard makes a second call do nothing.
- **A switch showed on while the server ran it off.** The schema (`crates/dashboard/src/schema.rs`) gave `giveaways.enabled` the default `true`, but `GiveawaysCfg::default` is off (owner decision), so a server with no saved giveaways settings showed the switch on while Start stayed off. *Avoid:* the `default` of a schema field must equal the Rust `Default`; a test checks the giveaways switch, and every new `dv(.., true)` on an `enabled` field needs the same check against its Rust default.

## The preview of a default message missed the buttons Brim adds
Default messages can carry buttons that code adds (`giveaway.running`, `ticket.confirm`, link buttons under logs). The editor and preview drew only the stored message. The bot now saves the real button rows with its samples (`previews.rs`, `added_buttons`, key to rows); `added_buttons_belong_to_real_messages` fails when a key is not a message. Label and emoji come from the same builders and constants the bot posts with (`enter_row`, `claim_row`, `link_row`, `GO_MESSAGE`, `GO_POLL`, `GO_CHANNEL`).
*Avoid:* a new button under a default message is added to that table in the same change. The preview must never show an example that the bot cannot post (a running giveaway shows a count of winners).

## Giveaway card pictures
**A picture variable stayed as typed text in the link slot.** `fill_value` skips every `url` key, so only `fill_links` fills a link. *Avoid:* a new link variable (`{sponsor_picture}`, `{banner}`) goes in the `LINKS` list of `fill_links` in `botmsg.rs`, and its message key must be one that calls it (`feed.*` and `giveaway.*`). An empty or non-web value then drops the slot, so Discord never gets an empty link.
**A failed look-up replaced a good cached value with an empty one.** The sponsor picture is cached in memory for hours. *Avoid:* never store the result of a failed fetch over a good cache entry. On a Discord error (5xx, 429, timeout) keep the old picture and ask again in a few minutes. Only a first look-up that fails stays empty.
**The giveaway cards show their facts as fields.** The default running and ended messages keep Hosted by, Sponsored by and the requirements as lines under the title, and Ends, Entries and Winners as inline fields (the ended card adds a full-width Result field, `Ends` reads just "Ended", and `{ga_winner_count}` is the number asked for). `drop_blank_fields` drops a field with an empty value. *Avoid:* a new fact needs its own field, never a line that can be empty next to a label. A server that saved its own copy keeps it; only the default changed. The place under the title is kept free for a host's own text later.

## A load test of the plan runner shared the database with other runners
**`many_due_plans_all_start_once` failed with 0 or a few started plans.** Two causes outside the plan code. (1) The test asked `due` for the oldest 200 plans of all servers and then kept its own; plans left by another test run filled the 200, so a lane saw none and stopped. (2) A dashboard server on the same database took the due plans with its own runner and moved them a minute on ("Brim could not run this plan"), because it cannot reach Discord. *Avoid:* a test of a runner picks its plans in the query, by its own server range, never by filtering after a limit. Never run a dashboard server on the database the tests use: give the server its own database (`createdb`), and start it only with that one.

## One dropped request ended the whole access matrix and named nothing
In CI the browser sweep failed with `FAIL matrix: crashed: page.evaluate: TypeError: Failed to fetch`. Page tests passed, the server log held no error, and the same code passed on the next commit. One thrown error ended the whole matrix loop and only the crash text was kept, so the dropped call among hundreds could not be named.
*Avoid:* a test loop that makes many calls catches the error of each call and fails with the call's own name (user, method, path), then goes on. A crash that names nothing is a finding nobody can follow up. The matrix in `tests/e2e/deep.js` does this. If the same route drops again, it is a real fault in that route, not the runner.

## Stock emoji in bot messages
**The "Ticket Closed" DM showed a stock mailbox next to Brim's own emojis.** The title used `TICKET_CLOSE`, but the first line had the Unicode `📪` typed in. A typed Unicode emoji never turns into a Brim emoji, so it shows the system look. The same was true for the first line of every punishment DM, the verify and appeal DMs, the verify panel and captcha titles, the join raid and voice raid log titles, the Bot check logs, the lockdown update lines, the note title, and the dashboard owner alerts (MCP token, Secure my account, update, "Not you?"). *Avoid:* a message Brim posts never has a typed emoji. Use a constant from `crates/logging/src/emoji.rs` (its fallback is the Unicode emoji, so tests and self-hosted bots still work) in a `format!` (`{{server}}` for a placeholder). The dashboard loads the ids too, so its owner alerts use the constants the same way. `emoji::fill` (`{e:name}` tags) drops an emoji that is not uploaded and has no fallback: use it only for server owners' own text. A new emoji needs its icon in `assets/icons` first. Left on purpose: the sample values in the editor variable lists, and test strings. The giveaway Enter default is the Brim ticket (`{e:giveaway_enter}`): a server owner's own button emoji stays, but the default is never a typed Unicode emoji.

**The Update page showed "Newest: Could not reach GitHub right now." and an empty release list after a release, with nothing in the log.** `update::get_json` turned every failure (no connection, the hourly limit of 60 unauthenticated calls per server address, a bad answer) into `None` with no log, and `builds` kept no list, so one failed refresh emptied the page. The limit is shared with the GitHub feeds and with the compare and pull request calls of one page load. *Avoid:* log a failed call (WARN with the url and reason) and send its reason to the page (`error`); name the limit with its reset time; keep the last good release list on a failed refresh, and do not retry a failed refresh for 30 s. `GITHUB_TOKEN` (public read only, already passed to the dashboard) is sent to api.github.com calls only, never to the registry. Every GitHub call of the dashboard is in `update.rs` (`get_json`, attestation check, which returns its own error) and `feedfollow.rs` (already names its limit).

## An identity value from a failed lookup went into a commit
**A commit author email held an error text.** The email was read from a lookup that failed, and the failure text was stored as the value. It showed in the author and committer of a pushed commit and pointed to a tool. *Avoid:* set the author name and email as fixed literal values in the repo config, never from a command or an API call. Before every push, print `git log --format='%an <%ae> | %cn <%ce>'` for each commit to be pushed and read it. A wrong value in a pushed commit is not fixed by a new push: the commit stays public on its branch until the branch is deleted.

## Restoring names that must be unique
**A backup load could fail on category names that were swapped after the backup.** The load freed a clashing name by renaming it to "name (id)". That name could already exist (staff can type "B (5)"), and the unique check runs row by row, so the whole load stopped. *Avoid:* free every name first with a value the dashboard can never make (a leading space: names are trimmed), then set the names from the backup. Fixed in `crates/bot/src/msgbackup.rs`; test `swapped_category_names_still_load`.

## The optional Staff role
- **A rollback would have turned the Staff role off with no channel steps.** `store::write_levels` deleted every `level_roles` row and wrote back only Trusted Admin and Owner, so a rollback or Auto Setup run dropped the `staff` row, and Brim took the role from everyone while its channel overwrites stayed. *Avoid:* only `staff_role.rs` writes the `staff` row (on, off, and the Overview Fix); `write_levels` keeps it, and the rollback's owner check compares the levels without it. A new level that has steps of its own gets its own route, not a key in `permits.levels`.
- **A permit's rank decides who gets the Staff role, but a rank change queued no sync.** `store::held` compared names, roles and members only. *Avoid:* the rank is in `held`. When a new reader depends on a permit field, check that a change to that field queues `permit_sync`.
- **An existing role picked as the Staff role goes to every staff member.** A role with Administrator, a main role or one Discord hands out would give that to all staff, or Brim would take a main role from every member. *Avoid:* `staff_role::unfit` refuses @everyone, managed roles, roles at or above Brim's top role, roles with a dangerous permission, roles Discord hands out, and a role another setting uses (`channels::used_by`, the alert and ping lists left out).
- **Do not replace the permit role overwrites with the Staff role.** A deleted Staff role (a nuke action) would lock every staff member out of the logs during the attack. *Avoid:* the Staff role opens a Staff channel next to the permit roles (`Ctx::staff_role` in Channel Setup, `all_staff_roles` for the Logs and Staff categories), never an Admins or Owner channel. Off takes its overwrites first, then the row, then the role, so no step closes a channel to staff.
- **The Staff role is no member list group.** `all_staff_roles` holds it (security score, anti-nuke Mention @everyone rule); the hoist pass and its Overview row read `grouped_staff_roles`, which leaves it out.
- **Off locked staff out where only the Staff role opened a channel.** Members can hold a permit by user ID with no permit role, so the delete left no role with View. *Avoid:* off gives View to the live permit staff roles first, and refuses when the role opens a channel and no such role exists. Off takes the draft lock alone, and Auto Setup holds it shared over its channel steps, so neither writes the Staff role's overwrites while the other runs.
- **A picked role could carry rights.** Only dangerous permissions were refused, so Move or Mute Members went to every staff member. *Avoid:* `unfit` refuses any permission that @everyone does not have.
- **A setting could have Brim give a level role to every member.** The quarantine role, main roles and verification roles took any role, so the Staff, Trusted Admin or Owner role could go to members, and `permit_sync` then took it back: a fight, or staff access for a moment. *Avoid:* `api::given_roles` names the roles a section gives; a save, rollback (also one that brings back a level role) or import that adds a level role there is refused, the bot's settings command refuses it too (`reply.settings.staff_only_role`), and a value saved before shows as the Overview row `level_role_given`. Ids are read as text or number (`api::id_text`), as the bot reads both. A new setting that gives a role goes into `given_roles` and the settings command list.
- **Picking an existing role took it from its members with no word.** The next sync takes the Staff role from everyone who is not staff. *Avoid:* the switch asks first with the live count (`GET /staff-role?pick=`, members who hold it and are not staff).
- **A backup from before off gave the old Staff role its channel access back.** The role was no longer a level role, so nothing guarded it, and anyone with Manage Roles could hand that access out. *Avoid:* off, "Make it again" and a relink record the role and the time in `former_staff_roles`; every read of a backup image for a restore (`backup::latest`, `load`, `recreate`, the protected-channel copy) drops its overwrites from images taken before that time (`backup::Former`), while it is no level or permit role. Access given after off stays. A new read of backup images must go through `Former` too.

## Emoji tags with underscores showed as italics in the preview
The CONGRATS word is eight tags in a row (`{e:congrats_1}{e:congrats_2}`). The dashboard preview read the underscores between two tags as an emphasis pair, so the word showed as raw text with slanted parts, because `md::inline` set emphasis before `icons::fill_tags` drew the tags. *Avoid:* `inline` keeps a `{e:name}` tag whole, like a code span. A name a default uses must also be in the `icons!` list in `icons.rs`, or the preview shows the tag as text. A default that opens a text with an emoji word has an empty first line when the emojis are not uploaded (`emoji::fill` drops the tags); Discord trims it.

## A fixed line in a default shows in every state of that message
The CONGRATS word was a fixed first line of the ended giveaway card, so a giveaway that nobody won said CONGRATS above "Result: Nobody". *Avoid:* a line that must not show in some state is a variable alone on its line (`{ga_congrats}`, code name `congrats`, passed by `giveaways.rs`), empty in that state; `fill_text` drops a line whose variables are all empty. When a default gets a celebratory or state-bound line, search for the same pattern: a state without that fact needs its own default or an empty variable.

## The test send of a default message posted raw emoji tags
"Send test to me" posted `{e:trophy}` and the CONGRATS tags as text in winners, reroll and every other message whose text sits above the embeds, because `fill_emoji` fills only the title, description, footer and fields of embeds. *Avoid:* the test send fills every text of the message with `fill_all` (all strings, like the bot's own `fill_tags`), so it matches the real post.
